Grammar & commentary

Madhyama Carita · मध्यमचरितम् · Chapter 4 of 13

शक्रादिस्तुतिःThe Praise by Indra and the Gods (Śakrādi Stuti)

śakrādistutiḥ

42 mantras · 4.1–4.42

❁

Overview

Medhas resumes (4.1). With Mahiṣa and his army slain, Indra and the gods, bowed and thrilling with joy, praise the Devī (4.2). Their hymn (4.3–23) bows to Ambikā, whose form is the gathered powers of all the gods but who pervades the world by her own power (4.3), whose majesty not even Viṣṇu, Brahmā and Śiva can tell (4.4); she is fortune in the homes of the good and misfortune in the homes of the wicked, understanding, faith and modesty (4.5). The gods cannot describe her form, valour or deeds (4.6), yet name her: the cause of the worlds and the primal Prakṛti (4.7), Svāhā and Svadhā (4.8), the knowledge that liberates (4.9), the Veda whose self is sound, and Vārttā, livelihood (4.10), Medhā, Durgā, Śrī and Gaurī (4.11). They recall the battle: that Mahiṣa could look on her gentle, moon-like face and still strike, and that he did not die at the sight of it in anger (4.12–13), and they pray for her grace, having seen what her anger does (4.14). Her favour brings honour, wealth and a household at peace (4.15), heaven to the doer of good (4.16), and freedom from fear to whoever remembers her (4.17). Why she kills with weapons and not with a glance: so that even her enemies, purified, reach heaven (4.18–19); their eyes were spared because they gazed at her face (4.20); compassion and relentlessness are found together in her alone (4.21–22); the worlds are saved, the enemies sent to heaven, the gods’ fear gone (4.23). In four anuṣṭubh verses they ask her protection with trident, sword, bell and bow, in every quarter, through her gentle and terrible forms and all her weapons (4.24–27). Medhas tells how the gods worshipped her with flowers of Nandana, perfumes and incense (4.28–30). The Devī offers a boon (4.31–32); the gods answer that nothing remains to be done now that Mahiṣa is dead, but ask that, whenever remembered, she destroy their calamities, and that she increase the wealth and household of any mortal who praises her with these hymns (4.33–37). Saying ‘So be it’, Bhadrakālī vanishes (4.38–39). Medhas closes the story of her rising from the gods’ bodies and announces the next: how she arose again from Gaurī’s body to slay Śumbha and Niśumbha (4.40–42).

  • The Goddess is not the sum of the gods’ powers
  • One Devī in fortune, intellect, faith and the Veda
  • Gentle and terrible: one face
  • Compassion even to enemies
  • Remembrance, recitation and the boon
About this chapter
Forms of the Devī
Ambikā, ‘the Mother’, the name the gods use most (4.3, 4.24, 4.27, 4.37); Caṇḍikā (4.4, 4.25); Bhagavatī (4.9, 4.10, 4.34) and the sovereign titles Īśvarī (4.25) and Maheśvarī (4.35). In the hymn she is named as qualities and powers: Śrī and Alakṣmī, Buddhi, Śraddhā and Lajjā (4.5); the primal, unmanifest Prakṛti with the three guṇas (4.7); Svāhā and Svadhā (4.8); the supreme Vidyā, cause of liberation (4.9); Trayī, the Veda, whose self is sound (शब्दात्मिका), and Vārttā (4.10); Medhā, Durgā, Śrī as Viṣṇu’s consort and Gaurī as Śiva’s (4.11). The gods address her as Durgā (4.17) and as giver of boons (वरदे, 4.22), liken her angry face to Death (Antaka) enraged (4.13), and ask the protection of her gentle and her terrible forms alike (4.26). In the narrative she is ‘the sustainer of the worlds’ (जगतां धात्री, 4.29), gracious of face (प्रसादसुमुखी, 4.30), and, as she vanishes, Bhadrakālī (4.39).
Significance of the events
The chapter turns the victory of chapter 3 into praise, and the praise into a promise. Its hymn is the first in the text addressed to the Goddess as warrior — Brahmā’s hymn in chapter 1 was to Yoganidrā within the sleeping Viṣṇu — and it says what the battle meant: the gods did not lend her their power (4.3), and her killing is itself compassion (4.18–23). The boon the gods ask (4.36–37) is the first statement in the text of the fruit of reciting it, which the Goddess sets out at length in chapter 12. The closing verses (4.40–42) end the middle carita and announce the third, so that the chapter is both an ending and a hinge.
Sanskrit patterns to watch
Most of the hymn (4.2–23) is in vasantatilakā, fourteen syllables to a printed line, so words and junctions often run across a line-end (दोषै- / र्न, 4.7; एत- / न्नीतं, 4.14; भवतीप्रसादा- / ल्लोकत्रये, 4.16); the petitions (4.24–27) return to anuṣṭubh. The hymn style is relative and correlative (या … तां त्वाम्, 4.5; यस्याः … सा, 4.4; यानि … तैः, 4.26–27), with predicates in the nominative and असि understood or stated (मेधासि, दुर्गासि, 4.11). The Goddess is the subject of second-person passives (ज्ञायसे, 4.7; उच्चार्यसे, 4.8; अभ्यस्यसे, 4.9); impersonal passives carry the wonders of the battle (प्रहृतम्, 4.12; कैर्जीव्यते, 4.13). Prayer uses the imperative (प्रसीद, पाहि, रक्ष), deliberation the first-person imperative (किं वर्णयाम, 4.6), courtesy the passive imperative (व्रियताम्, 4.32) and the respectful third person (भवती, भगवत्या). The gods’ boons are asked in epic ātmanepada optatives (हिंसेथाः, भवेथाः, 4.36–37), with the repeated participle संस्मृता संस्मृता ‘each time remembered’ (4.36).
The teaching, as distinct from the narrative
Literally, the gods praise the Goddess who has saved them, worship her, ask two boons, and she grants them and disappears; Medhas then announces the next story. As teaching, the Śākta tradition reads the hymn as a statement of who the Goddess is: one power behind fortune and misfortune, intellect and faith, sacrifice and liberation (4.5–4.11), whose anger and grace are one act — she destroys the asuras and purifies them in the same stroke (4.18–4.23). It also draws from the boon that remembrance and the recitation of these verses are enough to draw her help (4.17, 4.36–4.37). A modern reflection, which is ours and not the text’s, might add that the gods, once rescued, ask first for nothing and then for others: every mortal who will say their words. The text itself states only the hymn, the worship, the boons and her promise.
Carita
Madhyama Carita (the middle episode, chapters 2–4), of which this is the closing chapter. In the recitation tradition the middle carita’s presiding form is Mahālakṣmī; this is the Gita Press viniyoga (printed before chapter 2), not a statement of the Purāṇa text, which does not use the name Mahālakṣmī here. In the Gita Press pāṭha chapter 4 has no viniyoga of its own, only a dhyāna: one should meditate on Durgā called Jayā, dark as a black cloud, whose sidelong glances bring fear to the hosts of enemies, the crescent moon bound on her crown, holding in her hands conch, discus, sword and trident, three-eyed, seated on the lion’s shoulders, filling the three worlds with her radiance, surrounded by the gods and served by those who seek siddhis.
Recited before the chapter

These lines belong to the recitation (the Gita Press pāṭha), not to the Mārkaṇḍeya Purāṇa text, and are kept apart from the numbered mantras.

Dhyāna

ॐ कालाभ्राभां कटाक्षैररिकुलभयदां मौलिबद्धेन्दुरेखांशङ्खं चक्रं कृपाणं त्रिशिखमपि करैरुद्वहन्तीं त्रिनेत्राम्।सिंहस्कन्धाधिरूढां त्रिभुवनमखिलं तेजसा पूरयन्तींध्यायेद् दुर्गां जयाख्यां त्रिदशपरिवृतां सेवितां सिद्धिकामैः॥

ॐ kālābhrābhāṃ kaṭākṣairarikulabhayadāṃ maulibaddhendurekhāṃśaṅkhaṃ cakraṃ kṛpāṇaṃ triśikhamapi karairudvahantīṃ trinetrām |siṃhaskandhādhirūḍhāṃ tribhuvanamakhilaṃ tejasā pūrayantīṃdhyāyed durgāṃ jayākhyāṃ tridaśaparivṛtāṃ sevitāṃ siddhikāmaiḥ ||

Verse 4.1

Speaker
‹›

ॐऋषिरुवाच॥⁠१⁠॥

ṛṣiruvāca ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

ऋषिः · उवाच

ṛṣiḥ · uvāca

Word by word

  1. ऋषिःṛṣiḥthe seer (Medhas)m. nom. sg. of ऋषि (i-stem); subject of उवाच
  2. उवाचuvācasaid√वच् (adādi, 2P ‘to speak’), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, kartari prayoga

Translation

The seer said:

In readable EnglishThe seer said:

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • ऋषिरुवाच ṛṣiruvāca → ऋषिः + उवाच

    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)

    After a vowel other than a/ā, the visarga (as रु) is sounded r before a vowel: ऋषिः → ऋषिर् before उ, written together.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

No compound in this verse needs analysis.

Morphology & anvaya

  • उवाच uvāca

    Perfect of √वच् with samprasāraṇa in the reduplicating syllable (व → उ): ‘(he) said’, the narrative past.

Anvaya · prose order

ऋषिः उवाच।

ṛṣiḥ uvāca |

Subject and verb: a speaker label.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The sage Medhas resumes his story for King Suratha (and the merchant Samādhi). Chapter 3 ended with Mahiṣāsura beheaded (3.42), his army destroyed (3.43) and the gods beginning to praise the Devī (3.44); this chapter gives that praise in full. In recitation the mantra carries the bīja ॐ, which belongs to the pāṭha, not to the Purāṇa.

Verse 4.2

Śloka · vasantatilakā
‹›

शक्रादयः सुरगणा निहतेऽतिवीर्येतस्मिन्दुरात्मनि सुरारिबले च देव्या।तां तुष्टुवुः प्रणतिनम्रशिरोधरांसावाग्भिः प्रहर्षपुलकोद्गमचारुदेहाः॥⁠२⁠॥

śakrādayaḥ suragaṇā nihate'tivīryetasmindurātmani surāribale ca devyā |tāṃ tuṣṭuvuḥ praṇatinamraśirodharāṃsāvāgbhiḥ praharṣapulakodgamacārudehāḥ ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

शक्रादयः · सुरगणाः · निहते · अतिवीर्येतस्मिन् · दुरात्मनि · सुरारिबले · च · देव्याताम् · तुष्टुवुः · प्रणतिनम्रशिरोधरांसाःवाग्भिः · प्रहर्षपुलकोद्गमचारुदेहाः

śakrādayaḥ · suragaṇāḥ · nihate · ativīryetasmin · durātmani · surāribale · ca · devyātām · tuṣṭuvuḥ · praṇatinamraśirodharāṃsāḥvāgbhiḥ · praharṣapulakodgamacārudehāḥ

Word by word

  1. शक्रादयःśakrādayaḥŚakra (Indra) and the restm. nom. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); subject of तुष्टुवुः
  2. सुरगणाःsuragaṇāḥthe hosts of the godsm. nom. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); in apposition to शक्रादयः
  3. निहतेnihatehaving been slainpast passive participle (kta) of नि + √हन् (adādi, 2P ‘to strike, kill’), m. loc. sg.; predicate of the locative absolute
  4. अतिवीर्येativīryeof surpassing valourm. loc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies तस्मिन्
  5. तस्मिन्tasminthat one (Mahiṣāsura)pronoun तद्, m. loc. sg.; subject of the locative absolute
  6. दुरात्मनिdurātmanithe evil-souledm. loc. sg. of the an-stem दुरात्मन्; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies तस्मिन्
  7. सुरारिबलेsurāribalethe army of the gods’ enemiesn. loc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); second subject of the locative absolute
  8. चcaandindeclinable (conjunction); joins तस्मिन् … and सुरारिबले
  9. देव्याdevyāby the Devīf. instr. sg. of देवी; agent of निहते
  10. ताम्tāmherpronoun तद्, f. acc. sg.; object of तुष्टुवुः
  11. तुष्टुवुःtuṣṭuvuḥpraised√स्तु (adādi, 2U ‘to praise’), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa bahuvacana, parasmaipada
  12. प्रणतिनम्रशिरोधरांसाःpraṇatinamraśirodharāṃsāḥwith necks and shoulders bent low in reverencem. nom. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies सुरगणाः
  13. वाग्भिःvāgbhiḥwith wordsf. instr. pl. of वाच् ‘speech’; instrument of तुष्टुवुः
  14. प्रहर्षपुलकोद्गमचारुदेहाःpraharṣapulakodgamacārudehāḥtheir bodies made beautiful by hair standing on end for joym. nom. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies सुरगणाः

Translation

When that one of surpassing valour, the evil-souled, and the army of the gods’ enemies had been slain by the Devī, the hosts of the gods, Śakra and the rest, their necks and shoulders bent low in reverence, their bodies beautiful with hair standing on end for joy, praised her with words.

In readable EnglishWhen the Devī had slain that mighty, evil-hearted one and the army of the gods’ enemies, Indra and the hosts of the gods, necks and shoulders bowed in reverence, their bodies thrilling with joy, praised her in words.

‘That one’ (तस्मिन्) is Mahiṣāsura, who is named only in 4.12.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • सुरगणा निहते suragaṇā nihate → सुरगणाः + निहते

    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), भोभगोअघोअपूर्वस्य योऽशि (8.3.17)

    -āḥ before a voiced sound: the visarga (as रु) becomes y, and that y is dropped before a consonant (a further rule, not cited here by number), leaving -ā. The same change gives प्रणतिनम्रशिरोधरांसा before वाग्भिः.

  • निहतेऽतिवीर्ये nihate'tivīrye → निहते + अतिवीर्ये

    vowel sandhi (pūrvarūpa) · एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109)

    Final -e before initial a: the a is absorbed (avagraha).

  • सुरारिबले surāribale → सुर + अरि + बले

    vowel sandhi inside a compound (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    a + a → ā.

  • तां तुष्टुवुः tāṃ tuṣṭuvuḥ → ताम् + तुष्टुवुः

    anusvāra sandhi · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)

    Final m before a consonant is written as anusvāra.

  • प्रहर्षपुलकोद्गमचारुदेहाः praharṣapulakodgamacārudehāḥ → प्रहर्ष + पुलक + उद्गम + चारु + देहाः

    vowel sandhi inside a compound (guṇa) · आद्गुणः (6.1.87)

    a + u → o (पुलक + उद्गम → पुलकोद्गम). Inside उद्गम the t of the prefix उत् is voiced before g (झलां जशोऽन्ते, 8.2.39).

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • शक्रादयः śakrādayaḥ

    bahuvrīhi (tadguṇasaṃvijñāna: Śakra himself is among them)

    Members
    शक्र + आदि
    Vigraha
    शक्रः आदिः येषां ते śakraḥ ādiḥ yeṣāṃ te
    Literally
    “those of whom Śakra is the first”
    In the verse
    Indra and the other gods

  • सुरगणाः suragaṇāḥ

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa

    Members
    सुर + गण
    Vigraha
    सुराणां गणाः surāṇāṃ gaṇāḥ
    Literally
    “hosts of the gods”
    In the verse
    the assembled gods

  • अतिवीर्ये ativīrye

    bahuvrīhi (with the prefix अति as first member)

    Members
    अति + वीर्य
    Vigraha
    अतिशयितं वीर्यं यस्य सः, तस्मिन् atiśayitaṃ vīryaṃ yasya saḥ, tasmin
    Literally
    “one whose valour is excessive”
    In the verse

    bahuvrīhi
