  • परिपूर्णचन्द्रबिम्बानुकारि paripūrṇacandrabimbānukāri

    upapada tatpuruṣa (agent noun अनुकारिन्) containing a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa and a karmadhāraya (परिपूर्णचन्द्र)

    Members
    परिपूर्णचन्द्र + बिम्ब + अनुकारिन्
    Vigraha
    परिपूर्णः चन्द्रः परिपूर्णचन्द्रः; तस्य बिम्बं परिपूर्णचन्द्रबिम्बम्; तत् अनुकरोति इति paripūrṇaḥ candraḥ paripūrṇacandraḥ; tasya bimbaṃ paripūrṇacandrabimbam; tat anukaroti iti
    Literally
    “imitating the disc of the full moon”
    In the verse
    round and radiant as the full moon

  • कनकोत्तमकान्तिकान्तम् kanakottamakāntikāntam

    tṛtīyā tatpuruṣa containing a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa and a saptamī tatpuruṣa (कनकोत्तम)

    Members
    कनकोत्तम + कान्ति + कान्त
    Vigraha
    कनकेषु उत्तमं कनकोत्तमम्; कनकोत्तमस्य कान्तिः कनकोत्तमकान्तिः; तया कान्तम् kanakeṣu uttamaṃ kanakottamam; kanakottamasya kāntiḥ kanakottamakāntiḥ; tayā kāntam
    Literally
    “lovely with the lustre of the best among golds”
    In the verse
    glowing like the finest gold
    Alternative
    कनकोत्तम may also be taken as a karmadhāraya with the qualifier placed second, उत्तमं कनकम्; the sense is the same.

  • अत्यद्भुतम् atyadbhutam

    prādi tatpuruṣa

    Members
    अति + अद्भुत
    Vigraha
    अतिशयेन अद्भुतम् atiśayena adbhutam
    Literally
    “excessively wonderful”
    In the verse
    a great marvel

  • आत्तरुषा āttaruṣā

    bahuvrīhi

    Members
    आत्त + रुष्
    Vigraha
    आत्ता रुट् येन सः, तेन āttā ruṭ yena saḥ, tena
    Literally
    “by one by whom wrath was seized”
    In the verse
    in a fury

  • महिषासुरेण mahiṣāsureṇa

    karmadhāraya

    Members
    महिष + असुर
    Vigraha
    महिषः च असौ असुरः च, तेन mahiṣaḥ ca asau asuraḥ ca, tena
    Literally
    “by the buffalo who is an asura”
    In the verse
    by the buffalo-demon
Morphology & anvaya

  • प्रहृतम् prahṛtam

    An impersonal (bhāve) past participle: neuter singular with the agent in the instrumental, ‘it was struck by Mahiṣāsura’ = ‘Mahiṣāsura struck’. The fact itself is the subject of अत्यद्भुतम्.

  • विलोक्य vilokya

    Absolutive in -य (lyap), used because the root has a prefix (वि + √लोक्): ‘having seen’.

  • आत्तरुषा āttaruṣā

    आत्त is the past participle of आ + √दा ‘to take’ (आ + दत्त → आत्त); रुष् ‘anger’ is a feminine consonant stem whose instrumental is रुषा, and the bahuvrīhi आत्तरुष् declines the same way.

Anvaya · prose order

(देवि,) ईषत्सहासम् अमलं परिपूर्णचन्द्रबिम्बानुकारि कनकोत्तमकान्तिकान्तं (तव) वक्त्रं विलोक्य तथा अपि आत्तरुषा महिषासुरेण सहसा प्रहृतम् (इति यत्, तत्) अत्यद्भुतम्।

(devi,) īṣatsahāsam amalaṃ paripūrṇacandrabimbānukāri kanakottamakāntikāntaṃ (tava) vaktraṃ vilokya tathā api āttaruṣā mahiṣāsureṇa sahasā prahṛtam (iti yat, tat) atyadbhutam |

The statement ‘Mahiṣāsura, having seen your face, struck’ (an impersonal passive with the agent in the instrumental) is the subject of the predicate अत्यद्भुतम्. The stanza is answered by 4.13 (‘but that, seeing it angry, he did not die at once — that is more wonderful’).

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The hymn turns to the battle of chapter 3 and to the Devī’s face. Its beauty — smiling, pure, moon-like, golden — should have disarmed the asura; that he could see it and still strike is the first wonder. The second, greater wonder follows in 4.13: that seeing the same face in anger he did not die on the spot.

Traditional interpretation

The pair 4.12–4.13 sets the Devī’s gentle (saumya) and terrible (raudra) aspects side by side: the same face is lovely and dreadful. The gods’ astonishment is also a judgement on Mahiṣa’s blindness: the asura sees beauty and answers it with violence.

Modern reflection

A modern reading: the stanza asks what it takes to look at goodness and still choose to attack it. Anger (रुष्) is named as what blinds.

Verse 4.13

Śloka · vasantatilakā
‹›

दृष्ट्वा तु देवि कुपितं भ्रुकुटीकराल-मुद्यच्छशाङ्कसदृशच्छवि यन्न सद्यः।प्राणान्मुमोच महिषस्तदतीव चित्रंकैर्जीव्यते हि कुपितान्तकदर्शनेन॥⁠१३⁠॥

dṛṣṭvā tu devi kupitaṃ bhrukuṭīkarāla-mudyacchaśāṅkasadṛśacchavi yanna sadyaḥ |prāṇānmumoca mahiṣastadatīva citraṃkairjīvyate hi kupitāntakadarśanena ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

दृष्ट्वा · तु · देवि · कुपितम् · भ्रुकुटीकरालम्उद्यच्छशाङ्कसदृशच्छवि · यत् · न · सद्यःप्राणान् · मुमोच · महिषः · तत् · अतीव · चित्रम्कैः · जीव्यते · हि · कुपितान्तकदर्शनेन

dṛṣṭvā · tu · devi · kupitam · bhrukuṭīkarālamudyacchaśāṅkasadṛśacchavi · yat · na · sadyaḥprāṇān · mumoca · mahiṣaḥ · tat · atīva · citramkaiḥ · jīvyate · hi · kupitāntakadarśanena

Word by word

  1. दृष्ट्वाdṛṣṭvāhaving seenabsolutive (ktvā) of √दृश् (bhvādi 1P ‘to see’); its subject is महिषः, its object the face (वक्त्रम्) carried over from 4.12
  2. तुtubutindeclinable (adversative particle): turns from the smiling face of 4.12 to the angry one
  3. देविdeviO Devīf. voc. sg. of देवी
  4. कुपितम्kupitamangry, enragedpast passive participle (kta) of √कुप् (divādi 4P ‘to be angry’), n. acc. sg.; qualifies the understood वक्त्रम्
  5. भ्रुकुटीकरालम्bhrukuṭīkarālamdreadful with its frownn. acc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies the understood वक्त्रम्
  6. उद्यच्छशाङ्कसदृशच्छविudyacchaśāṅkasadṛśacchaviwhose hue is like the rising moonn. acc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa), a bahuvrīhi ending in छवि and so declined as a neuter i-stem; qualifies the understood वक्त्रम्
  7. यत्yatthat (the fact that)indeclinable conjunction (यद् ‘that’); its clause is resumed by तत्
  8. नnanotindeclinable (negative particle)
  9. सद्यःsadyaḥat once, on the spotindeclinable (adverb of time)
  10. प्राणान्prāṇānhis life-breaths, his lifem. acc. pl. of प्राण (the plural means ‘life’); object of मुमोच
  11. मुमोचmumocagave up, let go√मुच् (tudādi 6U ‘to release’), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  12. महिषःmahiṣaḥMahiṣam. nom. sg. of महिष (‘buffalo’, the asura’s name); subject of दृष्ट्वा and मुमोच
  13. तत्tatthatpronoun तद्, n. nom. sg.; resumes the यत्-clause; subject of the nominal sentence
  14. अतीवatīvaexceedinglyindeclinable (intensive adverb, अति + इव)
  15. चित्रम्citramstrange, astonishingn. nom. sg. of चित्र; predicate of तत्
  16. कैःkaiḥby whom? (who?)interrogative pronoun किम्, m. instr. pl.; logical subject of the impersonal passive जीव्यते
  17. जीव्यतेjīvyateis it lived, does one live√जीव् (bhvādi 1P ‘to live’), bhāve prayoga (impersonal passive), laṭ (present), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana
  18. हिhifor, surelyindeclinable (causal particle)
  19. कुपितान्तकदर्शनेनkupitāntakadarśanenaafter the sight of Death enragedn. instr. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); instrumental of attendant circumstance

Translation

But having seen (your face), O Devī, angry, dreadful with its frown, its hue like the rising moon — that Mahiṣa did not at once give up his life, that is exceedingly strange; for by whom is it lived after the sight of Death enraged?

In readable EnglishBut, O Devī, Mahiṣa saw your face in anger — dreadful with its frown, flushed like the rising moon — and did not die on the spot: that is the stranger wonder. For who can live once he has looked on Death enraged?

‘Your face’ is supplied from वक्त्रम् in 4.12. ‘The stranger wonder’ renders अतीव चित्रम् ‘exceedingly strange’, which answers the अत्यद्भुतम् of 4.12.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • उद्यच्छशाङ्क udyacchaśāṅka → उद्यत् + शशाङ्क

    consonant sandhi inside a compound (palatal assimilation; ś → ch) · स्तोः श्चुना श्चुः (8.4.40); शश्छोऽटि (8.4.63)

    The t of उद्यत् becomes c beside the palatal ś, and the ś after the stop becomes ch: उद्यत् + शशाङ्क → उद्यच्छशाङ्क.

  • सदृशच्छवि sadṛśacchavi → सदृश + छवि

    consonant sandhi inside a compound (doubling of ch) · छे च (6.1.73)

    After a short vowel, ch is doubled (written च्छ). Inside a compound the doubling is obligatory.

  • यन्न yanna → यत् + न

    consonant sandhi (nasal assimilation) · यरोऽनुनासिकेऽनुनासिको वा (8.4.45)

    Final t before the nasal n becomes n.

  • महिषस्तदतीव mahiṣastadatīva → महिषः + तत् + अतीव

    visarga sandhi; consonant sandhi (voicing) · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34); झलां जशोऽन्ते (8.2.39)

    Visarga before t becomes s; the t of तत् is voiced to d before the vowel of अतीव.

  • कैर्जीव्यते kairjīvyate → कैः + जीव्यते

    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)

    The final s of कैः becomes ru; after a vowel other than a/ā it stands as r before a voiced sound (here j).

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • भ्रुकुटीकरालम् bhrukuṭīkarālam

    tṛtīyā tatpuruṣa

    Members
    भ्रुकुटी + कराल
    Vigraha
    भ्रुकुट्या करालम् bhrukuṭyā karālam
    Literally
    “dreadful through the frown”
    In the verse
    a face made terrible by knitted brows

  • उद्यच्छशाङ्कसदृशच्छवि udyacchaśāṅkasadṛśacchavi

    bahuvrīhi, containing a karmadhāraya (उद्यच्छशाङ्क) and a tṛtīyā tatpuruṣa (उद्यच्छशाङ्कसदृशी)

    Members
    उद्यत् + शशाङ्क + सदृश + छवि
    Vigraha
    उद्यन् शशाङ्कः उद्यच्छशाङ्कः; उद्यच्छशाङ्केन सदृशी छविः यस्य तत् udyan śaśāṅkaḥ udyacchaśāṅkaḥ; udyacchaśāṅkena sadṛśī chaviḥ yasya tat
    Literally
    “whose hue is like the rising hare-marked (moon)”
    In the verse
    flushed red, as the moon is when it rises: the colour of anger, kept in the moon-image of 4.12
    Alternative
    शशाङ्क is itself a bahuvrīhi (शशः अङ्के यस्य सः, ‘the one with a hare on its lap’), the moon.

  • कुपितान्तकदर्शनेन kupitāntakadarśanena

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa containing a karmadhāraya (कुपितान्तक)

    Members
    कुपित + अन्तक + दर्शन
    Vigraha
    कुपितः अन्तकः कुपितान्तकः; कुपितान्तकस्य दर्शनम्, तेन kupitaḥ antakaḥ kupitāntakaḥ; kupitāntakasya darśanam, tena
    Literally
    “by the sight of the angry Ender”
    In the verse
    after looking on Death himself in wrath
Morphology & anvaya

  • मुमोच mumoca

    Perfect of √मुच्: reduplication मु- and guṇa of the root vowel in the singular (मोच्). With प्राणान्: ‘gave up his life’.

  • जीव्यते jīvyate

    Impersonal passive (bhāve prayoga) of the intransitive √जीव्: there is no grammatical subject, and the one who lives is put in the instrumental (कैः). The question is rhetorical: no one lives.

  • उद्यच्छशाङ्कसदृशच्छवि udyacchaśāṅkasadṛśacchavi

    A bahuvrīhi takes the gender of what it qualifies. Ending in the feminine छवि, it becomes a neuter i-stem to agree with वक्त्रम्; its acc. sg. is the bare stem.

Anvaya · prose order

(हे) देवि, कुपितं भ्रुकुटीकरालम् उद्यच्छशाङ्कसदृशच्छवि (तव वक्त्रं) दृष्ट्वा तु महिषः यत् सद्यः प्राणान् न मुमोच, तत् अतीव चित्रम्। हि कुपितान्तकदर्शनेन कैः जीव्यते?

(he) devi, kupitaṃ bhrukuṭīkarālam udyacchaśāṅkasadṛśacchavi (tava vaktraṃ) dṛṣṭvā tu mahiṣaḥ yat sadyaḥ prāṇān na mumoca, tat atīva citram | hi kupitāntakadarśanena kaiḥ jīvyate?

The face of 4.12 (वक्त्रम्) is the understood object of दृष्ट्वा. The यत्-clause is the subject that तत् resumes: ‘that he did not die — that is most strange’. The last line is a rhetorical question in the impersonal passive.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The gods continue the paradox of 4.12. That Mahiṣa could strike the gentle, smiling face was strange; that he survived the sight of the same face in anger is stranger still, for the angry Goddess is Death (Antaka) himself.

Traditional interpretation

The two verses are read as a pair: the Goddess is seen first as gentle and then as terrible, the two kinds of form that 4.26 names together (सौम्यानि … अत्यर्थघोराणि). The rising moon is red, so the comparison gives the colour of anger while keeping the moon of 4.12.

Verse 4.14

Śloka · vasantatilakā
‹›

देवि प्रसीद परमा भवती भवायसद्यो विनाशयसि कोपवती कुलानि।विज्ञातमेतदधुनैव यदस्तमेत-न्नीतं बलं सुविपुलं महिषासुरस्य॥⁠१४⁠॥

devi prasīda paramā bhavatī bhavāyasadyo vināśayasi kopavatī kulāni |vijñātametadadhunaiva yadastameta-nnītaṃ balaṃ suvipulaṃ mahiṣāsurasya ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

देवि · प्रसीद · परमा · भवती · भवायसद्यः · विनाशयसि · कोपवती · कुलानिविज्ञातम् · एतत् · अधुना · एव · यत् · अस्तम् · एतत्नीतम् · बलम् · सुविपुलम् · महिषासुरस्य

devi · prasīda · paramā · bhavatī · bhavāyasadyaḥ · vināśayasi · kopavatī · kulānivijñātam · etat · adhunā · eva · yat · astam · etatnītam · balam · suvipulam · mahiṣāsurasya

Word by word

  1. देविdeviO Devīf. voc. sg. of देवी
  2. प्रसीदprasīdabe graciousप्र + √सद् (bhvādi 1P ‘to sit, settle’; present stem सीद-), loṭ (imperative), madhyama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  3. परमाparamāsupremef. nom. sg. of परम; qualifies भवती
  4. भवतीbhavatīyou (Your Ladyship)f. nom. sg. of the honorific pronoun भवत्; subject of a nominal clause
  5. भवायbhavāyalit. ‘for coming-to-be’; here ‘for (our) flourishing, for welfare’m. dat. sg. of भव; dative of purpose
  6. सद्यःsadyaḥat onceindeclinable (adverb of time)
  7. विनाशयसिvināśayasiyou destroyवि + √नश् (divādi 4P ‘to perish’), causative (ṇic) stem नाशय-, laṭ (present), madhyama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  8. कोपवतीkopavatīwhen angry, being wrathfulf. nom. sg. of कोपवत् (कोप + matup ‘possessing anger’); agrees with the understood ‘you’
  9. कुलानिkulānifamilies, whole racesn. acc. pl. of कुल; object of विनाशयसि
  10. विज्ञातम्vijñātam(has been) known, (is) understoodpast passive participle (kta) of वि + √ज्ञा (kryādi 9 ‘to know’), n. nom. sg.; predicate of the first एतत्
  11. एतत्etatthispronoun एतद्, n. nom. sg.; points forward to the यत्-clause
  12. अधुनाadhunānowindeclinable (adverb of time)
  13. एवevajust, this very (moment)indeclinable (emphatic particle)
  14. यत्yatthatindeclinable conjunction introducing what एतत् refers to
  15. अस्तम्astamto its setting, to an endindeclinable अस्तम्; with √नी ‘to bring to an end, destroy’
  16. एतत्etatthispronoun एतद्, n. nom. sg.; qualifies बलम्
  17. नीतम्nītamhas been broughtpast passive participle (kta) of √नी (bhvādi 1U ‘to lead’), n. nom. sg.; predicate of बलम्
  18. बलम्balamarmy, forcen. nom. sg. of बल; subject
  19. सुविपुलम्suvipulamvery vastn. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies बलम्
  20. महिषासुरस्यmahiṣāsurasyaof Mahiṣāsuram. gen. sg.; compound (see Samāsa)

Translation

O Devī, be gracious. You, supreme, are for (our) welfare; angry, you at once destroy whole families. This has been known just now, that this very vast army of Mahiṣāsura has been brought to its end.

In readable EnglishBe gracious, O Devī! Supreme, you are there for our flourishing; in anger you destroy whole races in a moment. We have seen it just now: the vast army of Mahiṣāsura has been brought to nothing.

The contrast of line 1 with कोपवती ‘when angry’ is left implicit, as in the Sanskrit. ‘We have seen it’ renders the impersonal विज्ञातम् ‘it has been known’.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • सद्यो विनाशयसि sadyo vināśayasi → सद्यः + विनाशयसि

    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)

    -aḥ before the voiced v becomes -o.

  • अधुनैव adhunaiva → अधुना + एव

    vowel sandhi (vṛddhi) · वृद्धिरेचि (6.1.88)

    ā + e → ai.

  • यदस्तमेत yadastameta → यत् + अस्तम् + एतत्

    consonant sandhi (voicing) · झलां जशोऽन्ते (8.2.39)

    Final t becomes d before a vowel; the m of अस्तम् simply joins the next vowel.

  • एतन्नीतं etannītaṃ → एतत् + नीतम्

    consonant sandhi (nasal assimilation) across the line break · यरोऽनुनासिकेऽनुनासिको वा (8.4.45)

    t before n becomes n. The word is broken across the printed lines (एत- / न्नीतं) but recited as one junction; एतत् belongs to the third line, where it begins.

  • महिषासुरस्य mahiṣāsurasya → महिष + असुरस्य

    vowel sandhi inside a compound (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    a + a → ā.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • सुविपुलम् suvipulam

    tatpuruṣa of the prādi kind (the prefix सु ‘well, very’ with an adjective)

    Members
    सु + विपुल
    Vigraha
    सुष्ठु विपुलम् suṣṭhu vipulam
    Literally
    “well-vast”
    In the verse
    immense

  • महिषासुरस्य mahiṣāsurasya

    karmadhāraya

    Members
    महिष + असुर
    Vigraha
    महिषः च असौ असुरः च, तस्य mahiṣaḥ ca asau asuraḥ ca, tasya
    Literally
    “of the asura who is Mahiṣa”
    In the verse
    of the buffalo-demon
    Alternative
    Also analysed with the middle member dropped: महिषरूपः असुरः, ‘the asura in buffalo form’. Since महिष is his name in 4.13 and throughout, the plain appositional reading fits.
Morphology & anvaya

  • प्रसीद prasīda

    √सद् takes the stem सीद- in the present system: प्र-सीद ‘settle down, become clear’, and so ‘be pleased, be gracious’.

  • विनाशयसि vināśayasi

    Causative of √नश् ‘perish’: the root vowel is lengthened (नाश्) and -अय- added — ‘cause to perish’, destroy.

  • नीतम् nītam

    With अस्तम् this is an idiom: अस्तं नी ‘lead to the setting (as of the sun)’, i.e. bring to an end. The agent, ‘by you’, is understood.

Anvaya · prose order

(हे) देवि, प्रसीद। परमा भवती भवाय (भवति); कोपवती (सती त्वं) सद्यः कुलानि विनाशयसि। यत् एतत् सुविपुलं महिषासुरस्य बलम् अस्तं नीतम्, एतत् अधुना एव विज्ञातम्।

(he) devi, prasīda | paramā bhavatī bhavāya (bhavati); kopavatī (satī tvaṃ) sadyaḥ kulāni vināśayasi | yat etat suvipulaṃ mahiṣāsurasya balam astaṃ nītam, etat adhunā eva vijñātam |

Line 1 is a prayer followed by a nominal clause (‘you are for welfare’); line 2 states the opposite (‘angry, you destroy’). In lines 3–4 the first एतत् ‘this’ is explained by the यत्-clause.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

A plea, and the lesson of the battle just ended. The same Goddess who is supreme for the world’s welfare destroys whole races when angered; the gods have just watched Mahiṣa’s army go down.

Traditional interpretation

The sound-play भवती / भवाय (‘you’ / ‘for welfare’) is in the Sanskrit. The verse is read as the reason for the prayer प्रसीद: the face of anger has done its work, and the gods ask her to turn to them the face of grace.

Verse 4.15

Śloka · vasantatilakā
‹›

ते सम्मता जनपदेषु धनानि तेषांतेषां यशांसि न च सीदति धर्मवर्गः।धन्यास्त एव निभृतात्मजभृत्यदारायेषां सदाभ्युदयदा भवती प्रसन्ना॥⁠१५⁠॥

te sammatā janapadeṣu dhanāni teṣāṃteṣāṃ yaśāṃsi na ca sīdati dharmavargaḥ |dhanyāsta eva nibhṛtātmajabhṛtyadārāyeṣāṃ sadābhyudayadā bhavatī prasannā ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

ते · सम्मताः · जनपदेषु · धनानि · तेषाम्तेषाम् · यशांसि · न · च · सीदति · धर्मवर्गःधन्याः · ते · एव · निभृतात्मजभृत्यदाराःयेषाम् · सदा · अभ्युदयदा · भवती · प्रसन्ना

te · sammatāḥ · janapadeṣu · dhanāni · teṣāmteṣām · yaśāṃsi · na · ca · sīdati · dharmavargaḥdhanyāḥ · te · eva · nibhṛtātmajabhṛtyadārāḥyeṣām · sadā · abhyudayadā · bhavatī · prasannā

Word by word

  1. तेtetheypronoun तद्, m. nom. pl.; subject
  2. सम्मताःsammatāḥhonoured, esteemedpast passive participle (kta) of सम् + √मन् (divādi 4Ā ‘to think’), m. nom. pl.; predicate
  3. जनपदेषुjanapadeṣuin the lands, among the peoplem. loc. pl. of जनपद
  4. धनानिdhanānirichesn. nom. pl. of धन; subject of a nominal clause (‘theirs are riches’)
  5. तेषाम्teṣāmtheirspronoun तद्, m. gen. pl.; possessive
  6. तेषाम्teṣāmtheirspronoun तद्, m. gen. pl.; possessive, repeated for emphasis
  7. यशांसिyaśāṃsiglories, renownn. nom. pl. of यशस् (s-stem)
  8. नnanotindeclinable (negative particle)
  9. चcaandindeclinable (conjunctive particle)
  10. सीदतिsīdatisinks, fails√सद् (bhvādi 1P; present stem सीद-), laṭ (present), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  11. धर्मवर्गःdharmavargaḥlit. ‘the group of dharma’; here ‘their righteous life in all its parts’m. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); subject of सीदति
  12. धन्याःdhanyāḥblessed, fortunatem. nom. pl. of धन्य; predicate
  13. तेtetheypronoun तद्, m. nom. pl.; subject
  14. एवevatruly, they aloneindeclinable (emphatic particle)
  15. निभृतात्मजभृत्यदाराःnibhṛtātmajabhṛtyadārāḥwhose sons, servants and wives are loyalm. nom. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies ते
  16. येषाम्yeṣāmto whom (lit. of whom)relative pronoun यद्, m. gen. pl.; construed with प्रसन्ना
  17. सदाsadāalwaysindeclinable (adverb of time)
  18. अभ्युदयदाabhyudayadāthe giver of prosperityf. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies भवती
  19. भवतीbhavatīyou (Your Ladyship)f. nom. sg. of the honorific pronoun भवत्; subject
  20. प्रसन्नाprasannāgracious, pleasedpast passive participle (kta) of प्र + √सद्, f. nom. sg.; predicate (अस्ति understood)

Translation

They are honoured in the lands; theirs are riches, theirs are glories, and their body of dharma does not sink; blessed indeed are they, whose sons, servants and wives are loyal — to whom you, giver of prosperity, are always gracious.

In readable EnglishThose to whom you, the giver of prosperity, are always gracious are honoured in every land. Wealth is theirs, and fame, and their righteousness never fails. Blessed are they, and loyal their sons, their servants and their wives.

The relative clause of line 4 is placed first in English.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • सम्मता जनपदेषु sammatā janapadeṣu → सम्मताः + जनपदेषु

    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), भोभगोअघोअपूर्वस्य योऽशि (8.3.17)

    -āḥ before a voiced consonant: the visarga becomes y, and the y is dropped before the consonant; the long ā remains.

  • तेषां तेषां teṣāṃ teṣāṃ → तेषाम् + तेषाम्

    anusvāra sandhi · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)

    Final m before a consonant is written as anusvāra (here also at the line’s end, as printed).

  • धन्यास्त एव dhanyāsta eva → धन्याः + ते + एव

    visarga sandhi; vowel sandhi (ayādi with loss of y) · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34); एचोऽयवायावः (6.1.78), लोपः शाकल्यस्य (8.3.19)

    Visarga before t becomes s. Then -e before e becomes -ay, the y is dropped, and the vowels stand apart: त एव.

  • सदाभ्युदयदा sadābhyudayadā → सदा + अभ्युदयदा

    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    ā + a → ā.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • धर्मवर्गः dharmavargaḥ

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa

    Members
    धर्म + वर्ग
    Vigraha
    धर्मस्य वर्गः dharmasya vargaḥ
    Literally
    “the group of dharma”
    In the verse
    their righteous life as a whole — duties, rites and conduct
    Alternative
    वर्ग may point to the trivarga (dharma, artha, kāma) with dharma at its head; the plain sense, that their dharma never falters, is the same.

  • निभृतात्मजभृत्यदाराः nibhṛtātmajabhṛtyadārāḥ

    bahuvrīhi containing an itaretara dvandva (आत्मजभृत्यदाराः)

    Members
    निभृत + आत्मज + भृत्य + दार
    Vigraha
    आत्मजाः च भृत्याः च दाराः च आत्मजभृत्यदाराः; निभृताः आत्मजभृत्यदाराः येषां ते ātmajāḥ ca bhṛtyāḥ ca dārāḥ ca ātmajabhṛtyadārāḥ; nibhṛtāḥ ātmajabhṛtyadārāḥ yeṣāṃ te
    Literally
    “whose sons, servants and wives are steady (devoted)”
    In the verse
    men whose households are loyal and at peace
    Alternative
    आत्मज is itself an upapada tatpuruṣa (आत्मनः जायते इति, ‘born of oneself’), a son. दार ‘wife’ is masculine and used in the plural.

  • अभ्युदयदा abhyudayadā

    upapada tatpuruṣa

    Members
    अभ्युदय + दा
    Vigraha
    अभ्युदयं ददाति इति abhyudayaṃ dadāti iti
    Literally
    “prosperity-giving”
    In the verse
    she who grants worldly flourishing
Morphology & anvaya

  • सम्मताः sammatāḥ

    Past participle of सम् + √मन्: the root’s nasal drops before -त (मन् + त → मत). ‘Thought well of’, honoured.

  • सीदति sīdati

    Present of √सद् ‘sit, sink’ (stem सीद-). The same root with प्र gives प्रसन्ना in line 4: their dharma does not sink, because she is ‘settled’ in grace towards them.

  • प्रसन्ना prasannā

    Past participle of प्र + √सद्: the d of the root and the t of the suffix both become n (सद् + त → सन्न). With the genitive येषाम्: ‘gracious to whom’.

Anvaya · prose order

येषां (प्रति) अभ्युदयदा भवती सदा प्रसन्ना (भवति), ते जनपदेषु सम्मताः, तेषां धनानि, तेषां यशांसि, (तेषां) धर्मवर्गः च न सीदति; निभृतात्मजभृत्यदाराः ते एव धन्याः।

yeṣāṃ (prati) abhyudayadā bhavatī sadā prasannā (bhavati), te janapadeṣu sammatāḥ, teṣāṃ dhanāni, teṣāṃ yaśāṃsi, (teṣāṃ) dharmavargaḥ ca na sīdati; nibhṛtātmajabhṛtyadārāḥ te eva dhanyāḥ |

The relative clause of line 4 (येषां … प्रसन्ना) defines the ते of lines 1 and 3. The main clauses are nominal (‘theirs are riches’) except सीदति.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The fruits of her grace in this world are listed: honour, wealth, fame, a dharma that does not fail and a household at peace. All of them hang on the condition of the last line: that she is gracious (प्रसन्ना).

Traditional interpretation

The verse turns from the battle to the worshipper. Its blessings are the householder’s abhyudaya, worldly flourishing; the next verse carries them on to heaven, and the hymn’s closing requests (4.37) ask for the same wealth, family and prosperity for whoever recites it.

Verse 4.16

Śloka · vasantatilakā
‹›

धर्म्याणि देवि सकलानि सदैव कर्मा-ण्यत्यादृतः प्रतिदिनं सुकृती करोति।स्वर्गं प्रयाति च ततो भवतीप्रसादा-ल्लोकत्रयेऽपि फलदा ननु देवि तेन॥⁠१६⁠॥

dharmyāṇi devi sakalāni sadaiva karmā-ṇyatyādṛtaḥ pratidinaṃ sukṛtī karoti |svargaṃ prayāti ca tato bhavatīprasādā-llokatraye'pi phaladā nanu devi tena ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

धर्म्याणि · देवि · सकलानि · सदा · एव · कर्माणिअत्यादृतः · प्रतिदिनम् · सुकृती · करोतिस्वर्गम् · प्रयाति · च · ततः · भवतीप्रसादात्लोकत्रये · अपि · फलदा · ननु · देवि · तेन

dharmyāṇi · devi · sakalāni · sadā · eva · karmāṇiatyādṛtaḥ · pratidinam · sukṛtī · karotisvargam · prayāti · ca · tataḥ · bhavatīprasādātlokatraye · api · phaladā · nanu · devi · tena

Word by word

  1. धर्म्याणिdharmyāṇirighteous, in accord with dharman. acc. pl. of धर्म्य (taddhita -य from धर्म: ‘not departing from dharma’); qualifies कर्माणि
  2. देविdeviO Devīf. voc. sg. of देवी
  3. सकलानिsakalānialln. acc. pl. of सकल; qualifies कर्माणि
  4. सदाsadāalwaysindeclinable (adverb of time)
  5. एवevaindeed, without failindeclinable (emphatic particle)
  6. कर्माणिkarmāṇideeds, ritesn. acc. pl. of कर्मन्; object of करोति
  7. अत्यादृतःatyādṛtaḥwith great care, wholly devotedpast participle (kta) of अति + आ + √दृ (tudādi 6Ā ‘to heed, respect’), active in sense, m. nom. sg.; qualifies सुकृती
  8. प्रतिदिनम्pratidinamevery dayindeclinable; compound (see Samāsa)
  9. सुकृतीsukṛtīthe man of merit, the virtuous manm. nom. sg. of सुकृतिन् (सुकृत + possessive -इन्); subject of करोति and प्रयाति
  10. करोतिkarotiperforms√कृ (tanādi 8U ‘to do’), laṭ (present), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  11. स्वर्गम्svargamto heavenm. acc. sg. of स्वर्ग; accusative of the goal
  12. प्रयातिprayātigoesप्र + √या (adādi 2P ‘to go’), laṭ (present), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  13. चcaandindeclinable (conjunctive particle)
  14. ततःtataḥthen, thereuponindeclinable (तद् + the ablative suffix -तस्)
  15. भवतीप्रसादात्bhavatīprasādātthrough your gracem. abl. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); ablative of cause
  16. लोकत्रयेlokatrayein the three worldsn. loc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa)
  17. अपिapieven, in all of themindeclinable (particle of inclusion)
  18. फलदाphaladāthe giver of the fruit (of action)f. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); predicate, with ‘you are’ understood
  19. ननुnanusurely, is it not so?indeclinable (particle of assertion that appeals for agreement)
  20. देविdeviO Devīf. voc. sg. of देवी
  21. तेनtenatherefore, for that reasonpronoun तद्, n. instr. sg., used adverbially

Translation

O Devī, the man of merit, with great care, every day always performs all righteous deeds; and then, through your grace, he goes to heaven. Therefore, O Devī, are you not the giver of fruit even in the three worlds?

In readable EnglishO Devī, the virtuous man performs every righteous deed, day after day, with devoted care, and then by your grace he goes to heaven. So is it not you, O Devī, who give the fruit of action in all three worlds?

ननु is rendered as a question expecting ‘yes’.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • सदैव sadaiva → सदा + एव

    vowel sandhi (vṛddhi) · वृद्धिरेचि (6.1.88)

    ā + e → ai.

  • कर्माण्यत्यादृतः karmāṇyatyādṛtaḥ → कर्माणि + अत्यादृतः

    vowel sandhi (yaṇ) across the line break · इको यणचि (6.1.77)

    Final i before a vowel becomes y. The word is broken across the printed lines (कर्मा- / ण्यत्यादृतः); कर्माणि belongs to the first line, where it begins. Inside अत्यादृतः the same rule joins अति + आ.

  • ततो भवती tato bhavatī → ततः + भवतीप्रसादात्

    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)

    -aḥ before the voiced bh becomes -o.

  • भवतीप्रसादाल्लोकत्रये bhavatīprasādāllokatraye → भवतीप्रसादात् + लोकत्रये

    consonant sandhi (t + l → ll) across the line break · तोर्लि (8.4.60)

    Final t before l becomes l. Printed भवतीप्रसादा- / ल्लोकत्रये.

  • लोकत्रयेऽपि lokatraye'pi → लोकत्रये + अपि

    vowel sandhi (pūrvarūpa) · एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109)

    -e + a- → -e’-: the initial a is elided (avagraha).

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • प्रतिदिनम् pratidinam

    avyayībhāva

    Members
    प्रति + दिन
    Vigraha
    दिनं दिनं प्रति dinaṃ dinaṃ prati
    Literally
    “day by day”
    In the verse
    every day, without a break

  • भवतीप्रसादात् bhavatīprasādāt

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa

    Members
    भवती + प्रसाद
    Vigraha
    भवत्याः प्रसादः, तस्मात् bhavatyāḥ prasādaḥ, tasmāt
    Literally
    “from Your Ladyship’s grace”
    In the verse
    by your favour

  • लोकत्रये lokatraye

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa

    Members
    लोक + त्रय
    Vigraha
    लोकानां त्रयम्, तस्मिन् lokānāṃ trayam, tasmin
    Literally
    “in the triad of worlds”
    In the verse
    in earth, the middle region and heaven alike

  • फलदा phaladā

    upapada tatpuruṣa

    Members
    फल + दा
    Vigraha
    फलं ददाति इति phalaṃ dadāti iti
    Literally
    “fruit-giving”
    In the verse
    she who gives deeds their reward
Morphology & anvaya

  • अत्यादृतः atyādṛtaḥ

    Past participle of आ + √दृ ‘to heed’, here active in sense, as often with this verb: ‘one who has given heed’, careful, devoted. अति intensifies it.

  • सुकृती sukṛtī

    Possessive -इन् on सुकृत ‘a good deed’: सुकृतिन्, ‘one who has good deeds’; nom. sg. सुकृती.

  • ननु nanu

    A particle that asserts while inviting assent: ‘surely you are …, are you not?’

Anvaya · prose order

(हे) देवि, सुकृती अत्यादृतः (सन्) प्रतिदिनं सकलानि धर्म्याणि कर्माणि सदा एव करोति, ततः च भवतीप्रसादात् स्वर्गं प्रयाति। तेन (हे) देवि, (त्वं) लोकत्रये अपि फलदा ननु।

(he) devi, sukṛtī atyādṛtaḥ (san) pratidinaṃ sakalāni dharmyāṇi karmāṇi sadā eva karoti, tataḥ ca bhavatīprasādāt svargaṃ prayāti | tena (he) devi, (tvaṃ) lokatraye api phaladā nanu |

Two clauses with one subject (सुकृती): he does the deeds, and then reaches heaven through her grace. The conclusion (तेन … ननु) is a nominal sentence with ‘you’ understood.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The good man’s deeds bear their fruit only through her grace (भवतीप्रसादात्). The gods conclude that she is the giver of reward in all three worlds, not in heaven alone.

Traditional interpretation

Ritual duty is not set aside: it is done daily and with care. But its fruit comes from her favour, and this is why, in the Śākta tradition, devotion is joined to duty rather than replacing it.

Comparative Śrīvidyā notes

Comparative Śākta / Śrīvidyā

Lalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) names the Goddess स्वर्गापवर्गदा (name 764), ‘giver of heaven and of release’. The Devī Māhātmya speaks here of heaven and of fruit in the three worlds; release is the subject of 4.9 (मुक्तिहेतुः).

Verse 4.17

Śloka · vasantatilakā
‹›

दुर्गे स्मृता हरसि भीतिमशेषजन्तोःस्वस्थैः स्मृता मतिमतीव शुभां ददासि।दारिद्र्यदुःखभयहारिणि का त्वदन्यासर्वोपकारकरणाय सदाऽऽर्द्रचित्ता॥⁠१७⁠॥

durge smṛtā harasi bhītimaśeṣajantoḥsvasthaiḥ smṛtā matimatīva śubhāṃ dadāsi |dāridryaduḥkhabhayahāriṇi kā tvadanyāsarvopakārakaraṇāya sadā''rdracittā ||

दुर्गे · स्मृता · हरसि · भीतिम् · अशेषजन्तोःस्वस्थैः · स्मृता · मतिम् · अतीव · शुभाम् · ददासिदारिद्र्यदुःखभयहारिणि · का · त्वदन्यासर्वोपकारकरणाय · सदा · आर्द्रचित्ता

durge · smṛtā · harasi · bhītim · aśeṣajantoḥsvasthaiḥ · smṛtā · matim · atīva · śubhām · dadāsidāridryaduḥkhabhayahāriṇi · kā · tvadanyāsarvopakārakaraṇāya · sadā · ārdracittā

Word by word

  1. दुर्गेdurgeO Durgāf. voc. sg. of दुर्गा
  2. स्मृताsmṛtā(when) remembered, called to mindpast passive participle (kta) of √स्मृ (bhvādi 1P ‘to remember’), f. nom. sg.; agrees with the understood ‘you’
  3. हरसिharasiyou take away√हृ (bhvādi 1U ‘to take’), laṭ (present), madhyama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  4. भीतिम्bhītimfearf. acc. sg. of भीति; object of हरसि
  5. अशेषजन्तोःaśeṣajantoḥof every creaturem. gen. sg.; compound (see Samāsa)
  6. स्वस्थैःsvasthaiḥby those at ease (well in body and mind)m. instr. pl. of स्वस्थ; compound (see Samāsa); agent of स्मृता
  7. स्मृताsmṛtā(when) rememberedpast passive participle (kta) of √स्मृ, f. nom. sg.
  8. मतिम्matimunderstanding, a frame of mindf. acc. sg. of मति; object of ददासि
  9. अतीवatīvaexceedinglyindeclinable (intensive adverb, अति + इव)
  10. शुभाम्śubhāmauspicious, goodf. acc. sg. of शुभ; qualifies मतिम्
  11. ददासिdadāsiyou give√दा (juhotyādi 3U ‘to give’), laṭ (present), madhyama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  12. दारिद्र्यदुःखभयहारिणिdāridryaduḥkhabhayahāriṇiO remover of poverty, sorrow and fearf. voc. sg. of दारिद्र्यदुःखभयहारिणी; compound (see Samāsa)
  13. काkāwho?interrogative pronoun किम्, f. nom. sg.; subject
  14. त्वदन्याtvadanyāother than youf. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies का
  15. सर्वोपकारकरणायsarvopakārakaraṇāyafor doing good to alln. dat. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); dative of purpose
  16. सदाsadāalwaysindeclinable (adverb of time)
  17. आर्द्रचित्ताārdracittātender-hearted (lit. ‘moist-minded’)f. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); predicate of का

Translation

O Durgā, remembered, you take away the fear of every creature; remembered by those at ease, you give an exceedingly auspicious understanding. O remover of poverty, sorrow and fear, who other than you is always tender-hearted for doing good to all?

In readable EnglishO Durgā, when you are remembered you take away the fear of every creature; remembered by those at ease, you give a mind turned wholly to the good. Remover of poverty, sorrow and fear — who but you has a heart ever tender, ready to help all?

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • स्वस्थैः स्मृता svasthaiḥ smṛtā → स्वस्थैः + स्मृता

    visarga sandhi (visarga kept before a sibilant) · खरवसानयोर्विसर्जनीयः (8.3.15)

    Before s the visarga may stand (as printed here) or be assimilated to the sibilant (स्वस्थैस्स्मृता); the Gita Press keeps the visarga.

  • शुभां ददासि śubhāṃ dadāsi → शुभाम् + ददासि

    anusvāra sandhi · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)

    Final m before a consonant is written as anusvāra.

  • त्वदन्या tvadanyā → त्वत् + अन्या

    consonant sandhi inside a compound (voicing) · झलां जशोऽन्ते (8.2.39)

    The pronoun stem त्वत् ends in d before a vowel.

  • सर्वोपकार sarvopakāra → सर्व + उपकार

    vowel sandhi inside a compound (guṇa) · आद्गुणः (6.1.87)

    a + u → o.

  • सदाऽऽर्द्रचित्ता sadā''rdracittā → सदा + आर्द्रचित्ता

    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    ā + ā → ā. The Gita Press marks the absorbed long vowel with a double avagraha (ऽऽ), a reading aid only.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • अशेषजन्तोः aśeṣajantoḥ

    karmadhāraya (its first member अशेष is a nañ-bahuvrīhi: न विद्यते शेषः यस्य)

    Members
    अशेष + जन्तु
    Vigraha
    अशेषः जन्तुः, तस्य aśeṣaḥ jantuḥ, tasya
    Literally
    “of a creature without remainder”
    In the verse
    of every living being, none excepted
    Alternative
    It can also be taken as a collective, ‘of all creatures’ (अशेषाणां जन्तूनाम्); the singular is then distributive. The sense is the same.

  • स्वस्थैः svasthaiḥ

    upapada tatpuruṣa

    Members
    स्व + स्थ
    Vigraha
    स्वे तिष्ठन्ति इति स्वस्थाः, तैः sve tiṣṭhanti iti svasthāḥ, taiḥ
    Literally
    “by those who stand in themselves”
    In the verse
    by the healthy and untroubled, those not in distress

  • दारिद्र्यदुःखभयहारिणि dāridryaduḥkhabhayahāriṇi

    upapada tatpuruṣa whose first member is an itaretara dvandva

    Members
    दारिद्र्य + दुःख + भय + हारिणी
    Vigraha
    दारिद्र्यं च दुःखं च भयं च दारिद्र्यदुःखभयानि; तानि हरति इति दारिद्र्यदुःखभयहारिणी, तत्सम्बुद्धौ dāridryaṃ ca duḥkhaṃ ca bhayaṃ ca dāridryaduḥkhabhayāni; tāni harati iti dāridryaduḥkhabhayahāriṇī, tatsambuddhau
    Literally
    “O you who take away poverty, sorrow and fear”
    In the verse
    O remover of want, grief and fear
    Alternative
    दारिद्र्यदुःख may be read as a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa, ‘the sorrow of poverty’ (दारिद्र्यस्य दुःखम्), giving two things removed instead of three; the list of three is the usual reading.

  • त्वदन्या tvadanyā

    pañcamī tatpuruṣa

    Members
    त्वत् + अन्या
    Vigraha
    त्वत् अन्या tvat anyā
    Literally
    “other than you”
    In the verse
    anyone but you

  • सर्वोपकारकरणाय sarvopakārakaraṇāya

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa containing a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (सर्वोपकार)

    Members
    सर्व + उपकार + करण
    Vigraha
    सर्वेषाम् उपकारः सर्वोपकारः; तस्य करणम्, तस्मै sarveṣām upakāraḥ sarvopakāraḥ; tasya karaṇam, tasmai
    Literally
    “for the doing of help to all”
    In the verse
    to do good to everyone

  • आर्द्रचित्ता ārdracittā

    bahuvrīhi

    Members
    आर्द्र + चित्त
    Vigraha
    आर्द्रं चित्तं यस्याः सा ārdraṃ cittaṃ yasyāḥ sā
    Literally
    “she whose mind is moist”
    In the verse
    soft-hearted, compassionate
Morphology & anvaya

  • स्मृता smṛtā

    A passive participle with the force of a condition: ‘(when you are) remembered’. In line 2 its agent is स्वस्थैः; in line 1 the agent is left open, so anyone at all who remembers her.

  • ददासि dadāsi

    Reduplicated present of √दा (juhotyādi class): द-दा-सि ‘you give’.

  • दारिद्र्यदुःखभयहारिणि dāridryaduḥkhabhayahāriṇi

    Vocative of the feminine of हारिन् (√हृ + -इन्, ‘taking away’), which adds -ई: हारिणी, voc. हारिणि.

Anvaya · prose order

(हे) दुर्गे, स्मृता (त्वम्) अशेषजन्तोः भीतिं हरसि; स्वस्थैः स्मृता (त्वम्) अतीव शुभां मतिं ददासि। (हे) दारिद्र्यदुःखभयहारिणि, सर्वोपकारकरणाय सदा आर्द्रचित्ता त्वदन्या का (अस्ति)?

(he) durge, smṛtā (tvam) aśeṣajantoḥ bhītiṃ harasi; svasthaiḥ smṛtā (tvam) atīva śubhāṃ matiṃ dadāsi | (he) dāridryaduḥkhabhayahāriṇi, sarvopakārakaraṇāya sadā ārdracittā tvadanyā kā (asti)?

Two parallel clauses, each conditioned by स्मृता; then a rhetorical question, ‘who other than you …?’, whose answer is ‘no one’.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

Two kinds of people remember her: any creature in fear, whose fear she removes, and those at ease (स्वस्थैः), to whom she gives a good understanding. The verse ends by asking who else is so tender towards all; the unspoken answer is no one.

Traditional interpretation

Remembrance alone (स्मृता) is enough; no rite or offering is named. The verse is one of the seven gathered in the Saptaślokī Durgā, a short devotional selection from the Saptaśatī, and it is among the best known of the whole text.

Comparative Śrīvidyā notes

Comparative Śākta / Śrīvidyā

Lalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) has the names दुर्गा (190), दुःखहन्त्री (191) and भयापहा (121), close to this verse’s दुर्गे and दारिद्र्यदुःखभयहारिणि. The parallel is one of epithets; the two hymns are separate works.

Verse 4.18

Śloka · vasantatilakā
‹›

एभिर्हतैर्जगदुपैति सुखं तथैतेकुर्वन्तु नाम नरकाय चिराय पापम्।संग्राममृत्युमधिगम्य दिवं प्रयान्तुमत्वेति नूनमहितान् विनिहंसि देवि॥⁠१८⁠॥

ebhirhatairjagadupaiti sukhaṃ tathaitekurvantu nāma narakāya cirāya pāpam |saṃgrāmamṛtyumadhigamya divaṃ prayāntumatveti nūnamahitān vinihaṃsi devi ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

एभिः · हतैः · जगत् · उपैति · सुखम् · तथा · एतेकुर्वन्तु · नाम · नरकाय · चिराय · पापम्संग्राममृत्युम् · अधिगम्य · दिवम् · प्रयान्तुमत्वा · इति · नूनम् · अहितान् · विनिहंसि · देवि

ebhiḥ · hataiḥ · jagat · upaiti · sukham · tathā · etekurvantu · nāma · narakāya · cirāya · pāpamsaṃgrāmamṛtyum · adhigamya · divam · prayāntumatvā · iti · nūnam · ahitān · vinihaṃsi · devi

Word by word

  1. एभिःebhiḥthese (asuras)pronoun इदम्, m. instr. pl.; with हतैः
  2. हतैःhataiḥslainpast passive participle (kta) of √हन् (adādi 2P ‘to strike, kill’), m. instr. pl.; with एभिः an instrumental of cause: ‘by these being slain’
  3. जगत्jagatthe worldn. nom. sg. of जगत्; subject of उपैति
  4. उपैतिupaitiattains, comes toउप + √इ (adādi 2P ‘to go’), laṭ (present), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  5. सुखम्sukhamhappinessn. acc. sg. of सुख; object of उपैति
  6. तथाtathāand so, likewiseindeclinable (adverb of manner, connective)
  7. एतेetethese (asuras)pronoun एतद्, m. nom. pl.; subject of कुर्वन्तु and प्रयान्तु
  8. कुर्वन्तुkurvantulet them do — granted they have done√कृ (tanādi 8U ‘to do’), loṭ (imperative), prathama-puruṣa bahuvacana, parasmaipada; concessive with नाम
  9. नामnāmagranted, admittedlyindeclinable (particle; here concessive)
  10. नरकायnarakāya(such as leads) to hellm. dat. sg. of नरक; dative of result
  11. चिरायcirāyafor a long timeindeclinable (dative of चिर used as an adverb)
  12. पापम्pāpamsinn. acc. sg. of पाप; object of कुर्वन्तु
  13. संग्राममृत्युम्saṃgrāmamṛtyumdeath in battlem. acc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); object of अधिगम्य
  14. अधिगम्यadhigamyahaving attained, having metabsolutive (lyap) of अधि + √गम् (bhvādi 1P ‘to go’)
  15. दिवम्divamto heavenf. acc. sg. of दिव् (‘sky, heaven’); accusative of the goal
  16. प्रयान्तुprayāntulet them goप्र + √या (adādi 2P ‘to go’), loṭ (imperative), prathama-puruṣa bahuvacana, parasmaipada
  17. मत्वाmatvāhaving thoughtabsolutive (ktvā) of √मन् (divādi 4Ā ‘to think’)
  18. इतिitithus (closing the thought)indeclinable (quotative particle); closes the thought of lines 1–3
  19. नूनम्nūnamsurelyindeclinable (particle of certainty)
  20. अहितान्ahitānthe hostile, enemiesm. acc. pl. of अहित; compound (see Samāsa); object of विनिहंसि
  21. विनिहंसिvinihaṃsiyou strike downवि + नि + √हन् (adādi 2P), laṭ (present), madhyama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  22. देविdeviO Devīf. voc. sg. of देवी

Translation

‘By these being slain the world attains happiness; and likewise these — granted they do sin (leading) to hell for long — having met death in battle, let them go to heaven’: having thought thus, surely, you strike down the enemies, O Devī.

In readable English‘With these slain, the world will find happiness; and though they have earned a long hell by their sins, let them meet death in battle and go to heaven’ — surely it is with this thought, O Devī, that you strike your enemies down.

कुर्वन्तु नाम (‘let them do, granted’) is rendered as a concession, ‘though they have earned … by their sins’.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • एभिर्हतैर्जगदुपैति ebhirhatairjagadupaiti → एभिः + हतैः + जगत् + उपैति

    visarga sandhi; consonant sandhi (voicing) · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66); झलां जशोऽन्ते (8.2.39)

    After i and ai, the visarga (as ru) stands as r before the voiced h and j. The t of जगत् becomes d before the vowel of उपैति.

  • उपैति upaiti → उप + एति

    vowel sandhi between prefix and verb (vṛddhi) · वृद्धिरेचि (6.1.88)

    a + e → ai. With most verbs beginning in e a prefix in a gives e (the a is absorbed), but forms of √इ ‘go’ keep the vṛddhi: उप + एति → उपैति.

  • तथैते tathaite → तथा + एते

    vowel sandhi (vṛddhi) · वृद्धिरेचि (6.1.88)

    ā + e → ai.

  • मत्वेति matveti → मत्वा + इति

    vowel sandhi (guṇa) · आद्गुणः (6.1.87)

    ā + i → e.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • संग्राममृत्युम् saṃgrāmamṛtyum

    saptamī tatpuruṣa

    Members
    संग्राम + मृत्यु
    Vigraha
    संग्रामे मृत्युः, तम् saṃgrāme mṛtyuḥ, tam
    Literally
    “death in battle”
    In the verse
    a warrior’s death on the field

  • अहितान् ahitān

    nañ tatpuruṣa

    Members
    अ + हित
    Vigraha
    न हिताः, तान् na hitāḥ, tān
    Literally
    “the not-friendly”
    In the verse
    enemies
Morphology & anvaya

  • कुर्वन्तु kurvantu

    Imperative with the particle नाम has concessive force: ‘let them indeed do (sin)’, i.e. ‘granted that they sin’. It sets up the contrast with प्रयान्तु ‘(yet) let them go (to heaven)’.

  • दिवम् divam

    Accusative singular of दिव् (nominative द्यौः), ‘sky, heaven’, feminine in this sense.

  • विनिहंसि vinihaṃsi

    Present of √हन् with two prefixes: हन् + सि → हंसि (the n becomes anusvāra before s). The 2nd person addresses the Goddess directly.

Anvaya · prose order

एभिः हतैः जगत् सुखम् उपैति; तथा एते चिराय नरकाय पापं कुर्वन्तु नाम, (तथापि) संग्राममृत्युम् अधिगम्य दिवं प्रयान्तु — इति मत्वा नूनं (हे) देवि, (त्वम्) अहितान् विनिहंसि।

ebhiḥ hataiḥ jagat sukham upaiti; tathā ete cirāya narakāya pāpaṃ kurvantu nāma, (tathāpi) saṃgrāmamṛtyum adhigamya divaṃ prayāntu — iti matvā nūnaṃ (he) devi, (tvam) ahitān vinihaṃsi |

Lines 1–3 are the Goddess’s thought as the gods imagine it, closed by इति; the main clause is मत्वा … विनिहंसि.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The gods explain why she kills: the world is freed, and the slain asuras themselves, whatever their sins, reach heaven through death in battle at her hands. One act serves both the world and the killed.

Traditional interpretation

That death in battle opens heaven belongs to the warrior ethic of the epics and Purāṇas; the hymn applies it to the Goddess’s enemies. With 4.19–20 it builds the theme the gods state outright in 4.21: her compassion reaches even her enemies (वैरिष्वपि … दया).

Modern reflection

A modern reading: the verse refuses to divide beings into those who deserve good and those who do not. Even her destruction is imagined as a form of care.

Verse 4.19

Śloka · vasantatilakā
‹›

दृष्ट्वैव किं न भवती प्रकरोति भस्मसर्वासुरानरिषु यत्प्रहिणोषि शस्त्रम्।लोकान् प्रयान्तु रिपवोऽपि हि शस्त्रपूताइत्थं मतिर्भवति तेष्वपि तेऽतिसाध्वी॥⁠१९⁠॥

dṛṣṭvaiva kiṃ na bhavatī prakaroti bhasmasarvāsurānariṣu yatprahiṇoṣi śastram |lokān prayāntu ripavo'pi hi śastrapūtāitthaṃ matirbhavati teṣvapi te'tisādhvī ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

दृष्ट्वा · एव · किम् · न · भवती · प्रकरोति · भस्मसर्वासुरान् · अरिषु · यत् · प्रहिणोषि · शस्त्रम्लोकान् · प्रयान्तु · रिपवः · अपि · हि · शस्त्रपूताःइत्थम् · मतिः · भवति · तेषु · अपि · ते · अतिसाध्वी

dṛṣṭvā · eva · kim · na · bhavatī · prakaroti · bhasmasarvāsurān · ariṣu · yat · prahiṇoṣi · śastramlokān · prayāntu · ripavaḥ · api · hi · śastrapūtāḥittham · matiḥ · bhavati · teṣu · api · te · atisādhvī

Word by word

  1. दृष्ट्वाdṛṣṭvāby looking (lit. having looked)absolutive (ktvā) of √दृश् (bhvādi 1P ‘to see’)
  2. एवevamerely, justindeclinable (restrictive particle)
  3. किम्kimwhy?interrogative किम् used as an indeclinable adverb
  4. नnanotindeclinable (negative particle)
  5. भवतीbhavatīyou (Your Ladyship)f. nom. sg. of the honorific pronoun भवत्; takes a third-person verb
  6. प्रकरोतिprakarotimake (into)प्र + √कृ (tanādi 8U ‘to do, make’), laṭ (present), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada; agrees with the honorific भवती
  7. भस्मbhasmaashesn. acc. sg. of भस्मन्; predicative accusative with प्रकरोति (‘make … ash’)
  8. सर्वासुरान्sarvāsurānall the asurasm. acc. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); object of प्रकरोति
  9. अरिषुariṣuat the enemiesm. loc. pl. of अरि (i-stem); locative of the target
  10. यत्yatthat (instead)indeclinable conjunction (यद् ‘that’); its clause states what the question is about
  11. प्रहिणोषिprahiṇoṣiyou hurl, you send forthप्र + √हि (svādi 5P ‘to impel, send’), laṭ (present), madhyama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  12. शस्त्रम्śastramweaponn. acc. sg. of शस्त्र; object of प्रहिणोषि
  13. लोकान्lokānto the (higher) worldsm. acc. pl. of लोक; accusative of the goal
  14. प्रयान्तुprayāntulet them goप्र + √या (adādi 2P ‘to go’), loṭ (imperative), prathama-puruṣa bahuvacana, parasmaipada
  15. रिपवःripavaḥthe enemiesm. nom. pl. of रिपु (u-stem); subject of प्रयान्तु
  16. अपिapievenindeclinable (particle of inclusion)
  17. हिhifor (this is the reason)indeclinable (causal particle)
  18. शस्त्रपूताःśastrapūtāḥpurified by the weaponm. nom. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies रिपवः
  19. इत्थम्itthamthus, in this wayindeclinable (adverb of manner)
  20. मतिःmatiḥthought, intentionf. nom. sg. of मति; subject of भवति
  21. भवतिbhavatiis√भू (bhvādi 1P), laṭ (present), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  22. तेषुteṣutowards thempronoun तद्, m. loc. pl.; locative of the object of feeling
  23. अपिapievenindeclinable (particle of inclusion)
  24. तेteyourenclitic form of युष्मद्, gen. sg.
  25. अतिसाध्वीatisādhvīexceedingly kind (good)f. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); predicate of मतिः

Translation

Why do you not make all the asuras ash merely by looking, that you hurl a weapon at the enemies? ‘Let even the enemies, purified by the weapon, go to the worlds’ — for thus is your thought, exceedingly kind even towards them.

In readable EnglishWhy do you not burn all the asuras to ash with a single glance, but hurl your weapons at them instead? Because you would have even your enemies, made pure by your weapons, reach the higher worlds. So kind is your intent, even towards them.

‘Because’ renders हि; ‘the higher worlds’ interprets लोकान् ‘the worlds’, as 4.18 (दिवं प्रयान्तु) shows.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • दृष्ट्वैव dṛṣṭvaiva → दृष्ट्वा + एव

    vowel sandhi (vṛddhi) · वृद्धिरेचि (6.1.88)

    ā + e → ai.

  • सर्वासुरान sarvāsurāna → सर्व + असुरान्

    vowel sandhi inside a compound (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    a + a → ā. The final n then joins the vowel of अरिषु unchanged; it is not doubled, because the vowel before it is long.

  • रिपवोऽपि ripavo'pi → रिपवः + अपि

    visarga sandhi with elision of a · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), अतो रोरप्लुतादप्लुते (6.1.113), आद्गुणः (6.1.87), एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109)

    -aḥ before a becomes -o, and the following a is elided (avagraha).

  • शस्त्रपूता इत्थं śastrapūtā itthaṃ → शस्त्रपूताः + इत्थम्

    visarga sandhi across the line break · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), भोभगोअघोअपूर्वस्य योऽशि (8.3.17), लोपः शाकल्यस्य (8.3.19)

    -āḥ before a vowel: the visarga becomes y and the y is dropped; the vowels stand apart. The sandhi is applied across the end of the third line.

  • मतिर्भवति matirbhavati → मतिः + भवति

    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)

    After i the visarga (as ru) stands as r before the voiced bh.

  • तेष्वपि तेऽतिसाध्वी teṣvapi te'tisādhvī → तेषु + अपि + ते + अतिसाध्वी

    vowel sandhi (yaṇ; pūrvarūpa) · इको यणचि (6.1.77); एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109)

    u before a vowel becomes v; -e + a- → -e’- with avagraha.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • सर्वासुरान् sarvāsurān

    karmadhāraya

    Members
    सर्व + असुर
    Vigraha
    सर्वे च ते असुराः च, तान् sarve ca te asurāḥ ca, tān
    Literally
    “all-asuras”
    In the verse
    the whole host of the asuras

  • शस्त्रपूताः śastrapūtāḥ

    tṛtīyā tatpuruṣa

    Members
    शस्त्र + पूत
    Vigraha
    शस्त्रेण पूताः śastreṇa pūtāḥ
    Literally
    “purified by the weapon”
    In the verse
    cleansed of their sin by the stroke of her weapon

  • अतिसाध्वी atisādhvī

    tatpuruṣa of the prādi kind (the prefix अति ‘exceedingly’ with an adjective)

    Members
    अति + साध्वी
    Vigraha
    अतिशयेन साध्वी atiśayena sādhvī
    Literally
    “exceedingly good”
    In the verse
    supremely kind
Morphology & anvaya

  • प्रहिणोषि prahiṇoṣi

    Present of प्र + √हि (svādi class, stem हिनो-): ‘you send forth’. After the r of the prefix प्र, the n of the stem becomes ṇ (हिनोषि → प्रहिणोषि).

  • प्रकरोति prakaroti

    Third person with the honorific भवती. The next clause turns to the direct second person (प्रहिणोषि): both address the Goddess.

  • भस्म bhasma

    Neuter of भस्मन् ‘ash’. With √कृ it is a predicative accusative: ‘make (them) ash’.

Anvaya · prose order

(हे देवि) भवती दृष्ट्वा एव सर्वासुरान् भस्म किं न प्रकरोति, यत् अरिषु शस्त्रं प्रहिणोषि? ‘रिपवः अपि शस्त्रपूताः (सन्तः) लोकान् प्रयान्तु’ हि — इत्थं तेषु अपि ते मतिः अतिसाध्वी भवति।

(he devi) bhavatī dṛṣṭvā eva sarvāsurān bhasma kiṃ na prakaroti, yat ariṣu śastraṃ prahiṇoṣi? ‘ripavaḥ api śastrapūtāḥ (santaḥ) lokān prayāntu’ hi — itthaṃ teṣu api te matiḥ atisādhvī bhavati |

A question (किं न … प्रकरोति), whose occasion is the यत्-clause (‘that you hurl a weapon’). Line 3 gives the Goddess’s own wish as the answer; line 4 is the gods’ verdict on it.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The gods answer their own question. She could destroy the asuras by sight alone, but she uses weapons so that their touch may purify her enemies and send them to the higher worlds. Even her choice of weapon is an act of kindness.

Traditional interpretation

The verse carries 4.18 further: death at the Goddess’s hand is read as purification (पूत), not only punishment. The tradition draws from it that her anger and her grace are one act seen from two sides.

Verse 4.20

Śloka · vasantatilakā
‹›

खड्गप्रभानिकरविस्फुरणैस्तथोग्रैःशूलाग्रकान्तिनिवहेन दृशोऽसुराणाम्।यन्नागता विलयमंशुमदिन्दुखण्ड-योग्याननं तव विलोकयतां तदेतत्॥⁠२०⁠॥

khaḍgaprabhānikaravisphuraṇaistathograiḥśūlāgrakāntinivahena dṛśo'surāṇām |yannāgatā vilayamaṃśumadindukhaṇḍa-yogyānanaṃ tava vilokayatāṃ tadetat ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

खड्गप्रभानिकरविस्फुरणैः · तथा · उग्रैःशूलाग्रकान्तिनिवहेन · दृशः · असुराणाम्यत् · न · आगताः · विलयम् · अंशुमदिन्दुखण्डयोग्याननम्तव · विलोकयताम् · तत् · एतत्

khaḍgaprabhānikaravisphuraṇaiḥ · tathā · ugraiḥśūlāgrakāntinivahena · dṛśaḥ · asurāṇāmyat · na · āgatāḥ · vilayam · aṃśumadindukhaṇḍayogyānanamtava · vilokayatām · tat · etat

Word by word

  1. खड्गप्रभानिकरविस्फुरणैःkhaḍgaprabhānikaravisphuraṇaiḥby the flashings of the mass of light from (your) swordn. instr. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); instrumental of cause
  2. तथाtathāand likewiseindeclinable (connective)
  3. उग्रैःugraiḥfiercen. instr. pl. of उग्र; qualifies …विस्फुरणैः
  4. शूलाग्रकान्तिनिवहेनśūlāgrakāntinivahenaby the flood of brilliance from the trident’s pointm. instr. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); instrumental of cause
  5. दृशःdṛśaḥthe eyesf. nom. pl. of the root-noun दृश् ‘sight, eye’; subject of आगताः
  6. असुराणाम्asurāṇāmof the asurasm. gen. pl. of असुर
  7. यत्yatthat (the fact that)indeclinable conjunction; its clause is resumed by तत्
  8. नnanotindeclinable (negative particle)
  9. आगताःāgatāḥcame (to), went (to)past participle (kta) of आ + √गम् (bhvādi 1P ‘to go’), active in sense, f. nom. pl.; agrees with दृशः
  10. विलयम्vilayamdestruction, dissolutionm. acc. sg. of विलय; accusative of the goal with आगताः
  11. अंशुमदिन्दुखण्डयोग्याननम्aṃśumadindukhaṇḍayogyānanamface fit to be likened to the radiant crescent of the moonn. acc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); object of विलोकयताम्
  12. तवtavayourयुष्मद्, gen. sg.; with …आननम्
  13. विलोकयताम्vilokayatām(of them) gazing atpresent participle (śatṛ) of वि + √लोक् (curādi 10, stem लोकय- ‘to look’), m. gen. pl.; agrees with असुराणाम्
  14. तत्tatthatpronoun तद्, n. nom. sg.; resumes the यत्-clause
  15. एतत्etat(is) thispronoun एतद्, n. nom. sg.; points to the reason just given, their gazing at your face

Translation

That, by the fierce flashings of the mass of the sword’s light and likewise by the stream of lustre from the trident’s point, the eyes of the asuras did not go to destruction, (they) gazing at your face fit for the radiant crescent of the moon — that is this.

In readable EnglishThe blazing flashes of your sword and the flood of light from the point of your trident did not destroy the asuras’ eyes — because they were gazing at your face, like the radiant crescent of the moon.

तत् एतत् (‘that is this’) points to the participle विलोकयताम् as the reason; English makes it ‘because’.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • विस्फुरणैस्तथोग्रैः visphuraṇaistathograiḥ → …विस्फुरणैः + तथा + उग्रैः

    visarga sandhi; vowel sandhi (guṇa) · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34); आद्गुणः (6.1.87)

    Visarga before t becomes s; ā + u → o.

  • दृशोऽसुराणाम् dṛśo'surāṇām → दृशः + असुराणाम्

    visarga sandhi with elision of a · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), अतो रोरप्लुतादप्लुते (6.1.113), आद्गुणः (6.1.87), एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109)

    -aḥ before a becomes -o, and the following a is elided (avagraha).

  • यन्नागता yannāgatā → यत् + न + आगताः

    consonant sandhi (nasal assimilation); vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · यरोऽनुनासिकेऽनुनासिको वा (8.4.45); अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    t before n becomes n; a + ā → ā.

  • आगता विलयम् āgatā vilayam → आगताः + विलयम्

    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), भोभगोअघोअपूर्वस्य योऽशि (8.3.17)

    -āḥ before a voiced consonant: the visarga becomes y, which is dropped before the consonant.

  • अंशुमदिन्दु aṃśumadindu → अंशुमत् + इन्दु

    consonant sandhi inside a compound (voicing) · झलां जशोऽन्ते (8.2.39)

    The t of अंशुमत् becomes d before a vowel. The compound runs across the line break (…खण्ड- / योग्याननं) and belongs to the third line, where it begins.

  • तदेतत् tadetat → तत् + एतत्

    consonant sandhi (voicing) · झलां जशोऽन्ते (8.2.39)

    Final t becomes d before a vowel.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • खड्गप्रभानिकरविस्फुरणैः khaḍgaprabhānikaravisphuraṇaiḥ

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa, nested three times

    Members
    खड्ग + प्रभा + निकर + विस्फुरण
    Vigraha
    खड्गस्य प्रभा खड्गप्रभा; तस्याः निकरः खड्गप्रभानिकरः; तस्य विस्फुरणानि, तैः khaḍgasya prabhā khaḍgaprabhā; tasyāḥ nikaraḥ khaḍgaprabhānikaraḥ; tasya visphuraṇāni, taiḥ
    Literally
    “by the flashings of the mass of the sword’s light”
    In the verse
    by the blinding flashes of her sword

  • शूलाग्रकान्तिनिवहेन śūlāgrakāntinivahena

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa, nested three times

    Members
    शूल + अग्र + कान्ति + निवह
    Vigraha
    शूलस्य अग्रम् शूलाग्रम्; तस्य कान्तिः शूलाग्रकान्तिः; तस्याः निवहः, तेन śūlasya agram śūlāgram; tasya kāntiḥ śūlāgrakāntiḥ; tasyāḥ nivahaḥ, tena
    Literally
    “by the stream of lustre of the trident’s tip”
    In the verse
    by the glare from the point of her trident

  • अंशुमदिन्दुखण्डयोग्याननम् aṃśumadindukhaṇḍayogyānanam

    karmadhāraya; its first member is a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (…योग्य) built on a karmadhāraya (अंशुमदिन्दुखण्ड) that contains a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (इन्दुखण्ड)

    Members
    अंशुमत् + इन्दु + खण्ड + योग्य + आनन
    Vigraha
    इन्दोः खण्डम् इन्दुखण्डम्; अंशुमत् इन्दुखण्डम् अंशुमदिन्दुखण्डम्; तस्य योग्यम् अंशुमदिन्दुखण्डयोग्यम्; तत् च तत् आननम् indoḥ khaṇḍam indukhaṇḍam; aṃśumat indukhaṇḍam aṃśumadindukhaṇḍam; tasya yogyam aṃśumadindukhaṇḍayogyam; tat ca tat ānanam
    Literally
    “the face fit for a ray-bearing piece of the moon”
    In the verse
    your face, like the radiant crescent moon
    Alternative
    अंशुमत् ‘ray-bearing’ is also a name of the sun; as a dvandva (अंशुमान् च इन्दुखण्डं च) the face would be ‘worthy of the sun and the crescent’. The verse needs an image that spares the eyes after the glare of the weapons, which points to the moon; so अंशुमत् is taken as its qualifier.
Morphology & anvaya

  • विलोकयताम् vilokayatām

    Present participle of the curādi stem लोकय- with वि, gen. pl.: ‘of (them) gazing at’. It agrees with असुराणाम्; it is not an imperative, which would not fit the sentence.

  • आगताः āgatāḥ

    Past participle of a verb of motion, active in sense: ‘(they) went (to)’. Feminine plural, to agree with दृशः.

  • दृशः dṛśaḥ

    Nominative plural of the root-noun दृश् (feminine), ‘eye’, ‘sight’.

Anvaya · prose order

खड्गप्रभानिकरविस्फुरणैः उग्रैः तथा शूलाग्रकान्तिनिवहेन तव अंशुमदिन्दुखण्डयोग्याननं विलोकयताम् असुराणां दृशः यत् विलयं न आगताः, तत् एतत् (तव आननस्य दर्शनस्य फलम्)।

khaḍgaprabhānikaravisphuraṇaiḥ ugraiḥ tathā śūlāgrakāntinivahena tava aṃśumadindukhaṇḍayogyānanaṃ vilokayatām asurāṇāṃ dṛśaḥ yat vilayaṃ na āgatāḥ, tat etat (tava ānanasya darśanasya phalam) |

The यत्-clause carries the fact: the asuras’ eyes were not destroyed. तत् एतत् ‘that is this’ points to the participle phrase as its reason: they were gazing at your face.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

Another wonder of the battle: the glare of her weapons should have blinded the asuras, but their eyes were not destroyed. The gods give the reason: they were looking at her face, which is like the moon.

Traditional interpretation

The moon is the standing image of what cools and soothes; the verse sets it against the fierce light of sword and trident. The same face that terrifies in 4.13 here protects even her enemies, the theme of 4.18–21.

Comparative Śrīvidyā notes

Comparative Śākta / Śrīvidyā

Lalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) also likens her face to the moon: शरच्चन्द्रनिभानना (name 129), ‘whose face is like the autumn moon’. The image is shared devotional language; the works are separate.

Verse 4.21

Śloka · vasantatilakā
‹›

दुर्वृत्तवृत्तशमनं तव देवि शीलंरूपं तथैतदविचिन्त्यमतुल्यमन्यैः।वीर्यं च हन्तृ हृतदेवपराक्रमाणांवैरिष्वपि प्रकटितैव दया त्वयेत्थम्॥⁠२१⁠॥

durvṛttavṛttaśamanaṃ tava devi śīlaṃrūpaṃ tathaitadavicintyamatulyamanyaiḥ |vīryaṃ ca hantṛ hṛtadevaparākramāṇāṃvairiṣvapi prakaṭitaiva dayā tvayettham ||
