  21. इत्थम्itthamthus, in this wayindeclinable (adverb of manner)

Translation

O Devī, your nature is the quelling of the conduct of the wicked; likewise this form is inconceivable and unequalled by others; and (your) valour is the slayer of those who had robbed the gods of their prowess; thus compassion has indeed been shown by you even towards enemies.

In readable EnglishO Devī, it is your nature to put an end to the ways of the wicked; your form is beyond thought, and none can equal it; your valour destroys those who robbed the gods of their might — and in this way you have shown compassion even to your enemies.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • दुर्वृत्त durvṛtta → दुस् + वृत्त

    visarga (s) sandhi inside a word · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)

    The s of the prefix दुस् ‘ill’ becomes ru, which stands as r before the voiced v.

  • तथैतदविचिन्त्यमतुल्यमन्यैः tathaitadavicintyamatulyamanyaiḥ → तथा + एतत् + अविचिन्त्यम् + अतुल्यम् + अन्यैः

    vowel sandhi (vṛddhi); consonant sandhi (voicing) · वृद्धिरेचि (6.1.88); झलां जशोऽन्ते (8.2.39)

    ā + e → ai; the t of एतत् becomes d before a vowel; the final m of the next two words simply joins the following vowel.

  • वैरिष्वपि vairiṣvapi → वैरिषु + अपि

    vowel sandhi (yaṇ) · इको यणचि (6.1.77)

    u before a vowel becomes v.

  • प्रकटितैव prakaṭitaiva → प्रकटिता + एव

    vowel sandhi (vṛddhi) · वृद्धिरेचि (6.1.88)

    ā + e → ai.

  • त्वयेत्थम् tvayettham → त्वया + इत्थम्

    vowel sandhi (guṇa) · आद्गुणः (6.1.87)

    ā + i → e.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • दुर्वृत्तवृत्तशमनम् durvṛttavṛttaśamanam

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa containing a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (दुर्वृत्तवृत्त)

    Members
    दुर्वृत्त + वृत्त + शमन
    Vigraha
    दुर्वृत्तानां वृत्तम् दुर्वृत्तवृत्तम्; तस्य शमनम् durvṛttānāṃ vṛttam durvṛttavṛttam; tasya śamanam
    Literally
    “the quieting of the conduct of the ill-behaved”
    In the verse
    putting an end to the deeds of the wicked
    Alternative
    दुर्वृत्त is itself a bahuvrīhi, दुष्टं वृत्तं येषां ते, ‘those whose conduct is bad’.

  • अविचिन्त्यम् avicintyam

    nañ tatpuruṣa

    Members
    अ + विचिन्त्य
    Vigraha
    न विचिन्त्यम् na vicintyam
    Literally
    “not to be thought out”
    In the verse
    beyond the reach of thought

  • अतुल्यम् atulyam

    nañ tatpuruṣa

    Members
    अ + तुल्य
    Vigraha
    न तुल्यम् na tulyam
    Literally
    “not equal”
    In the verse
    without a match

  • हृतदेवपराक्रमाणाम् hṛtadevaparākramāṇām

    bahuvrīhi containing a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (देवपराक्रम)

    Members
    हृत + देव + पराक्रम
    Vigraha
    देवानां पराक्रमः देवपराक्रमः; हृतः देवपराक्रमः यैः तेषाम् devānāṃ parākramaḥ devaparākramaḥ; hṛtaḥ devaparākramaḥ yaiḥ teṣām
    Literally
    “of those by whom the gods’ valour was taken”
    In the verse
    of the asuras who had stripped the gods of their power
Morphology & anvaya

  • हन्तृ hantṛ

    Agent noun of √हन् (suffix tṛc), ‘slayer’, here neuter to agree with वीर्यम्. Like other agent nouns it takes its object in the genitive (हृतदेवपराक्रमाणाम्).

  • प्रकटिता prakaṭitā

    Past participle of the denominative प्रकटय- ‘make evident’ (from the adjective प्रकट), feminine to agree with दया; त्वया is its agent.

  • अविचिन्त्यम् avicintyam

    The negative prefix with the gerundive विचिन्त्य (वि + √चिन्त्, curādi, + -य): ‘not to be conceived’.

Anvaya · prose order

(हे) देवि, तव शीलं दुर्वृत्तवृत्तशमनम्; तथा एतत् रूपम् अविचिन्त्यम् अन्यैः अतुल्यं (च); वीर्यं च हृतदेवपराक्रमाणां हन्तृ; इत्थं त्वया वैरिषु अपि दया प्रकटिता एव।

(he) devi, tava śīlaṃ durvṛttavṛttaśamanam; tathā etat rūpam avicintyam anyaiḥ atulyaṃ (ca); vīryaṃ ca hṛtadevaparākramāṇāṃ hantṛ; itthaṃ tvayā vairiṣu api dayā prakaṭitā eva |

Three nominal sentences (on शील, रूप and वीर्य), then a passive verdict (दया … प्रकटिता) with त्वया as agent; इत्थम् sums up what 4.18–20 described.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The gods sum up the Goddess under three heads, her nature (शील), her form (रूप) and her valour (वीर्य), and draw the conclusion of 4.18–20: in this very way, by slaying them in battle, she has shown compassion even to her enemies.

Traditional interpretation

The three heads echo the gods’ earlier question in 4.6 (रूपमचिन्त्यमेतत्, अतिवीर्यम्, चरितानि). The point traditionally drawn is that her severity is itself mercy: the act that protects the gods also purifies the asuras.

Comparative Śrīvidyā notes

Comparative Śākta / Śrīvidyā

Lalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) calls the Goddess अव्याजकरुणामूर्तिः (name 992), ‘compassion embodied, without a pretext’. The Devī Māhātmya makes a related point by example: compassion shown to those who did nothing to earn it.

Verse 4.22

Śloka · vasantatilakā
‹›

केनोपमा भवतु तेऽस्य पराक्रमस्यरूपं च शत्रुभयकार्यतिहारि कुत्र।चित्ते कृपा समरनिष्ठुरता च दृष्टात्वय्येव देवि वरदे भुवनत्रयेऽपि॥⁠२२⁠॥

kenopamā bhavatu te'sya parākramasyarūpaṃ ca śatrubhayakāryatihāri kutra |citte kṛpā samaraniṣṭhuratā ca dṛṣṭātvayyeva devi varade bhuvanatraye'pi ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

केन · उपमा · भवतु · ते · अस्य · पराक्रमस्यरूपम् · च · शत्रुभयकारि · अतिहारि · कुत्रचित्ते · कृपा · समरनिष्ठुरता · च · दृष्टात्वयि · एव · देवि · वरदे · भुवनत्रये · अपि

kena · upamā · bhavatu · te · asya · parākramasyarūpam · ca · śatrubhayakāri · atihāri · kutracitte · kṛpā · samaraniṣṭhuratā · ca · dṛṣṭātvayi · eva · devi · varade · bhuvanatraye · api

Word by word

  1. केनkenawith what?interrogative pronoun किम्, n. instr. sg.; instrumental with उपमा (‘comparison with’)
  2. उपमाupamācomparison, likenessf. nom. sg. of उपमा; subject of भवतु
  3. भवतुbhavatucould there be (lit. let there be)√भू (bhvādi 1P), loṭ (imperative), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada; deliberative in a question
  4. तेteyourenclitic form of युष्मद्, gen. sg.
  5. अस्यasyathispronoun इदम्, m. gen. sg.; qualifies पराक्रमस्य
  6. पराक्रमस्यparākramasyaof the valour, of the prowessm. gen. sg. of पराक्रम; the thing to be compared
  7. रूपम्rūpama formn. nom. sg. of रूप; subject of the nominal question
  8. चcaandindeclinable (conjunctive particle)
  9. शत्रुभयकारिśatrubhayakāristriking fear into enemiesn. nom. sg. of शत्रुभयकारिन्; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies रूपम्
  10. अतिहारिatihāriexceedingly captivatingn. nom. sg. of अतिहारिन्; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies रूपम्
  11. कुत्रkutrawhere?indeclinable (interrogative adverb of place)
  12. चित्तेcittein the heartn. loc. sg. of चित्त
  13. कृपाkṛpācompassionf. nom. sg. of कृपा; subject
  14. समरनिष्ठुरताsamaraniṣṭhuratārelentlessness in battlef. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); subject
  15. चcaandindeclinable (conjunctive particle)
  16. दृष्टाdṛṣṭā(are) seenpast passive participle (kta) of √दृश्, f. nom. sg.; agrees with the nearer subject
  17. त्वयिtvayiin youयुष्मद्, loc. sg.
  18. एवevaonlyindeclinable (restrictive particle)
  19. देविdeviO Devīf. voc. sg. of देवी
  20. वरदेvaradeO giver of boonsf. voc. sg. of वरदा; compound (see Samāsa)
  21. भुवनत्रयेbhuvanatrayein the three worldsn. loc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa)
  22. अपिapieven (throughout)indeclinable (particle of inclusion)

Translation

With what can there be a comparison of this valour of yours? And where (is) a form fear-causing to enemies (and) exceedingly captivating? Compassion in the heart and relentlessness in battle are seen, O Devī, giver of boons, in you alone, even in the three worlds.

In readable EnglishTo what could this valour of yours be compared? Where else is a form that strikes terror into enemies and yet captivates the heart? Compassion within and relentlessness in battle — in all three worlds, O Devī, giver of boons, these are seen together in you alone.

‘The heart’ after ‘captivates’ is supplied: अतिहारि means ‘exceedingly taking (the mind)’. ‘Together’ makes explicit what एव implies: the pair is found nowhere else.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • केनोपमा kenopamā → केन + उपमा

    vowel sandhi (guṇa) · आद्गुणः (6.1.87)

    a + u → o.

  • तेऽस्य te'sya → ते + अस्य

    vowel sandhi (pūrvarūpa) · एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109)

    -e + a- → -e’-: the initial a is elided (avagraha).

  • शत्रुभयकार्यतिहारि śatrubhayakāryatihāri → शत्रुभयकारि + अतिहारि

    vowel sandhi (yaṇ) · इको यणचि (6.1.77)

    Final i before a vowel becomes y. Two adjectives, written as one string: शत्रुभयकारि and अतिहारि.

  • त्वय्येव tvayyeva → त्वयि + एव

    vowel sandhi (yaṇ) · इको यणचि (6.1.77)

    i before e becomes y; the y after y is written as a doubled य्य.

  • भुवनत्रयेऽपि bhuvanatraye'pi → भुवनत्रये + अपि

    vowel sandhi (pūrvarūpa) · एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109)

    -e + a- → -e’- with avagraha.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • शत्रुभयकारि śatrubhayakāri

    upapada tatpuruṣa containing a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (शत्रुभय)

    Members
    शत्रु + भय + कारिन्
    Vigraha
    शत्रूणां भयम् शत्रुभयम्; तत् करोति इति śatrūṇāṃ bhayam śatrubhayam; tat karoti iti
    Literally
    “making the fear of enemies”
    In the verse
    terrifying to foes

  • अतिहारि atihāri

    tatpuruṣa of the prādi kind (अति with the agent adjective हारिन्)

    Members
    अति + हारिन्
    Vigraha
    अतिशयेन हरति (मनः) इति atiśayena harati (manaḥ) iti
    Literally
    “exceedingly taking (the mind)”
    In the verse
    ravishingly beautiful

  • समरनिष्ठुरता samaraniṣṭhuratā

    saptamī tatpuruṣa

    Members
    समर + निष्ठुरता
    Vigraha
    समरे निष्ठुरता samare niṣṭhuratā
    Literally
    “hardness in battle”
    In the verse
    unyielding severity in war

  • वरदे varade

    upapada tatpuruṣa

    Members
    वर + दा
    Vigraha
    वरं ददाति इति वरदा, तत्सम्बुद्धौ varaṃ dadāti iti varadā, tatsambuddhau
    Literally
    “O boon-giving one”
    In the verse
    O giver of boons

  • भुवनत्रये bhuvanatraye

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa

    Members
    भुवन + त्रय
    Vigraha
    भुवनानां त्रयम्, तस्मिन् bhuvanānāṃ trayam, tasmin
    Literally
    “in the triad of worlds”
    In the verse
    anywhere in the three worlds
Morphology & anvaya

  • भवतु bhavatu

    Imperative used in a deliberative question: ‘with what may there be a comparison?’, i.e. ‘what could it be compared with?’. The expected answer is ‘nothing’.

  • शत्रुभयकारि śatrubhayakāri

    Neuter nom. sg. of a stem in -इन् (कारिन्): the n drops and the i stays short. अतिहारि is formed the same way.

  • दृष्टा dṛṣṭā

    Two subjects (कृपा and समरनिष्ठुरता) with a singular participle: it agrees with the nearer one, as often in verse.

  • समरनिष्ठुरता samaraniṣṭhuratā

    निष्ठुर ‘harsh, relentless’ + the abstract suffix -ता (tal), feminine: ‘harshness’.

Anvaya · prose order

(हे) वरदे देवि, ते अस्य पराक्रमस्य उपमा केन भवतु? शत्रुभयकारि अतिहारि च रूपं कुत्र (अस्ति)? चित्ते कृपा समरनिष्ठुरता च भुवनत्रये अपि त्वयि एव दृष्टा।

(he) varade devi, te asya parākramasya upamā kena bhavatu? śatrubhayakāri atihāri ca rūpaṃ kutra (asti)? citte kṛpā samaraniṣṭhuratā ca bhuvanatraye api tvayi eva dṛṣṭā |

Two rhetorical questions (केन … भवतु; रूपं … कुत्र) and a statement with a passive participle (दृष्टा) whose place is त्वयि एव.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

Nothing compares with her valour; no other form is at once terrible to enemies and supremely beautiful; nowhere in the three worlds are tenderness of heart and hardness in battle found together, except in her.

Traditional interpretation

The verse names the paradox the hymn has been describing since 4.13: the gentle and the terrible, mercy and severity, held in one person. In the Śākta tradition this union of opposites is a mark of the supreme Goddess.

Verse 4.23

Śloka · vasantatilakā
‹›

त्रैलोक्यमेतदखिलं रिपुनाशनेनत्रातं त्वया समरमूर्धनि तेऽपि हत्वा।नीता दिवं रिपुगणा भयमप्यपास्त-मस्माकमुन्मदसुरारिभवं नमस्ते॥⁠२३⁠॥

trailokyametadakhilaṃ ripunāśanenatrātaṃ tvayā samaramūrdhani te'pi hatvā |nītā divaṃ ripugaṇā bhayamapyapāsta-masmākamunmadasurāribhavaṃ namaste ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

त्रैलोक्यम् · एतत् · अखिलम् · रिपुनाशनेनत्रातम् · त्वया · समरमूर्धनि · ते · अपि · हत्वानीताः · दिवम् · रिपुगणाः · भयम् · अपि · अपास्तम्अस्माकम् · उन्मदसुरारिभवम् · नमः · ते

trailokyam · etat · akhilam · ripunāśanenatrātam · tvayā · samaramūrdhani · te · api · hatvānītāḥ · divam · ripugaṇāḥ · bhayam · api · apāstamasmākam · unmadasurāribhavam · namaḥ · te

Word by word

  1. त्रैलोक्यम्trailokyamthe three worldsn. nom. sg. of त्रैलोक्य (taddhita: ‘the triad of worlds’); subject of त्रातम्
  2. एतत्etatthispronoun एतद्, n. nom. sg.; qualifies त्रैलोक्यम्
  3. अखिलम्akhilamentiren. nom. sg. of अखिल; qualifies त्रैलोक्यम्
  4. रिपुनाशनेनripunāśanenaby the destruction of the enemiesn. instr. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); instrumental of means
  5. त्रातम्trātam(has been) saved, protectedpast passive participle (kta) of √त्रै (bhvādi 1Ā ‘to protect’), n. nom. sg.; predicate
  6. त्वयाtvayāby youयुष्मद्, instr. sg.; agent of त्रातम् (and of नीताः)
  7. समरमूर्धनिsamaramūrdhaniat the forefront of battlem. loc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa)
  8. तेtethey (the enemies)pronoun तद्, m. nom. pl.; anticipates रिपुगणाः (it could also be read as the enclitic युष्मद्, ‘your’, but अपि ‘they too’ favours the pronoun)
  9. अपिapitoo, evenindeclinable (particle of inclusion)
  10. हत्वाhatvāhaving slain (them)absolutive (ktvā) of √हन् (adādi 2P ‘to strike, kill’); its agent is the agent of नीताः
  11. नीताःnītāḥhave been ledpast passive participle (kta) of √नी (bhvādi 1U ‘to lead’), m. nom. pl.; predicate of रिपुगणाः
  12. दिवम्divamto heavenf. acc. sg. of दिव्; accusative of the goal
  13. रिपुगणाःripugaṇāḥthe hosts of enemiesm. nom. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); subject of नीताः
  14. भयम्bhayamfearn. nom. sg. of भय; subject of अपास्तम्
  15. अपिapialsoindeclinable (particle of inclusion)
  16. अपास्तम्apāstam(has been) cast awaypast passive participle (kta) of अप + √अस् (divādi 4P ‘to throw’), n. nom. sg.; predicate of भयम्
  17. अस्माकम्asmākamourअस्मद्, gen. pl.
  18. उन्मदसुरारिभवम्unmadasurāribhavamarising from the frenzied enemies of the godsn. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies भयम्
  19. नमःnamaḥsalutation, homagen. nom. sg. of नमस्
  20. तेteto youenclitic form of युष्मद्, dat. sg.; dative with नमः

Translation

This entire triple world has been saved by you through the destruction of the enemies; having slain (them) at the head of battle, they too, the hosts of enemies, have been led to heaven; and our fear, arising from the frenzied enemies of the gods, has been cast away. Salutation to you!

In readable EnglishBy destroying the enemies you have saved these three worlds entire. Slaying them at the forefront of battle, you have led even those enemy hosts to heaven; and you have driven away our fear of the frenzied foes of the gods. Salutation to you!

The passives (त्रातम्, नीताः, अपास्तम्) are turned into active sentences with ‘you’ as subject.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • त्रैलोक्यमेतदखिलं trailokyametadakhilaṃ → त्रैलोक्यम् + एतत् + अखिलम्

    consonant sandhi (voicing) · झलां जशोऽन्ते (8.2.39)

    The t of एतत् becomes d before a vowel; the m of त्रैलोक्यम् simply joins the next vowel.

  • तेऽपि te'pi → ते + अपि

    vowel sandhi (pūrvarūpa) · एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109)

    -e + a- → -e’- with avagraha.

  • नीता दिवं nītā divaṃ → नीताः + दिवम्

    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), भोभगोअघोअपूर्वस्य योऽशि (8.3.17)

    -āḥ before a voiced consonant: the visarga becomes y, which is dropped before the consonant. रिपुगणा भयम् is the same.

  • भयमप्यपास्त bhayamapyapāsta → भयम् + अपि + अपास्तम्

    vowel sandhi (yaṇ) · इको यणचि (6.1.77)

    i before a vowel becomes y. अपास्तम् runs across the line break (…पास्त- / मस्माकम्) and belongs to the third line.

  • सुरारि surāri → सुर + अरि

    vowel sandhi inside a compound (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    a + a → ā.

  • नमस्ते namaste → नमः + ते

    visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34)

    Visarga before t becomes s.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • रिपुनाशनेन ripunāśanena

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa

    Members
    रिपु + नाशन
    Vigraha
    रिपूणां नाशनम्, तेन ripūṇāṃ nāśanam, tena
    Literally
    “by the destroying of enemies”
    In the verse
    by slaying the foes

  • समरमूर्धनि samaramūrdhani

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa

    Members
    समर + मूर्धन्
    Vigraha
    समरस्य मूर्धा, तस्मिन् samarasya mūrdhā, tasmin
    Literally
    “at the head of battle”
    In the verse
    in the forefront of the fight

  • रिपुगणाः ripugaṇāḥ

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa

    Members
    रिपु + गण
    Vigraha
    रिपूणां गणाः ripūṇāṃ gaṇāḥ
    Literally
    “hosts of enemies”
    In the verse
    the enemy armies

  • उन्मदसुरारिभवम् unmadasurāribhavam

    pañcamī tatpuruṣa (भव ‘arising’ as the final member) containing a karmadhāraya (उन्मदसुरारि) and a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (सुरारि)

    Members
    उन्मद + सुर + अरि + भव
    Vigraha
    सुराणाम् अरयः सुरारयः; उन्मदाः सुरारयः उन्मदसुरारयः; तेभ्यः भवम् surāṇām arayaḥ surārayaḥ; unmadāḥ surārayaḥ unmadasurārayaḥ; tebhyaḥ bhavam
    Literally
    “arising from the maddened foes of the gods”
    In the verse
    the terror the frenzied asuras had caused
    Alternative
    उन्मद is itself a bahuvrīhi: उद्गतः मदः येषां ते, ‘in whom frenzy has risen’.
Morphology & anvaya

  • त्रातम् trātam

    Past participle of √त्रै ‘protect’: the root’s ai becomes ā before the suffix (त्रा + त → त्रात).

  • हत्वा hatvā

    An absolutive in a passive sentence: its subject is the logical agent of the main verb नीताः, i.e. त्वया. ‘(You) having slain them, they were led (by you) to heaven.’

  • अपास्तम् apāstam

    Past participle of अप + √अस् ‘throw’: ‘thrown away’, dispelled.

  • ते te

    Two different words: in line 2, the pronoun तद् (m. nom. pl., ‘they’); in line 4, the enclitic of युष्मद् (dat. sg., ‘to you’).

Anvaya · prose order

त्वया रिपुनाशनेन एतत् अखिलं त्रैलोक्यं त्रातम्; समरमूर्धनि हत्वा ते रिपुगणाः अपि (त्वया) दिवं नीताः; अस्माकम् उन्मदसुरारिभवं भयम् अपि अपास्तम्। ते नमः।

tvayā ripunāśanena etat akhilaṃ trailokyaṃ trātam; samaramūrdhani hatvā te ripugaṇāḥ api (tvayā) divaṃ nītāḥ; asmākam unmadasurāribhavaṃ bhayam api apāstam | te namaḥ |

Three passive clauses with the one agent त्वया (त्रातम्, नीताः, अपास्तम्), then the salutation नमस्ते.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The hymn sums up what the Goddess has done: the three worlds saved, the enemies themselves sent to heaven, and the gods’ fear removed. It closes the long vasantatilakā praise with नमस्ते.

Traditional interpretation

Three beneficiaries are named in order, the world, the slain enemies and the gods, so the verse gathers the themes of 4.18–22. After the salutation the gods turn from praise to petition: the anuṣṭubh verses that follow (4.24–27) ask for protection.

Verse 4.24

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

शूलेन पाहि नो देवि पाहि खड्गेन चाम्बिके।घण्टास्वनेन नः पाहि चापज्यानिःस्वनेन च॥⁠२४⁠॥

śūlena pāhi no devi pāhi khaḍgena cāmbike |ghaṇṭāsvanena naḥ pāhi cāpajyāniḥsvanena ca ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

शूलेन · पाहि · नः · देवि · पाहि · खड्गेन · च · अम्बिकेघण्टास्वनेन · नः · पाहि · चापज्यानिःस्वनेन · च

śūlena · pāhi · naḥ · devi · pāhi · khaḍgena · ca · ambikeghaṇṭāsvanena · naḥ · pāhi · cāpajyāniḥsvanena · ca

Word by word

  1. शूलेनśūlenawith (your) tridentn. (also m.) instr. sg. of शूल; instrumental of means
  2. पाहिpāhiprotect√पा (adādi 2P ‘to protect’), loṭ (imperative), madhyama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  3. नःnaḥusenclitic form of अस्मद्, acc. pl.; object of पाहि
  4. देविdeviO Devīf. voc. sg. of देवी
  5. पाहिpāhiprotect√पा, loṭ, madhyama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  6. खड्गेनkhaḍgenawith (your) swordm. instr. sg. of खड्ग; instrumental of means
  7. चcaand, alsoindeclinable (conjunctive particle)
  8. अम्बिकेambikeO Ambikā, O Motherf. voc. sg. of अम्बिका
  9. घण्टास्वनेनghaṇṭāsvanenawith the ringing of (your) bellm. instr. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); instrumental of means
  10. नःnaḥusenclitic form of अस्मद्, acc. pl.
  11. पाहिpāhiprotect√पा, loṭ, madhyama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  12. चापज्यानिःस्वनेनcāpajyāniḥsvanenawith the twang of (your) bowstringm. instr. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); instrumental of means
  13. चcaandindeclinable (conjunctive particle)

Translation

With the trident protect us, O Devī; protect (us) with the sword too, O Ambikā; with the sound of the bell protect us, and with the twang of the bowstring.

In readable EnglishProtect us with your trident, O Devī; protect us with your sword, O Mother; protect us with the ringing of your bell and the twang of your bowstring.

‘Your’ is supplied throughout: these are the weapons the gods gave her in 2.20–24.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • पाहि नो देवि pāhi no devi → पाहि + नः + देवि

    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)

    -aḥ before the voiced d becomes -o.

  • चाम्बिके cāmbike → च + अम्बिके

    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    a + a → ā.

  • नः पाहि naḥ pāhi → नः + पाहि

    visarga sandhi (visarga kept) · खरवसानयोर्विसर्जनीयः (8.3.15)

    Before the unvoiced p the visarga stays. Compare नो देवि in the first line, where the next sound is voiced.

  • चापज्यानिःस्वनेन cāpajyāniḥsvanena → चाप + ज्या + निस् + स्वन

    visarga (s) sandhi inside a compound · खरवसानयोर्विसर्जनीयः (8.3.15)

    The s of the prefix निस् becomes visarga before the sibilant s of स्वन.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • घण्टास्वनेन ghaṇṭāsvanena

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa

    Members
    घण्टा + स्वन
    Vigraha
    घण्टायाः स्वनः, तेन ghaṇṭāyāḥ svanaḥ, tena
    Literally
    “with the sound of the bell”
    In the verse
    with the ringing of the bell that stunned the asuras in battle (2.56)

  • चापज्यानिःस्वनेन cāpajyāniḥsvanena

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa containing a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (चापज्या)

    Members
    चाप + ज्या + निःस्वन
    Vigraha
    चापस्य ज्या चापज्या; तस्याः निःस्वनः, तेन cāpasya jyā cāpajyā; tasyāḥ niḥsvanaḥ, tena
    Literally
    “with the out-sound of the bow’s string”
    In the verse
    with the twang of her bowstring
    Alternative
    निःस्वन is itself a prefixed noun, निस् + स्वन, ‘sound going out’, the resounding twang.
Morphology & anvaya

  • पाहि pāhi

    Imperative 2nd sg. of √पा ‘protect’ (adādi class): पा + हि. Said three times in the verse.

  • नः naḥ

    Enclitic acc. pl. of अस्मद्, used in place of अस्मान्. As an enclitic it never begins a sentence; here it follows पाहि and घण्टास्वनेन.

Anvaya · prose order

(हे) देवि, शूलेन नः पाहि; (हे) अम्बिके, खड्गेन च (नः) पाहि; घण्टास्वनेन चापज्यानिःस्वनेन च नः पाहि।

(he) devi, śūlena naḥ pāhi; (he) ambike, khaḍgena ca (naḥ) pāhi; ghaṇṭāsvanena cāpajyāniḥsvanena ca naḥ pāhi |

Three imperatives (पाहि) with one object (नः) and four instruments: trident, sword, the bell’s sound, the bowstring’s twang.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The hymn changes metre and mood: from the long vasantatilakā praises the gods turn to a short anuṣṭubh prayer for protection. Four of her attributes are invoked: trident, sword, bell and bow, each a god’s gift in 2.20–24 (Śiva’s trident, Vāyu’s bow, Indra’s bell from Airāvata, Kāla’s sword).

Traditional interpretation

Even the sounds of her weapons protect: the bell, whose ringing stunned the asuras (घण्टास्वनविमोहितान्, 2.56), and the bowstring, whose twang shook the worlds (धनुर्ज्यानिःस्वनेन, 2.38). The four verses 4.24–27 are often recited on their own as a prayer for protection.

Verse 4.25

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

प्राच्यां रक्ष प्रतीच्यां च चण्डिके रक्ष दक्षिणे।भ्रामणेनात्मशूलस्य उत्तरस्यां तथेश्वरि॥⁠२५⁠॥

prācyāṃ rakṣa pratīcyāṃ ca caṇḍike rakṣa dakṣiṇe |bhrāmaṇenātmaśūlasya uttarasyāṃ tatheśvari ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

प्राच्याम् · रक्ष · प्रतीच्याम् · च · चण्डिके · रक्ष · दक्षिणेभ्रामणेन · आत्मशूलस्य · उत्तरस्याम् · तथा · ईश्वरि

prācyām · rakṣa · pratīcyām · ca · caṇḍike · rakṣa · dakṣiṇebhrāmaṇena · ātmaśūlasya · uttarasyām · tathā · īśvari

Word by word

  1. प्राच्याम्prācyāmin the eastf. loc. sg. of प्राची (‘the eastern (quarter)’, दिशि understood)
  2. रक्षrakṣaprotect (us)√रक्ष् (bhvādi 1P ‘to guard’), loṭ (imperative), madhyama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  3. प्रतीच्याम्pratīcyāmin the westf. loc. sg. of प्रतीची (‘the western (quarter)’)
  4. चcaandindeclinable (conjunctive particle)
  5. चण्डिकेcaṇḍikeO Caṇḍikāf. voc. sg. of चण्डिका
  6. रक्षrakṣaprotect (us)√रक्ष्, loṭ, madhyama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  7. दक्षिणेdakṣiṇein the southloc. sg. of दक्षिण (m./n., ‘the southern side’), with ‘quarter’ or ‘region’ understood
  8. भ्रामणेनbhrāmaṇenaby the whirlingn. instr. sg. of भ्रामण (action noun in -अन from the causative भ्रामय- of √भ्रम्, divādi/bhvādi ‘to wander, whirl’); instrumental of means
  9. आत्मशूलस्यātmaśūlasyaof your own tridentn. (also m.) gen. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); objective genitive with भ्रामणेन
  10. उत्तरस्याम्uttarasyāmin the northf. loc. sg. of the pronominal उत्तर (‘the northern (quarter)’)
  11. तथाtathālikewiseindeclinable (adverb of manner)
  12. ईश्वरिīśvariO Sovereign Ladyf. voc. sg. of ईश्वरी

Translation

In the east protect (us), and in the west; O Caṇḍikā, protect (us) in the south; by the whirling of your own trident, likewise in the north, O Īśvarī.

In readable EnglishProtect us in the east and in the west, O Caṇḍikā; protect us in the south; and in the north as well, O Sovereign Lady, by the whirling of your trident.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • प्राच्यां रक्ष prācyāṃ rakṣa → प्राच्याम् + रक्ष

    anusvāra sandhi · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)

    Final m before a consonant is written as anusvāra; likewise प्रतीच्यां च.

  • भ्रामणेनात्म bhrāmaṇenātma → भ्रामणेन + आत्मशूलस्य

    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    a + ā → ā.

  • आत्मशूलस्य उत्तरस्यां ātmaśūlasya uttarasyāṃ → आत्मशूलस्य + उत्तरस्याम्

    no sandhi (hiatus at the pāda break) · आद्गुणः (6.1.87) would give …स्योत्तरस्यां; it is not applied here (metrical licence)

    Declared in sandhi_exceptions: the third and fourth pādas are kept apart.

  • तथेश्वरि tatheśvari → तथा + ईश्वरि

    vowel sandhi (guṇa) · आद्गुणः (6.1.87)

    ā + ī → e.

Left unjoined

  • आत्मशूलस्य | उत्तरस्याम् — The vowels stand apart at the pāda break (भ्रामणेनात्मशूलस्य | उत्तरस्यां): epic and Purāṇic verse often leaves sandhi unapplied between the third and fourth pādas, and joining them (…शूलस्योत्तरस्यां) would take a syllable from the half-line, leaving seven where the metre needs eight. A metrical licence, printed so in the Gita Press text.
Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • आत्मशूलस्य ātmaśūlasya

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa

    Members
    आत्मन् + शूल
    Vigraha
    आत्मनः शूलम्, तस्य ātmanaḥ śūlam, tasya
    Literally
    “of the trident of oneself”
    In the verse
    of your own trident
Morphology & anvaya

  • दक्षिणे dakṣiṇe

    The other three quarters are feminine locatives (प्राच्याम्, प्रतीच्याम्, उत्तरस्याम्, with दिशि understood). The south is named with the m./n. locative of दक्षिण, ‘on the southern side’; the feminine would be दक्षिणस्याम्.

  • उत्तरस्याम् uttarasyām

    उत्तर in the sense of a direction is declined like a pronoun: feminine loc. sg. -स्याम् (as सर्वस्याम्).

  • भ्रामणेन bhrāmaṇena

    Action noun from the causative of √भ्रम्: ‘by causing to whirl’, the trident swung round about.

Anvaya · prose order

(हे) चण्डिके, प्राच्यां (नः) रक्ष, प्रतीच्यां च (रक्ष), दक्षिणे रक्ष; तथा (हे) ईश्वरि, आत्मशूलस्य भ्रामणेन उत्तरस्यां (रक्ष)।

(he) caṇḍike, prācyāṃ (naḥ) rakṣa, pratīcyāṃ ca (rakṣa), dakṣiṇe rakṣa; tathā (he) īśvari, ātmaśūlasya bhrāmaṇena uttarasyāṃ (rakṣa) |

A series of imperatives with ‘us’ understood from 4.24. The instrumental भ्रामणेनात्मशूलस्य can go with the north alone or, through तथा ‘likewise’, with all four quarters.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The prayer now covers space: the four quarters are named one by one, and her own trident, whirled about her, is the guard.

Traditional interpretation

Guarding the quarters is the usual plan of protective prayers (kavacas), in which each direction is given a guardian; the Devī Kavaca recited before the Saptaśatī in the Gita Press pāṭha follows that plan. Here one Goddess guards them all.

Comparative Śrīvidyā notes

Comparative Śākta / Śrīvidyā

Lalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) has the name चण्डिका (755), the name by which the Devī Māhātmya addresses her here and throughout. The shared name belongs to a shared Śākta vocabulary; the works are separate.

Verse 4.26

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

सौम्यानि यानि रूपाणि त्रैलोक्ये विचरन्ति ते।यानि चात्यर्थघोराणि तै रक्षास्मांस्तथा भुवम्॥⁠२६⁠॥

saumyāni yāni rūpāṇi trailokye vicaranti te |yāni cātyarthaghorāṇi tai rakṣāsmāṃstathā bhuvam ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

सौम्यानि · यानि · रूपाणि · त्रैलोक्ये · विचरन्ति · तेयानि · च · अत्यर्थघोराणि · तैः · रक्ष · अस्मान् · तथा · भुवम्

saumyāni · yāni · rūpāṇi · trailokye · vicaranti · teyāni · ca · atyarthaghorāṇi · taiḥ · rakṣa · asmān · tathā · bhuvam

Word by word

  1. सौम्यानिsaumyānigentle, benignn. nom. pl. of सौम्य (taddhita from सोम, ‘moon-like’, mild); qualifies रूपाणि
  2. यानिyāniwhichrelative pronoun यद्, n. nom. pl.
  3. रूपाणिrūpāṇiformsn. nom. pl. of रूप; subject of विचरन्ति
  4. त्रैलोक्येtrailokyein the three worldsn. loc. sg. of त्रैलोक्य
  5. विचरन्तिvicarantimove about, roamवि + √चर् (bhvādi 1P ‘to move’), laṭ (present), prathama-puruṣa bahuvacana, parasmaipada
  6. तेteyourenclitic form of युष्मद्, gen. sg.
  7. यानिyāniwhichrelative pronoun यद्, n. nom. pl.
  8. चcaandindeclinable (conjunctive particle)
  9. अत्यर्थघोराणिatyarthaghorāṇiterrible beyond measuren. nom. pl.; compound (see Samāsa)
  10. तैःtaiḥwith thempronoun तद्, n. instr. pl.; correlative of the two यानि
  11. रक्षrakṣaprotect√रक्ष् (bhvādi 1P), loṭ (imperative), madhyama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  12. अस्मान्asmānusअस्मद्, acc. pl.; object of रक्ष
  13. तथाtathāand alsoindeclinable (connective)
  14. भुवम्bhuvamthe earthf. acc. sg. of the root-noun भू; object of रक्ष

Translation

Your gentle forms which move about in the three worlds, and those which are terrible beyond measure — with them protect us and the earth as well.

In readable EnglishWith all your forms that roam the three worlds, the gentle ones and those terrible beyond measure, protect us, and the earth as well.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • चात्यर्थघोराणि cātyarthaghorāṇi → च + अत्यर्थघोराणि

    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha); yaṇ inside the compound · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101); इको यणचि (6.1.77)

    a + a → ā; inside the compound अति + अर्थ → अत्यर्थ (i before a vowel becomes y).

  • तै रक्षा tai rakṣā → तैः + रक्ष

    visarga sandhi (r before r) · रो रि (8.3.14), ढ्रलोपे पूर्वस्य दीर्घोऽणः (6.3.111)

    The visarga of तैः (as r) is dropped before the r of रक्ष. The rule lengthens the vowel before it, but ai is already long, so nothing more changes.

  • रक्षास्मांस्तथा rakṣāsmāṃstathā → रक्ष + अस्मान् + तथा

    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha); consonant sandhi (n + t → ṃst) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101); नश्छव्यप्रशान् (8.3.7)

    a + a → ā; final n before t becomes anusvāra + s (अस्मांस्तथा).

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • अत्यर्थघोराणि atyarthaghorāṇi

    karmadhāraya (the first member, अत्यर्थ ‘beyond measure’, qualifies adverbially)

    Members
    अत्यर्थ + घोर
    Vigraha
    अत्यर्थं घोराणि atyarthaṃ ghorāṇi
    Literally
    “excessively terrible”
    In the verse
    fearsome beyond all measure
    Alternative
    अत्यर्थ is itself a prādi compound, अर्थम् अतिक्रान्तम्, ‘passing the measure’.
Morphology & anvaya

  • सौम्यानि saumyāni

    Taddhita from सोम (the moon, or Soma): ‘moon-like’, hence mild, gentle; the opposite of घोर.

  • तैः taiḥ

    The correlative of both relative clauses (यानि … यानि च …): ‘with those (forms)’.

  • भुवम् bhuvam

    Accusative singular of the feminine root-noun भू ‘earth’.

Anvaya · prose order

यानि ते सौम्यानि रूपाणि त्रैलोक्ये विचरन्ति, यानि च अत्यर्थघोराणि (रूपाणि त्रैलोक्ये विचरन्ति), तैः अस्मान् तथा भुवं रक्ष।

yāni te saumyāni rūpāṇi trailokye vicaranti, yāni ca atyarthaghorāṇi (rūpāṇi trailokye vicaranti), taiḥ asmān tathā bhuvaṃ rakṣa |

Two relative clauses (यानि … यानि च …) taken up by one correlative (तैः), then the imperative with two objects.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

After the quarters of space, the forms of the Goddess: her gentle forms and her terrible ones alike are asked to guard the gods and the earth.

Traditional interpretation

The pairing of gentle (सौम्य) and terrible (घोर) forms is basic to Śākta thought; both are hers, and both protect. The hymn has shown both: the moon-like face of 4.12 and 4.20 and the face of wrath in 4.13.

Comparative Śrīvidyā notes

Comparative Śākta / Śrīvidyā

Lalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) includes the name सौम्या (910), ‘the gentle one’. The Devī Māhātmya asks for the protection of the gentle and the terrible forms together.

Verse 4.27

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

खड्गशूलगदादीनि यानि चास्त्राणि तेऽम्बिके।करपल्लवसङ्गीनि तैरस्मान् रक्ष सर्वतः॥⁠२७⁠॥

khaḍgaśūlagadādīni yāni cāstrāṇi te'mbike |karapallavasaṅgīni tairasmān rakṣa sarvataḥ ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

खड्गशूलगदादीनि · यानि · च · अस्त्राणि · ते · अम्बिकेकरपल्लवसङ्गीनि · तैः · अस्मान् · रक्ष · सर्वतः

khaḍgaśūlagadādīni · yāni · ca · astrāṇi · te · ambikekarapallavasaṅgīni · taiḥ · asmān · rakṣa · sarvataḥ

Word by word

  1. खड्गशूलगदादीनिkhaḍgaśūlagadādīnisword, trident, mace and the restn. nom. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies अस्त्राणि
  2. यानिyāniwhichrelative pronoun यद्, n. nom. pl.
  3. चcaandindeclinable (conjunctive particle)
  4. अस्त्राणिastrāṇiweaponsn. nom. pl. of अस्त्र (‘missile, weapon’)
  5. तेteyourenclitic form of युष्मद्, gen. sg.
  6. अम्बिकेambikeO Ambikā, O Motherf. voc. sg. of अम्बिका
  7. करपल्लवसङ्गीनिkarapallavasaṅgīniresting in your tender hands (lit. clinging to your sprout-like hands)n. nom. pl. of करपल्लवसङ्गिन्; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies अस्त्राणि
  8. तैःtaiḥwith thempronoun तद्, n. instr. pl.; correlative of यानि
  9. अस्मान्asmānusअस्मद्, acc. pl.; object of रक्ष
  10. रक्षrakṣaprotect√रक्ष् (bhvādi 1P), loṭ (imperative), madhyama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  11. सर्वतःsarvataḥon every sideindeclinable (सर्व + the suffix -तस्)

Translation

And your weapons, O Ambikā — sword, trident, mace and the rest — which cling to your sprout-like hands: with them protect us on every side.

In readable EnglishAnd with all your weapons, O Mother — sword, trident, mace and the rest — that rest in your tender hands, protect us on every side.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • खड्गशूलगदादीनि khaḍgaśūlagadādīni → खड्गशूलगदा + आदीनि

    vowel sandhi inside a compound (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    ā + ā → ā.

  • चास्त्राणि cāstrāṇi → च + अस्त्राणि

    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    a + a → ā.

  • तेऽम्बिके te'mbike → ते + अम्बिके

    vowel sandhi (pūrvarūpa) · एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109)

    -e + a- → -e’-: the initial a is elided (avagraha).

  • तैरस्मान् tairasmān → तैः + अस्मान्

    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)

    After ai the visarga (as ru) stands as r before a vowel.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • खड्गशूलगदादीनि khaḍgaśūlagadādīni

    bahuvrīhi with आदि, whose first member is an itaretara dvandva

    Members
    खड्ग + शूल + गदा + आदि
    Vigraha
    खड्गः च शूलं च गदा च खड्गशूलगदाः; ताः आदौ येषां तानि khaḍgaḥ ca śūlaṃ ca gadā ca khaḍgaśūlagadāḥ; tāḥ ādau yeṣāṃ tāni
    Literally
    “having sword, trident and mace at their head”
    In the verse
    the sword, the trident, the mace and all the rest

  • करपल्लवसङ्गीनि karapallavasaṅgīni

    saptamī tatpuruṣa whose first member is a karmadhāraya of comparison (करपल्लव, with the standard of comparison पल्लव placed second)

    Members
    कर + पल्लव + सङ्गिन्
    Vigraha
    करः पल्लवः इव करपल्लवः; करपल्लवेषु सङ्गीनि karaḥ pallavaḥ iva karapallavaḥ; karapallaveṣu saṅgīni
    Literally
    “clinging to the sprout-like hands”
    In the verse
    held in her soft, leaf-tender hands
    Alternative
    करपल्लव can also be read as a metaphor, ‘the hand that is a tender shoot’ (करः एव पल्लवः); the meaning is the same.
Morphology & anvaya

  • करपल्लवसङ्गीनि karapallavasaṅgīni

    The last member सङ्गिन् (√सञ्ज् ‘cling’ + -इन्) is declined as a neuter -इन् stem: nom. pl. -ईनि.

  • सर्वतः sarvataḥ

    सर्व + the suffix -तस्, ‘from all sides’, hence ‘on every side’.

Anvaya · prose order

(हे) अम्बिके, खड्गशूलगदादीनि करपल्लवसङ्गीनि यानि च ते अस्त्राणि (सन्ति), तैः अस्मान् सर्वतः रक्ष।

(he) ambike, khaḍgaśūlagadādīni karapallavasaṅgīni yāni ca te astrāṇi (santi), taiḥ asmān sarvataḥ rakṣa |

A relative clause (यानि … अस्त्राणि) taken up by the correlative तैः; the imperative रक्ष ends the hymn’s petitions.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The last petition of the hymn: all her weapons, held in her soft hands, are asked to guard the gods from every side. It completes the prayer begun in 4.24 with trident and sword.

Traditional interpretation

Terrible weapons in leaf-tender hands repeat in small the hymn’s theme of the gentle and the fierce in one Goddess. With this verse the Śakrādi Stuti ends; the narrative resumes at 4.28, and the gods worship her with flowers and incense (4.29–30).

Comparative Śrīvidyā notes

Comparative Śākta / Śrīvidyā

Lalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) has the name शूलाद्यायुधसम्पन्ना (506), ‘endowed with the trident and other weapons’, built with the same ‘X and the rest’ (आदि) pattern as खड्गशूलगदादीनि. The works are separate.

Verse 4.28

Speaker
‹›

ऋषिरुवाच॥⁠२८⁠॥

ṛṣiruvāca ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

ऋषिः · उवाच

ṛṣiḥ · uvāca

Word by word

  1. ऋषिःṛṣiḥthe seer (Medhas)m. nom. sg. of ऋषि (i-stem); subject of उवाच
  2. उवाचuvācasaid√वच् (adādi, 2P ‘to speak’), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, kartari prayoga

Translation

The seer said:

In readable EnglishThe seer said:

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • ऋषिरुवाच ṛṣiruvāca → ऋषिः + उवाच

    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)

    The final s of ऋषिस् becomes ru. After a vowel other than a/ā and before a vowel it is not turned into visarga (खरवसानयोर्विसर्जनीयः, 8.3.15, applies only at a pause or before an unvoiced consonant), so it stands as r and joins the next word: ṛṣir uvāca.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

No compound in this verse needs analysis.

Morphology & anvaya

  • उवाच uvāca

    Perfect of √वच् with samprasāraṇa in the reduplicating syllable (व → उ): ‘(he) said’, the narrative past.

Anvaya · prose order

ऋषिः उवाच।

ṛṣiḥ uvāca |

Subject and verb: a speaker label.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The hymn of Indra and the gods (4.2–27) is over, and Medhas, the seer, resumes his narration to King Suratha and the merchant Samādhi. What follows is the close of the Madhyama Carita: the Goddess’s answer to the hymn and the boons the gods ask.

Verse 4.29

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

एवं स्तुता सुरैर्दिव्यैः कुसुमैर्नन्दनोद्भवैः।अर्चिता जगतां धात्री तथा गन्धानुलेपनैः॥⁠२९⁠॥

evaṃ stutā surairdivyaiḥ kusumairnandanodbhavaiḥ |arcitā jagatāṃ dhātrī tathā gandhānulepanaiḥ ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

एवम् · स्तुता · सुरैः · दिव्यैः · कुसुमैः · नन्दनोद्भवैःअर्चिता · जगताम् · धात्री · तथा · गन्धानुलेपनैः

evam · stutā · suraiḥ · divyaiḥ · kusumaiḥ · nandanodbhavaiḥarcitā · jagatām · dhātrī · tathā · gandhānulepanaiḥ

Word by word

  1. एवम्evamthus, in this wayindeclinable (adverb of manner); refers back to the hymn 4.2–27
  2. स्तुताstutāpraisedpast passive participle (kta) of √स्तु (adādi, 2U ‘to praise’), f. nom. sg.; qualifies धात्री
  3. सुरैःsuraiḥby the godsm. instr. pl. of सुर; agent of स्तुता (and, understood, of अर्चिता)
  4. दिव्यैःdivyaiḥheavenlyn. instr. pl. of दिव्य; qualifies कुसुमैः
  5. कुसुमैःkusumaiḥwith flowersn. instr. pl. of कुसुम; instrument of अर्चिता
  6. नन्दनोद्भवैःnandanodbhavaiḥgrown in Nandana, Indra’s gardenn. instr. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies कुसुमैः
  7. अर्चिताarcitāworshipped, honouredpast passive participle (kta, with iṭ) of √अर्च् (bhvādi, 1P ‘to honour’), f. nom. sg.; qualifies धात्री
  8. जगताम्jagatāmof the worldsn. gen. pl. of जगत्; depends on धात्री
  9. धात्रीdhātrīsustainer, nurse-motherf. nom. sg. of धात्री (√धा + tṛc, feminine in -ī); the subject, carried into 4.30 (प्राह)
  10. तथाtathālikewise, and alsoindeclinable (connective adverb)
  11. गन्धानुलेपनैःgandhānulepanaiḥwith perfumes and unguentsn. instr. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); instrument of अर्चिता

Translation

Thus praised by the gods, worshipped with heavenly flowers grown in Nandana, and likewise with perfumes and unguents, the sustainer of the worlds —

In readable EnglishPraised in this way by the gods, and honoured with heavenly flowers from the garden of Nandana, with perfumes and with fragrant pastes, the Mother of the worlds —

धात्री is rendered ‘Mother’, its common sense of nurse and sustainer; the sentence is completed in 4.30.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • एवं स्तुता evaṃ stutā → एवम् + स्तुता

    anusvāra sandhi · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)

    A word-final m before a consonant becomes anusvāra. The same gives जगतां धात्री.

  • सुरैर्दिव्यैः surairdivyaiḥ → सुरैः + दिव्यैः

    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)

    After ai (a vowel other than a/ā) the final s becomes ru and stays r before the voiced d. The same in कुसुमैर्नन्दनोद्भवैः.

  • नन्दनोद्भवैः nandanodbhavaiḥ → नन्दन + उद्भवैः

    vowel sandhi inside a compound (guṇa) · आद्गुणः (6.1.87)

    a + u → o.

  • गन्धानुलेपनैः gandhānulepanaiḥ → गन्ध + अनुलेपनैः

    vowel sandhi inside a compound (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    a + a → ā.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • नन्दनोद्भवैः nandanodbhavaiḥ

    bahuvrīhi (vyadhikaraṇa: the members are in different cases)

    Members
    नन्दन + उद्भव
    Vigraha
    नन्दने उद्भवः येषां तानि, तैः nandane udbhavaḥ yeṣāṃ tāni, taiḥ
    Literally
    “(flowers) whose origin is in Nandana”
    In the verse
    flowers from Nandana, the garden of Indra’s heaven
    Alternative
    It could be taken as a pañcamī tatpuruṣa with उद्भव in the sense ‘sprung’ (नन्दनात् उद्भवः); but उद्भव is an action noun ‘origin’, so the bahuvrīhi is the natural analysis. The sense is the same.

  • गन्धानुलेपनैः gandhānulepanaiḥ

    itaretara dvandva

    Members
    गन्ध + अनुलेपन
    Vigraha
    गन्धाः च अनुलेपनानि च, तैः gandhāḥ ca anulepanāni ca, taiḥ
    Literally
    “with perfumes and unguents”
    In the verse
    fragrant substances and anointing pastes, two of the offerings of worship
    Alternative
    Also possible as a tatpuruṣa, ‘with fragrant unguents’ (गन्धयुक्तानि अनुलेपनानि); with तथा marking a further class of offering, the dvandva reads more naturally.
Morphology & anvaya

  • स्तुता stutā

    √स्तु is aniṭ: the participle takes -ta directly (स्तुत), feminine स्तुता.

  • अर्चिता arcitā

    √अर्च् takes the connecting -i- (iṭ): अर्चित, feminine अर्चिता. Both participles are passive and agree with the Goddess as subject.

  • धात्री dhātrī

    Agent noun धातृ ‘one who holds, supports’ (√धा + tṛc) made feminine with -ī: ‘sustainer’; in common usage also ‘nurse, foster-mother’.

Anvaya · prose order

एवं सुरैः स्तुता, दिव्यैः नन्दनोद्भवैः कुसुमैः तथा गन्धानुलेपनैः अर्चिता जगतां धात्री (4.30 … प्राह)।

evaṃ suraiḥ stutā, divyaiḥ nandanodbhavaiḥ kusumaiḥ tathā gandhānulepanaiḥ arcitā jagatāṃ dhātrī (4.30 … prāha) |

The sentence runs into 4.30: two participial phrases (स्तुता …, अर्चिता …) describe the subject जगतां धात्री, whose verb is प्राह in the next verse.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

Medhas describes what followed the hymn: the gods do not stop at words but worship the Goddess with flowers from Nandana, Indra’s pleasure-garden, and with perfumes and unguents. The incense follows in 4.30.

Traditional interpretation

Flowers, fragrance and (in the next verse) incense are among the standard upacāras of pūjā. In the story the gods perform for the Goddess the worship that her devotees go on to offer when the text is recited.

Comparative Śrīvidyā notes

Comparative Śākta / Śrīvidyā

Lalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) has the name जगद्धात्री (935), ‘sustainer of the world’; the Devī Māhātmya says the same in two words, जगतां धात्री. The two texts remain separate works.

Verse 4.30

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

भक्त्या समस्तैस्त्रिदशैर्दिव्यैर्धूपैस्तु धूपिता।प्राह प्रसादसुमुखी समस्तान् प्रणतान् सुरान्॥⁠३०⁠॥

bhaktyā samastaistridaśairdivyairdhūpaistu dhūpitā |prāha prasādasumukhī samastān praṇatān surān ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

भक्त्या · समस्तैः · त्रिदशैः · दिव्यैः · धूपैः · तु · धूपिताप्राह · प्रसादसुमुखी · समस्तान् · प्रणतान् · सुरान्

bhaktyā · samastaiḥ · tridaśaiḥ · divyaiḥ · dhūpaiḥ · tu · dhūpitāprāha · prasādasumukhī · samastān · praṇatān · surān

Word by word

  1. भक्त्याbhaktyāwith devotionf. instr. sg. of भक्ति (√भज् + ktin); instrumental of manner
  2. समस्तैःsamastaiḥby allm. instr. pl. of समस्त (सम् + √अस् ‘to throw’ + kta: ‘thrown together’, hence ‘all’); qualifies त्रिदशैः
  3. त्रिदशैःtridaśaiḥby the gods (lit. ‘the Thirty’)m. instr. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); agent of धूपिता
  4. दिव्यैःdivyaiḥheavenlym. instr. pl. of दिव्य; qualifies धूपैः
  5. धूपैःdhūpaiḥwith incensesm. instr. pl. of धूप; instrument of धूपिता
  6. तुtuand, moreoverindeclinable (connective particle, adding a further act)
  7. धूपिताdhūpitācensed, perfumed with incensepast passive participle (kta, with iṭ) of the stem धूपय- (√धूप् ‘to fumigate’, curādi or causative), f. nom. sg.; qualifies the Goddess
  8. प्राहprāhaspoke, saidप्र + √ब्रू (adādi, 2U ‘to speak’) with the substitute stem आह्; laṭ, prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada; used as a narrative past
  9. प्रसादसुमुखीprasādasumukhīher face bright with favourf. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies the subject (जगतां धात्री, 4.29)
  10. समस्तान्samastānallm. acc. pl. of समस्त; qualifies सुरान्
  11. प्रणतान्praṇatānbowed downpast participle (kta) of प्र + √नम् (bhvādi, 1P ‘to bow’), m. acc. pl.; qualifies सुरान्
  12. सुरान्surānto the godsm. acc. pl. of सुर; the person addressed, object of प्राह

Translation

and censed with devotion by all the Thirty with heavenly incenses, she, fair-faced with favour, spoke to all the gods, who were bowed down.

In readable Englishand when all the gods had devoutly offered her heavenly incense, she, her face bright with grace, spoke to the whole company of gods as they bowed before her.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • समस्तैस्त्रिदशैर्दिव्यैर्धूपैस्तु samastaistridaśairdivyairdhūpaistu → समस्तैः + त्रिदशैः + दिव्यैः + धूपैः + तु

    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34)

    One run of four instrumentals shows both outcomes of a visarga after ai: before the unvoiced t it becomes s (समस्तैस्, धूपैस्); before the voiced d and dh the ru stays r (त्रिदशैर्, दिव्यैर्).

  • प्राह prāha → प्र + आह

    vowel sandhi of prefix and verb (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    The prefix प्र joins the verb आह: a + ā → ā.

  • समस्तान् प्रणतान् सुरान् samastān praṇatān surān → समस्तान् + प्रणतान् + सुरान्

    no change · —

    Final n before p and s needs no change; the Gita Press prints the n with a virāma and a space.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • त्रिदशैः tridaśaiḥ

    bahuvrīhi (of number)

    Members
    त्रि + दश
    Vigraha
    त्रिः आवृत्ताः दश परिमाणम् एषाम्, तैः triḥ āvṛttāḥ daśa parimāṇam eṣām, taiḥ
    Literally
    “those whose count is three tens”
    In the verse
    the gods, the ‘Thirty’, a round figure for the thirty-three gods of the Veda
    Alternative
    A traditional etymology reads तृतीया दशा (यौवनम्) येषां ते, ‘those who are ever in the third stage of life (youth)’, i.e. the ageless gods; also a bahuvrīhi. The numerical reading is the usual one.

  • प्रसादसुमुखी prasādasumukhī

    tṛtīyā tatpuruṣa, its second member a bahuvrīhi

    Members
    प्रसाद + सुमुखी
    Vigraha
    प्रसादेन सुमुखी (सुमुखी = शोभनं मुखं यस्याः सा) prasādena sumukhī (sumukhī = śobhanaṃ mukhaṃ yasyāḥ sā)
    Literally
    “fair-faced through favour”
    In the verse
    her face showing her pleasure and grace
    Alternative
    Also read as a pañcamī (cause) relation, प्रसादात् सुमुखी, ‘fair-faced because pleased’; the sense is the same.
Morphology & anvaya

  • प्राह prāha

    आह is the substitute that √ब्रू takes in the present (laṭ) parasmaipada, first five endings (आह, आहतुः, आहुः, आत्थ, आहथुः). It is used in narrative for ‘said’. Some grammarians instead treat आह as a perfect of a defective root अह्; either way it means ‘said’.

  • धूपिता dhūpitā

    Denominative-causative stem धूपय- ‘to perfume with incense’ + kta with iṭ: धूपित, ‘censed’. The cognate noun धूपैः stands beside it (‘censed with incense’).

  • प्रणतान् praṇatān

    प्र + √नम् + kta: the nasal is lost before -ta (नत), and n becomes ṇ after प्र: प्रणत, ‘bowed’.

Anvaya · prose order

(एवं स्तुता अर्चिता जगतां धात्री) समस्तैः त्रिदशैः भक्त्या दिव्यैः धूपैः तु धूपिता प्रसादसुमुखी (सती) समस्तान् प्रणतान् सुरान् प्राह।

(evaṃ stutā arcitā jagatāṃ dhātrī) samastaiḥ tridaśaiḥ bhaktyā divyaiḥ dhūpaiḥ tu dhūpitā prasādasumukhī (satī) samastān praṇatān surān prāha |

The main verb प्राह completes the sentence begun in 4.29; its subject is जगतां धात्री, described by a third participle (धूपिता) and the adjective प्रसादसुमुखी. सुरान् is the person addressed.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The sentence that began in 4.29 ends here. Praise, flowers, unguents and incense are followed by the Goddess’s favour, which shows on her face (प्रसादसुमुखी). The fierce face that terrified Mahiṣa (4.13) is now a gracious one.

Comparative Śrīvidyā notes

Comparative Śākta / Śrīvidyā

Lalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) names the Goddess सुमुखी (459) and सद्यःप्रसादिनी (383, ‘quickly gracious’). The Devī Māhātmya joins the two ideas in one epithet, प्रसादसुमुखी.

Verse 4.31

Speaker
‹›

देव्युवाच॥⁠३१⁠॥

devyuvāca ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

देवी · उवाच

devī · uvāca

Word by word

  1. देवीdevīthe Goddessf. nom. sg. of देवी (ī-stem); subject of उवाच
  2. उवाचuvācasaid√वच् (adādi, 2P), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana

Translation

The Goddess said:

In readable EnglishThe Goddess said:

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • देव्युवाच devyuvāca → देवी + उवाच

    vowel sandhi (yaṇ) · इको यणचि (6.1.77)

    Final ī before the unlike vowel u becomes y: devy uvāca.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

No compound in this verse needs analysis.

Morphology & anvaya

  • उवाच uvāca

    As in 4.28: perfect of √वच्, ‘said’.

Anvaya · prose order

देवी उवाच।

devī uvāca |

Subject and verb: a speaker label.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The Goddess speaks to the gods. In the Madhyama Carita she has spoken once before, the taunt to Mahiṣa before his death (3.37–38); now she speaks to offer a boon.

Verse 4.32

Half-śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

व्रियतां त्रिदशाः सर्वे यदस्मत्तोऽभिवाञ्छितम्॥⁠३२⁠॥

vriyatāṃ tridaśāḥ sarve yadasmatto'bhivāñchitam ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

व्रियताम् · त्रिदशाः · सर्वे · यत् · अस्मत्तः · अभिवाञ्छितम्

vriyatām · tridaśāḥ · sarve · yat · asmattaḥ · abhivāñchitam

Word by word

  1. व्रियताम्vriyatāmlet (it) be chosen — choose√वृ ‘to choose’ (वृङ्, kryādi, 9Ā; also svādi, 5U), karmaṇi prayoga, loṭ (imperative), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, ātmanepada
  2. त्रिदशाःtridaśāḥO gods (the Thirty)m. voc. pl. of त्रिदश; compound (see 4.30)
  3. सर्वेsarveallm. voc. pl. of सर्व; qualifies त्रिदशाः
  4. यत्yatwhateverrelative pronoun यद्, n. nom. sg.; the grammatical subject of the passive व्रियताम्
  5. अस्मत्तःasmattaḥfrom us — from meablative in -tas (tasil) of अस्मद्, plural stem; the plural of majesty, the Goddess speaking of herself
  6. अभिवाञ्छितम्abhivāñchitamdesiredpast passive participle (kta, with iṭ) of अभि + √वाञ्छ् (bhvādi, 1P ‘to wish’), n. nom. sg.; predicate of यत्

Translation

Let it be chosen, O all you Thirty, whatever is desired from us.

In readable EnglishAll you gods, choose whatever you desire of me.

The passive imperative is rendered as an active ‘choose’, and the plural of majesty (अस्मत्तः) as ‘me’.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • व्रियतां त्रिदशाः vriyatāṃ tridaśāḥ → व्रियताम् + त्रिदशाः

    anusvāra sandhi · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)

    Final m before a consonant becomes anusvāra.

  • यदस्मत्तो yadasmatto → यत् + अस्मत्तः

    consonant sandhi (voicing) · झलां जशोऽन्ते (8.2.39)

    Final t becomes d before a vowel.

  • अस्मत्तोऽभिवाञ्छितम् asmatto'bhivāñchitam → अस्मत्तः + अभिवाञ्छितम्

    visarga sandhi, then pūrvarūpa · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), अतो रोरप्लुतादप्लुते (6.1.113), आद्गुणः (6.1.87), एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109)

    -aḥ before a: the ru becomes u, a + u gives o, and the following a is absorbed into the o (avagraha).

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

No compound in this verse needs analysis.

Morphology & anvaya

  • व्रियताम् vriyatām

    Passive imperative: √वृ + yak, with ṛ becoming ri before the passive -ya- (व्रिय-), and the ātmanepada imperative ending -ताम्. ‘Let (whatever is desired) be chosen’ is the courteous form of ‘choose!’.

  • अस्मत्तः asmattaḥ

    The suffix -tas (tasil) gives ablative sense to pronouns: मत्तः ‘from me’, अस्मत्तः ‘from us’. The Goddess uses the plural of herself.

  • अभिवाञ्छितम् abhivāñchitam

    अभि + √वाञ्छ् + kta with iṭ: ‘wished for’. Neuter, agreeing with यत्.

Anvaya · prose order

(हे) सर्वे त्रिदशाः, यत् अस्मत्तः अभिवाञ्छितम् (तत्) व्रियताम्।

(he) sarve tridaśāḥ, yat asmattaḥ abhivāñchitam (tat) vriyatām |

A passive imperative with a relative clause as its subject: ‘let (that) be chosen which is desired from us’; the correlative तत् is understood.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

A half-verse: the Goddess, pleased by the hymn and the worship, offers the gods a boon. This is the classical response of a deity to praise, and it sets up the request that follows (4.34–37).

Traditional interpretation

The same pattern returns at the end of the Uttama Carita: after the hymn to Nārāyaṇī the Goddess says वरदाहं सुरगणा वरं यन्मनसेच्छथ (11.37), and the gods again ask for the destruction of their enemies and the removal of every affliction.

Verse 4.33

Speaker
‹›

देवा ऊचुः॥⁠३३⁠॥

devā ūcuḥ ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

देवाः · ऊचुः

devāḥ · ūcuḥ

Word by word

  1. देवाःdevāḥthe godsm. nom. pl. of देव; subject of ऊचुः
  2. ऊचुःūcuḥsaid√वच् (adādi, 2P), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa bahuvacana, parasmaipada

Translation

The gods said:

In readable EnglishThe gods said:

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • देवा ऊचुः devā ūcuḥ → देवाः + ऊचुः

    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), भोभगोअघोअपूर्वस्य योऽशि (8.3.17), लोपः शाकल्यस्य (8.3.19)

    -āḥ before a vowel: the ru becomes y, the y is dropped, and the two vowels stand apart (देवा | ऊचुः) without further joining.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

No compound in this verse needs analysis.

Morphology & anvaya

  • ऊचुः ūcuḥ

    Perfect plural of √वच्: in the weak forms the root takes samprasāraṇa (वच् → उच्), and the reduplicating उ + उच् gives ऊच्: ऊचुः ‘they said’. Compare the singular उवाच (4.28).

Anvaya · prose order

देवाः ऊचुः।

devāḥ ūcuḥ |

Subject and verb: a speaker label.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The gods answer the Goddess’s offer of a boon (4.32). Their reply runs to 4.37, the last speech of the Madhyama Carita before Medhas closes the story.

Verse 4.34

Half-śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

भगवत्या कृतं सर्वं न किंचिदवशिष्यते॥⁠३४⁠॥

bhagavatyā kṛtaṃ sarvaṃ na kiṃcidavaśiṣyate ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

भगवत्या · कृतम् · सर्वम् · न · किंचित् · अवशिष्यते

bhagavatyā · kṛtam · sarvam · na · kiṃcit · avaśiṣyate

Word by word

  1. भगवत्याbhagavatyāby the Blessed One — by youf. instr. sg. of भगवती; agent of the passive कृतम्; the respectful third person used for ‘you’
  2. कृतम्kṛtamdonepast passive participle (kta) of √कृ (tanādi, 8U ‘to do’), n. nom. sg.; predicate of सर्वम्
  3. सर्वम्sarvameverythingn. nom. sg. of सर्व; subject
  4. नnanotindeclinable (negative particle)
  5. किंचित्kiṃcitanything at allindefinite pronoun: किम् (n. nom. sg.) + the particle चित्; with न, ‘nothing’
  6. अवशिष्यतेavaśiṣyateremains, is left overअव + √शिष् (rudhādi, 7P ‘to leave’), karmaṇi form, laṭ (present), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, ātmanepada

Translation

Everything has been done by the Blessed One; nothing at all remains.

In readable EnglishYou, Blessed One, have done everything; nothing is left to be done.

The respectful third person भगवत्या is turned into the second person ‘you’.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • कृतं सर्वं न kṛtaṃ sarvaṃ na → कृतम् + सर्वम् + न

    anusvāra sandhi · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)

    Final m before a consonant becomes anusvāra, twice.

  • किंचिदवशिष्यते kiṃcidavaśiṣyate → किंचित् + अवशिष्यते

    consonant sandhi (voicing) · झलां जशोऽन्ते (8.2.39)

    Final t becomes d before a vowel.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

No compound in this verse needs analysis.

Morphology & anvaya

  • अवशिष्यते avaśiṣyate

    Passive of अव + √शिष् ‘to leave’ (stem शिष्य- with yak): ‘is left’, used in the intransitive sense ‘remains’.

  • भगवत्या bhagavatyā

    Instrumental of भगवती, the feminine of भगवत् (भग + matup, ‘possessing fortune’). Speaking of the person addressed in the third person is a mark of reverence.

Anvaya · prose order

भगवत्या सर्वं कृतम्; किंचित् न अवशिष्यते।

bhagavatyā sarvaṃ kṛtam; kiṃcit na avaśiṣyate |

Two short clauses: a passive statement with the agent in the instrumental (भगवत्या … कृतम्), then a negated present.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

A half-verse. The gods’ first answer is that they need nothing more: what they asked for has been accomplished. The reason is given in the next line (4.35): Mahiṣa is dead.

Modern reflection

A modern reading: the gods begin by declaring themselves content before they ask anything, and when they do ask, it is for protection and for others (4.36–37).

Verse 4.35

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

यदयं निहतः शत्रुरस्माकं महिषासुरः।यदि चापि वरो देयस्त्वयास्माकं महेश्वरि॥⁠३५⁠॥

yadayaṃ nihataḥ śatrurasmākaṃ mahiṣāsuraḥ |yadi cāpi varo deyastvayāsmākaṃ maheśvari ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

यत् · अयम् · निहतः · शत्रुः · अस्माकम् · महिषासुरःयदि · च · अपि · वरः · देयः · त्वया · अस्माकम् · महेश्वरि

yat · ayam · nihataḥ · śatruḥ · asmākam · mahiṣāsuraḥyadi · ca · api · varaḥ · deyaḥ · tvayā · asmākam · maheśvari

Word by word

  1. यत्yatin that, sinceindeclinable use of the relative यद् (conjunction ‘that, because’); gives the reason for 4.34
  2. अयम्ayamthisdemonstrative pronoun इदम्, m. nom. sg.; qualifies महिषासुरः
  3. निहतःnihataḥslainpast passive participle (kta) of नि + √हन् (adādi, 2P ‘to strike, kill’), m. nom. sg.; predicate
  4. शत्रुःśatruḥenemym. nom. sg. of शत्रु (u-stem); in apposition to महिषासुरः
  5. अस्माकम्asmākamourfirst-person pronoun अस्मद्, gen. pl.; depends on शत्रुः
  6. महिषासुरःmahiṣāsuraḥMahiṣāsura, the buffalo-demonm. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); subject
  7. यदिyadiifindeclinable (conditional conjunction)
  8. चcaandindeclinable (conjunction)
  9. अपिapistill, even soindeclinable (particle); with यदि च, ‘and if even so’
  10. वरःvaraḥa boonm. nom. sg. of वर; subject of the gerundive देयः
  11. देयःdeyaḥis to be givengerundive (kṛtya affix yat) of √दा (juhotyādi, 3U ‘to give’), m. nom. sg.; agrees with वरः
  12. त्वयाtvayāby yousecond-person pronoun युष्मद्, instr. sg.; agent of देयः
  13. अस्माकम्asmākamto usअस्मद्, gen. pl., in the sense of the recipient (genitive for dative)
  14. महेश्वरिmaheśvariO Great Sovereignf. voc. sg. of महेश्वरी; compound (see Samāsa)

Translation

in that this Mahiṣāsura, our enemy, has been slain. And if even so a boon is to be given to us by you, O Great Sovereign, —

In readable Englishfor Mahiṣāsura, our enemy, lies slain. Yet if you would still grant us a boon, O Supreme Sovereign, —

The gerundive ‘is to be given by you’ is rendered ‘if you would grant’; the sentence continues in 4.36.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • यदयं yadayaṃ → यत् + अयम्

    consonant sandhi (voicing) · झलां जशोऽन्ते (8.2.39)

    Final t becomes d before a vowel; the m of अयम् becomes anusvāra before n (मोऽनुस्वारः, 8.3.23).

  • निहतः शत्रुरस्माकं nihataḥ śatrurasmākaṃ → निहतः + शत्रुः + अस्माकम्

    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)

    Before the sibilant ś the visarga of निहतः may stand unchanged (an optional rule; the alternative would be निहतश्शत्रुः). After u and before a vowel the ru of शत्रुः stays r: शत्रुरस्माकम्.

  • चापि वरो देयस्त्वयास्माकं cāpi varo deyastvayāsmākaṃ → च + अपि + वरः + देयः + त्वया + अस्माकम्

    vowel and visarga sandhi · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101); हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87); विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34)

    a + a → ā (चापि) and ā + a → ā (त्वयास्माकम्); -aḥ before the voiced d → -o (वरो); visarga before t → s (देयस्त्वया).

  • महेश्वरि maheśvari → महा + ईश्वरि

    vowel sandhi inside a compound (guṇa) · आद्गुणः (6.1.87)

    ā + ī → e.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • महिषासुरः mahiṣāsuraḥ

    karmadhāraya

    Members
    महिष + असुर
    Vigraha
    महिषः च असौ असुरः च mahiṣaḥ ca asau asuraḥ ca
    Literally
    “the asura who is Mahiṣa (the buffalo)”
    In the verse
    Mahiṣa, the buffalo-demon
    Alternative
    Also analysed as a madhyamapadalopī karmadhāraya, महिषरूपः असुरः, ‘the asura in buffalo form’; the sense is the same.

  • महेश्वरि maheśvari

    karmadhāraya

    Members
    महत् + ईश्वरी
    Vigraha
    महती च असौ ईश्वरी च mahatī ca asau īśvarī ca
    Literally
    “great sovereign lady”
    In the verse
    Supreme Ruler — the feminine of Śiva’s title Maheśvara
    Alternative
    महत् takes the form महा- as the first member of a karmadhāraya, then महा + ईश्वरी → महेश्वरी.
Morphology & anvaya

  • निहतः nihataḥ

    नि + √हन् + kta: the root’s nasal is lost before -ta (हत): ‘struck down, slain’.

  • देयः deyaḥ

    Gerundive of √दा with the kṛtya affix yat; the ā becomes e before it: देय, ‘to be given’. With the agent in the instrumental (त्वया) it forms an impersonal ‘must/should be given by you’.

  • अस्माकम् asmākam

    The genitive plural appears twice: first possessive (‘our enemy’), then for the recipient (‘to us’), where classical prose would often use the dative.

Anvaya · prose order

यत् अयम् अस्माकं शत्रुः महिषासुरः निहतः, (अतः सर्वं कृतम्)। (हे) महेश्वरि, यदि च अपि त्वया अस्माकं वरः देयः, (4.36 …)

yat ayam asmākaṃ śatruḥ mahiṣāsuraḥ nihataḥ, (ataḥ sarvaṃ kṛtam) | (he) maheśvari, yadi ca api tvayā asmākaṃ varaḥ deyaḥ, (4.36 …)

The first line is a causal clause (यत् … निहतः) explaining 4.34. The second line is a conditional (यदि …) whose main clause comes in 4.36: ‘if a boon is still to be given, (then) …’.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The first line gives the reason for 4.34: the enemy who drove the gods from heaven (2.1–8) is dead. The second line turns, politely and conditionally, to the request; the boon itself is named in 4.36–37.

Traditional interpretation

महेश्वरी, the feminine of Maheśvara, Śiva’s title, names the Goddess as sovereign in her own right. The gods’ courtesy — ‘if a boon is still to be given’ — is the tone in which the text has them ask.

Verse 4.36

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

संस्मृता संस्मृता त्वं नो हिंसेथाः परमापदः।यश्च मर्त्यः स्तवैरेभिस्त्वां स्तोष्यत्यमलानने॥⁠३६⁠॥

saṃsmṛtā saṃsmṛtā tvaṃ no hiṃsethāḥ paramāpadaḥ |yaśca martyaḥ stavairebhistvāṃ stoṣyatyamalānane ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

संस्मृता · संस्मृता · त्वम् · नः · हिंसेथाः · परमापदःयः · च · मर्त्यः · स्तवैः · एभिः · त्वाम् · स्तोष्यति · अमलानने

saṃsmṛtā · saṃsmṛtā · tvam · naḥ · hiṃsethāḥ · paramāpadaḥyaḥ · ca · martyaḥ · stavaiḥ · ebhiḥ · tvām · stoṣyati · amalānane

Word by word

  1. संस्मृताsaṃsmṛtārememberedpast passive participle (kta) of सम् + √स्मृ (bhvādi, 1P ‘to remember’), f. nom. sg.; qualifies त्वम्
  2. संस्मृताsaṃsmṛtā(each time) rememberedthe same form repeated (āmreḍita): ‘whenever remembered’
  3. त्वम्tvamyousecond-person pronoun युष्मद्, nom. sg.; subject of हिंसेथाः
  4. नःnaḥourfirst-person pronoun अस्मद्, enclitic gen. pl. (= अस्माकम्); depends on परमापदः
  5. हिंसेथाःhiṃsethāḥmay you destroy√हिंस् ‘to injure, destroy’ (classically rudhādi, 7P हिनस्ति; here conjugated on a thematic stem हिंस-), vidhiliṅ (optative), madhyama-puruṣa ekavacana, ātmanepada — an epic (ārṣa) form
  6. परमापदःparamāpadaḥthe gravest calamitiesf. acc. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); object of हिंसेथाः
  7. यःyaḥwhoeverrelative pronoun यद्, m. nom. sg.; its correlative is तस्य in 4.37
  8. चcaandindeclinable (conjunction)
  9. मर्त्यःmartyaḥmortal, human beingm. nom. sg. of मर्त्य; qualifies यः
  10. स्तवैःstavaiḥwith hymnsm. instr. pl. of स्तव (√स्तु + ap); instrument of स्तोष्यति
  11. एभिःebhiḥthesedemonstrative pronoun इदम्, m. instr. pl.; qualifies स्तवैः
  12. त्वाम्tvāmyouयुष्मद्, acc. sg.; object of स्तोष्यति
  13. स्तोष्यतिstoṣyatiwill praise√स्तु (adādi, 2U ‘to praise’), lṛṭ (simple future), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  14. अमलाननेamalānaneO you of stainless facef. voc. sg. of अमलानना; compound (see Samāsa)

Translation

Remembered, remembered, may you destroy our gravest calamities; and whatever mortal will praise you with these hymns, O you of stainless face, —

In readable Englishwhenever we call you to mind, destroy our worst calamities. And whoever among mortals praises you with these hymns, O Goddess of the stainless face, —

The repeated participle is rendered ‘whenever we call you to mind’; the sentence of the second line continues in 4.37.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • नो हिंसेथाः no hiṃsethāḥ → नः + हिंसेथाः

    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)

    -aḥ before the voiced h becomes -o.

  • यश्च yaśca → यः + च

    visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34), स्तोः श्चुना श्चुः (8.4.40)

    Visarga before c becomes s, and the s becomes the palatal ś next to the palatal c.

  • स्तवैरेभिस्त्वां stavairebhistvāṃ → स्तवैः + एभिः + त्वाम्

    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34)

    After ai and before a vowel the ru stays r (स्तवैरेभिः); after i and before t the visarga becomes s (एभिस्त्वाम्).

  • स्तोष्यत्यमलानने stoṣyatyamalānane → स्तोष्यति + अमलानने

    vowel sandhi (yaṇ) · इको यणचि (6.1.77)

    Final i before the unlike vowel a becomes y.

  • परमापदः paramāpadaḥ → परम + आपदः

    vowel sandhi inside a compound (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    a + ā → ā; likewise अमल + आनने → अमलानने.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • परमापदः paramāpadaḥ

    karmadhāraya

    Members
    परम + आपद्
    Vigraha
    परमाः च ताः आपदः च, ताः paramāḥ ca tāḥ āpadaḥ ca, tāḥ
    Literally
    “supreme calamities”
    In the verse
    the worst of misfortunes

  • अमलानने amalānane

    bahuvrīhi (its first member अमल is itself a nañ-bahuvrīhi)

    Members
    अमल + आनन
    Vigraha
    अमलम् आननं यस्याः सा, तत्सम्बुद्धौ (अमल = न विद्यते मलं यस्य तत्) amalam ānanaṃ yasyāḥ sā, tatsambuddhau (amala = na vidyate malaṃ yasya tat)
    Literally
    “she whose face is without stain”
    In the verse
    O Goddess of the spotless, radiant face
Morphology & anvaya

  • संस्मृता saṃsmṛtā

    Repetition of a word (āmreḍita) expresses ‘again and again, every time’: संस्मृता संस्मृता, ‘remembered each time’. The participle has a conditional force: ‘(when) remembered’.

  • हिंसेथाः hiṃsethāḥ

    Optative in -ethāḥ (stem in -a + -īthāḥ), the ātmanepada ending, from a root classically conjugated in the seventh class and parasmaipada (optative हिंस्याः). Such forms are common in epic and Purāṇic Sanskrit (ārṣa). Compare भवेथाः in 4.37.

  • स्तोष्यति stoṣyati

    Simple future of √स्तु: guṇa of u (स्तो-) + -sya-, with s becoming ṣ after o: स्तोष्यति. √स्तु is aniṭ, so no connecting -i-.

Anvaya · prose order

(हे) अमलानने, संस्मृता संस्मृता त्वं नः परमापदः हिंसेथाः। यः च मर्त्यः एभिः स्तवैः त्वां स्तोष्यति, (4.37 तस्य …)

(he) amalānane, saṃsmṛtā saṃsmṛtā tvaṃ naḥ paramāpadaḥ hiṃsethāḥ | yaḥ ca martyaḥ ebhiḥ stavaiḥ tvāṃ stoṣyati, (4.37 tasya …)

The first line is the main clause of the conditional begun in 4.35 (‘if a boon is to be given, may you destroy …’). The second line opens a relative clause (यः …) whose correlative तस्य comes in 4.37.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The gods ask two boons. The first is for themselves: that the Goddess, whenever remembered, should destroy their gravest calamities. The second, begun here and completed in 4.37, is for any human being who praises her with ‘these hymns’, the hymn of 4.2–27.

Traditional interpretation

With these two verses the hymn acquires its fruit (phala) for later reciters. The Goddess herself confirms the promise at length in chapter 12, the Phalastuti, which opens in almost the same words: एभिः स्तवैश्च मां नित्यं स्तोष्यते यः समाहितः (12.2).

Modern reflection

A modern reading: the gods, once rescued, ask for help not only for themselves but for every person who will ever say their words. Gratitude turns into intercession.

Verse 4.37

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

तस्य वित्तर्द्धिविभवैर्धनदारादिसम्पदाम्।वृद्धयेऽस्मत्प्रसन्ना त्वं भवेथाः सर्वदाम्बिके॥⁠३७⁠॥

tasya vittarddhivibhavairdhanadārādisampadām |vṛddhaye'smatprasannā tvaṃ bhavethāḥ sarvadāmbike ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

तस्य · वित्तर्द्धिविभवैः · धनदारादिसम्पदाम्वृद्धये · अस्मत्प्रसन्ना · त्वम् · भवेथाः · सर्वदा · अम्बिके

tasya · vittarddhivibhavaiḥ · dhanadārādisampadāmvṛddhaye · asmatprasannā · tvam · bhavethāḥ · sarvadā · ambike

Word by word

  1. तस्यtasyaof him (that mortal)pronoun तद्, m. gen. sg.; correlative of यः in 4.36; depends on धनदारादिसम्पदाम्
  2. वित्तर्द्धिविभवैःvittarddhivibhavaiḥwith wealth, prosperity and splendourm. instr. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); instrumental of respect with वृद्धये (‘increase in …’)
  3. धनदारादिसम्पदाम्dhanadārādisampadāmof his possessions — riches, wife and the restf. gen. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); depends on वृद्धये
  4. वृद्धयेvṛddhayefor the increasef. dat. sg. of वृद्धि (√वृध् + ktin); dative of purpose
  5. अस्मत्प्रसन्नाasmatprasannāgracious to usf. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies त्वम्
  6. त्वम्tvamyouयुष्मद्, nom. sg.; subject of भवेथाः
  7. भवेथाःbhavethāḥmay you be√भू (bhvādi, 1P ‘to be’), vidhiliṅ (optative), madhyama-puruṣa ekavacana, ātmanepada — an epic (ārṣa) form for classical भवेः
  8. सर्वदाsarvadāalwaysindeclinable (adverb of time: सर्व + -dā)
  9. अम्बिकेambikeO Mother, O Ambikāf. voc. sg. of अम्बिका

Translation

for the increase of his possessions — riches, wife and the rest — in wealth, prosperity and splendour, may you, gracious to us, always be (present), O Ambikā.

In readable Englishmay you, O Mother, ever gracious to us, be with that person always, to increase his wealth, prosperity and fortune, his riches, his wife and family, and all he has.

The loose instrumental and the genitive are rendered together as a list of what increases; ‘be with that person’ makes explicit the sense of भवेथाः … वृद्धये, ‘be (there) for the increase’.

Textual verification / uncertain: The construction of the instrumental वित्तर्द्धिविभवैः is loose: it may go with वृद्धये (‘increase in wealth …’) or be read as a list parallel to धनदारादिसम्पदाम्. The meaning is the same either way; the reading of the text is not in doubt.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • वित्तर्द्धि vittarddhi → वित्त + ऋद्धि

    vowel sandhi inside a compound (guṇa) · आद्गुणः (6.1.87)

    a + ṛ → ar: vitta + ṛddhi → vittarddhi.

  • विभवैर्धन vibhavairdhana → विभवैः + धन…

    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)

    After ai and before the voiced dh the ru stays r.

  • वृद्धयेऽस्मत्प्रसन्ना vṛddhaye'smatprasannā → वृद्धये + अस्मत्प्रसन्ना

    vowel sandhi (pūrvarūpa) · एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109)

    Final e before initial a: the a is absorbed (avagraha). Inside the compound the d of अस्मद् is unvoiced before p (खरि च, 8.4.55).

  • सर्वदाम्बिके sarvadāmbike → सर्वदा + अम्बिके

    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    ā + a → ā; the same inside the compound धनदार + आदि → धनदारादि.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • वित्तर्द्धिविभवैः vittarddhivibhavaiḥ

    itaretara dvandva

    Members
    वित्त + ऋद्धि + विभव
    Vigraha
    वित्तं च ऋद्धिः च विभवः च, तैः vittaṃ ca ṛddhiḥ ca vibhavaḥ ca, taiḥ
    Literally
    “with wealth, prosperity and splendour”
    In the verse
    every kind of worldly fortune

  • धनदारादिसम्पदाम् dhanadārādisampadām

    karmadhāraya containing a bahuvrīhi in आदि, which contains a dvandva

    Members
    धन + दार + आदि + सम्पद्
    Vigraha
    धनं च दाराः च धनदाराः (itaretara dvandva); धनदाराः आदयः यासां ताः धनदारादयः (bahuvrīhi); धनदारादयः च ताः सम्पदः च, तासाम् (karmadhāraya) dhanaṃ ca dārāḥ ca dhanadārāḥ (itaretara dvandva); dhanadārāḥ ādayaḥ yāsāṃ tāḥ dhanadārādayaḥ (bahuvrīhi); dhanadārādayaḥ ca tāḥ sampadaḥ ca, tāsām (karmadhāraya)
    Literally
    “of the possessions that begin with wealth and wife”
    In the verse
    of his riches, his wife and family, and all he has
    Alternative
    Also possible as a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa, धनदारादीनां सम्पदः, ‘the prosperity of his wealth, wife and the rest’; the sense differs little.

  • अस्मत्प्रसन्ना asmatprasannā

    saptamī tatpuruṣa

    Members
    अस्मद् + प्रसन्ना
    Vigraha
    अस्मासु प्रसन्ना asmāsu prasannā
    Literally
    “gracious towards us”
    In the verse
    favourable to the gods who ask
    Alternative
    अस्मत् could also be read as a separate ablative plural, ‘pleased (by our praise) from us’; the printed text joins it, and ‘gracious to us’ is the natural sense.
Morphology & anvaya

  • भवेथाः bhavethāḥ

    Optative of √भू with the ātmanepada ending -ethāḥ; classical Sanskrit uses only the parasmaipada (भवेः). Like हिंसेथाः in 4.36, an epic (ārṣa) form, perhaps chosen for the metre.

  • वृद्धये vṛddhaye

    Dative of वृद्धि expressing purpose (tādarthya): ‘for the growth of …’.

  • सर्वदा sarvadā

    The suffix -dā forms adverbs of time from pronouns: सर्वदा ‘at all times’, यदा, तदा, कदा.

Anvaya · prose order

(हे) अम्बिके, अस्मत्प्रसन्ना त्वं सर्वदा तस्य वित्तर्द्धिविभवैः धनदारादिसम्पदां वृद्धये भवेथाः।

(he) ambike, asmatprasannā tvaṃ sarvadā tasya vittarddhivibhavaiḥ dhanadārādisampadāṃ vṛddhaye bhavethāḥ |

The main clause of the relative sentence begun in 4.36 (यः … स्तोष्यति — तस्य …). The genitive तस्य goes with धनदारादिसम्पदाम्; the instrumental वित्तर्द्धिविभवैः is loosely attached, best taken as ‘in respect of wealth …’ with वृद्धये.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The second boon, completed: whoever recites ‘these hymns’ should be granted increase in every worldly good — wealth, prosperity, splendour, riches, wife and household. The gods ask the Goddess to be always gracious to them, and so to him.

Traditional interpretation

The two boons together — protection from calamity when remembered (4.36) and prosperity for the reciter (4.37) — are the fruits the tradition attaches to the Śakrādi stuti. They are the first statement in the text of the reward of recitation, set out in full by the Goddess in chapter 12.

Modern reflection

A modern reading: the text does not separate devotion from ordinary life. Wealth, home and family are asked for openly, as things the Goddess is glad to increase.

Direct textual meaning

Medhas takes up the narration again after the dialogue of the Goddess and the gods (4.31–37), to tell Suratha how the episode ended and to announce the story still to come.

Verse 4.39

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

इति प्रसादिता देवैर्जगतोऽर्थे तथाऽऽत्मनः।तथेत्युक्त्वा भद्रकाली बभूवान्तर्हिता नृप॥⁠३९⁠॥

iti prasāditā devairjagato'rthe tathā''tmanaḥ |tathetyuktvā bhadrakālī babhūvāntarhitā nṛpa ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

इति · प्रसादिता · देवैः · जगतः · अर्थे · तथा · आत्मनःतथा · इति · उक्त्वा · भद्रकाली · बभूव · अन्तर्हिता · नृप

iti · prasāditā · devaiḥ · jagataḥ · arthe · tathā · ātmanaḥtathā · iti · uktvā · bhadrakālī · babhūva · antarhitā · nṛpa

Word by word

  1. इतिitithusindeclinable (closing the gods’ request)
  2. प्रसादिताprasāditāpropitiated, entreatedpast passive participle (kta, with iṭ) of the causative प्रसादय- (प्र + √सद् ‘to be gracious’; causative ‘to make gracious’), f. nom. sg.; qualifies भद्रकाली
  3. देवैःdevaiḥby the godsm. instr. pl. of देव; agent of प्रसादिता

  5. अर्थेarthefor the sake (of)m. loc. sg. of अर्थ; with a genitive, ‘for the sake of’
  6. तथाtathāand likewiseindeclinable (connective adverb)
  7. आत्मनःātmanaḥof themselvesm. gen. sg. of आत्मन्, the reflexive (singular in form, referring to the gods); depends on अर्थे understood
  8. तथाtathāso (be it)indeclinable; here a word of assent, quoted
  9. इतिiti(marks the quotation)indeclinable (quotative particle)
  10. उक्त्वाuktvāhaving saidabsolutive (ktvā) of √वच् (adādi, 2P), with samprasāraṇa
