Translation

Thus propitiated by the gods for the sake of the world and likewise of themselves, Bhadrakālī said ‘So be it’ and became vanished, O king.

In readable EnglishSo the gods entreated her, for the world’s sake and for their own; and Bhadrakālī said, ‘So be it,’ and vanished from their sight, O king.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • देवैर्जगतोऽर्थे devairjagato'rthe → देवैः + जगतः + अर्थे

    visarga sandhi, then pūrvarūpa · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66); अतो रोरप्लुतादप्लुते (6.1.113), आद्गुणः (6.1.87), एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109)

    After ai and before voiced j the ru stays r (देवैर्). -aḥ before a becomes -o and the a is absorbed (जगतोऽर्थे).

  • तथाऽऽत्मनः tathā''tmanaḥ → तथा + आत्मनः

    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    ā + ā → ā. The Gita Press writes a double avagraha (ऽऽ) to show that the long ā of आत्मनः has merged into the ā of तथा.

  • तथेत्युक्त्वा tathetyuktvā → तथा + इति + उक्त्वा

    vowel sandhi (guṇa, then yaṇ) · आद्गुणः (6.1.87); इको यणचि (6.1.77)

    ā + i → e (तथेति); then i before u becomes y (इत्युक्त्वा).

  • बभूवान्तर्हिता babhūvāntarhitā → बभूव + अन्तर्हिता

    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    a + a → ā.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • भद्रकाली bhadrakālī

    karmadhāraya

    Members
    भद्रा + काली
    Vigraha
    भद्रा च असौ काली च bhadrā ca asau kālī ca
    Literally
    “the auspicious Dark One”
    In the verse
    the Goddess as Bhadrakālī, fierce in form and auspicious in effect
    Alternative
    In a karmadhāraya a feminine adjective takes its masculine form as first member (भद्रा → भद्र-), hence भद्रकाली.

  • नृप nṛpa

    upapada tatpuruṣa

    Members
    नृ + प
    Vigraha
    नॄन् पाति इति nṝn pāti iti
    Literally
    “protector of men”
    In the verse
    king
Morphology & anvaya

  • प्रसादिता prasāditā

    Causative stem प्रसादय- (प्र + √सद् ‘to be clear, gracious’; causative ‘to make gracious, to propitiate’) + kta with iṭ, the causative -ay- dropped: प्रसादित.

  • उक्त्वा uktvā

    Absolutive of √वच् with samprasāraṇa (वच् → उच्) and c → k before t: उक्त्वा, ‘having said’.

  • अन्तर्हिता antarhitā

    अन्तर् is treated as a prefix of √धा; in the past participle धा becomes हि (हित): अन्तर्हित, ‘placed within, concealed’. With बभूव it forms a periphrasis ‘became hidden’, i.e. vanished.

Anvaya · prose order

(हे) नृप, इति देवैः जगतः अर्थे तथा आत्मनः (अर्थे) प्रसादिता भद्रकाली ‘तथा’ इति उक्त्वा अन्तर्हिता बभूव।

(he) nṛpa, iti devaiḥ jagataḥ arthe tathā ātmanaḥ (arthe) prasāditā bhadrakālī ‘tathā’ iti uktvā antarhitā babhūva |

The subject भद्रकाली is described by the participle प्रसादिता; the absolutive उक्त्वा precedes the main verb बभूव with its predicate अन्तर्हिता.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The Goddess grants the boons with one word, तथा, ‘so be it’, and disappears. Medhas reminds his listener (नृप, King Suratha) that he is telling a story. The gods asked both for the world (जगतः) and for themselves (आत्मनः), and both requests were granted.

Traditional interpretation

Here the narrative names her Bhadrakālī, the auspicious Kālī. The liturgical tradition of the Gita Press pāṭha assigns the Madhyama Carita to Mahālakṣmī; the Purāṇa itself moves freely between names for the one Goddess. Her vanishing completes this manifestation; she appears again when needed, as she promises in 11.54–55.

Verse 4.40

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

इत्येतत्कथितं भूप सम्भूता सा यथा पुरा।देवीदेवशरीरेभ्यो जगत्त्रयहितैषिणी॥⁠४०⁠॥

ityetatkathitaṃ bhūpa sambhūtā sā yathā purā |devīdevaśarīrebhyo jagattrayahitaiṣiṇī ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

इति · एतत् · कथितम् · भूप · सम्भूता · सा · यथा · पुरादेवी · देवशरीरेभ्यः · जगत्त्रयहितैषिणी

iti · etat · kathitam · bhūpa · sambhūtā · sā · yathā · purādevī · devaśarīrebhyaḥ · jagattrayahitaiṣiṇī

Word by word

  1. इतिitithusindeclinable (closing the account)
  2. एतत्etatthisdemonstrative pronoun एतद्, n. nom. sg.; subject of कथितम्
  3. कथितम्kathitamhas been toldpast passive participle (kta, with iṭ) of √कथ् (curādi, 10 ‘to tell’), n. nom. sg.; predicate
  4. भूपbhūpaO king (protector of the earth)m. voc. sg. of भूप; compound (see Samāsa)
  5. सम्भूताsambhūtācame into being, arosepast participle (kta) of सम् + √भू (bhvādi, 1P), active in sense, f. nom. sg.; predicate of सा
  6. साsāshepronoun तद्, f. nom. sg.
  7. यथाyathāhowindeclinable (relative adverb of manner)
  8. पुराpurāformerly, long agoindeclinable (adverb of time)
  9. देवीdevīthe Goddessf. nom. sg. of देवी; in apposition to सा
  10. देवशरीरेभ्यःdevaśarīrebhyaḥfrom the bodies of the godsn. abl. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); source of सम्भूता
  11. जगत्त्रयहितैषिणीjagattrayahitaiṣiṇīwho seeks the welfare of the three worldsf. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies देवी

Translation

Thus this has been told, O king: how she, the Devī, wishing the welfare of the three worlds, formerly arose from the bodies of the gods.

In readable EnglishSo, O king, I have told you how long ago the Goddess, who seeks the good of the three worlds, came forth from the bodies of the gods.

The impersonal passive ‘this has been told’ is rendered ‘I have told you’.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • इत्येतत्कथितं ityetatkathitaṃ → इति + एतत् + कथितम्

    vowel sandhi (yaṇ); consonant unchanged · इको यणचि (6.1.77); खरि च (8.4.55)

    i before e becomes y; the final t of एतत् stays unvoiced before k; the m of कथितम् becomes anusvāra before bh (मोऽनुस्वारः, 8.3.23).

  • देवशरीरेभ्यो जगत्त्रय devaśarīrebhyo jagattraya → देवशरीरेभ्यः + जगत्त्रय…

    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)

    -aḥ before the voiced j becomes -o. देवी and देवशरीरेभ्यः are printed together, but they are two words: देवी needs no sandhi before d.

  • हितैषिणी hitaiṣiṇī → हित + एषिणी

    vowel sandhi inside a compound (vṛddhi) · वृद्धिरेचि (6.1.88)

    a + e → ai.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • भूप bhūpa

    upapada tatpuruṣa

    Members
    भू + प
    Vigraha
    भुवं पाति इति bhuvaṃ pāti iti
    Literally
    “protector of the earth”
    In the verse
    king

  • देवशरीरेभ्यः devaśarīrebhyaḥ

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa

    Members
    देव + शरीर
    Vigraha
    देवानां शरीराणि, तेभ्यः devānāṃ śarīrāṇi, tebhyaḥ
    Literally
    “from the bodies of the gods”
    In the verse
    from the splendour that came out of the gods’ bodies (2.9–13)

  • जगत्त्रयहितैषिणी jagattrayahitaiṣiṇī

    upapada tatpuruṣa (with the affix ṇini), containing ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣas

    Members
    जगत्त्रय + हित + एषिणी
    Vigraha
    जगतां त्रयं जगत्त्रयम्; जगत्त्रयस्य हितम् जगत्त्रयहितम्; जगत्त्रयहितम् एषितुं शीलम् अस्याः सा jagatāṃ trayaṃ jagattrayam; jagattrayasya hitam jagattrayahitam; jagattrayahitam eṣituṃ śīlam asyāḥ sā
    Literally
    “she whose nature is to seek the good of the triad of worlds”
    In the verse
    who wishes well to all three worlds
Morphology & anvaya

  • कथितम् kathitam

    Passive participle of the curādi root √कथ्: the stem’s -ay- is dropped and iṭ inserted, कथित. With इति एतत् it makes a closing formula: ‘thus this has been told’.

  • सम्भूता sambhūtā

    Past participle of an intransitive verb, so active in meaning: ‘(she) arose’, not ‘was produced’.

  • जगत्त्रयहितैषिणी jagattrayahitaiṣiṇī

    The last member -एषिन् is √इष् ‘to seek, wish’ + ṇini (a habitual agent: ‘one who regularly seeks’), feminine in -ī: हितैषिणी, ‘well-wisher’.

Anvaya · prose order

(हे) भूप, जगत्त्रयहितैषिणी सा देवी पुरा देवशरीरेभ्यः यथा सम्भूता, (तत्) एतत् इति कथितम्।

(he) bhūpa, jagattrayahitaiṣiṇī sā devī purā devaśarīrebhyaḥ yathā sambhūtā, (tat) etat iti kathitam |

A main clause (इति एतत् कथितम्) followed by an indirect question of manner (यथा … सम्भूता) that says what ‘this’ was.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

Medhas sums up the Madhyama Carita (chapters 2–4) by its first event: the Goddess arose from the gathered splendour of the gods’ bodies (2.9–13) to destroy Mahiṣa. The epithet जगत्त्रयहितैषिणी names her purpose.

Traditional interpretation

In the first carita the Goddess appears from Viṣṇu’s body as Yoganidrā; in the second, from the bodies of all the gods; in the third (announced in the next verse), from Gaurī’s body. The tradition reads these three emergences as one Goddess taking form in different ways.

Verse 4.41

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

पुनश्च गौरीदेहात्सा समुद्भूता यथाभवत्।वधाय दुष्टदैत्यानां तथा शुम्भनिशुम्भयोः॥⁠४१⁠॥

punaśca gaurīdehātsā samudbhūtā yathābhavat |vadhāya duṣṭadaityānāṃ tathā śumbhaniśumbhayoḥ ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

पुनः · च · गौरीदेहात् · सा · समुद्भूता · यथा · अभवत्वधाय · दुष्टदैत्यानाम् · तथा · शुम्भनिशुम्भयोः

punaḥ · ca · gaurīdehāt · sā · samudbhūtā · yathā · abhavatvadhāya · duṣṭadaityānām · tathā · śumbhaniśumbhayoḥ

Word by word

  1. पुनःpunaḥagainindeclinable (adverb)
  2. चcaandindeclinable (conjunction)
  3. गौरीदेहात्gaurīdehātfrom the body of Gaurī (Pārvatī)m. abl. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); source of समुद्भूता
  4. साsāshepronoun तद्, f. nom. sg.; subject
  5. समुद्भूताsamudbhūtāhaving arisenpast participle (kta) of सम् + उत् + √भू (bhvādi, 1P), active in sense, f. nom. sg.
  6. यथाyathāhowindeclinable (relative adverb of manner)
  7. अभवत्abhavatcame to be√भू (bhvādi, 1P), laṅ (imperfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  8. वधायvadhāyafor the slayingm. dat. sg. of वध; dative of purpose
  9. दुष्टदैत्यानाम्duṣṭadaityānāmof the wicked Daityasm. gen. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); depends on वधाय
  10. तथाtathāand likewiseindeclinable (connective adverb)
  11. शुम्भनिशुम्भयोःśumbhaniśumbhayoḥof Śumbha and Niśumbham. gen. du.; compound (see Samāsa); depends on वधाय

Translation

And again how she, having arisen from Gaurī’s body, came to be, for the slaying of the wicked Daityas and likewise of Śumbha and Niśumbha,

In readable Englishand how once more she arose, this time from the body of Gaurī, to slay the wicked Daityas, and Śumbha and Niśumbha with them,

‘This time’ makes explicit the contrast with 4.40 that पुनः implies; the sentence continues in 4.42.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • पुनश्च punaśca → पुनः + च

    visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34), स्तोः श्चुना श्चुः (8.4.40)

    Visarga before c becomes s, and the s becomes ś next to c.

  • गौरीदेहात्सा gaurīdehātsā → गौरीदेहात् + सा

    no change · खरि च (8.4.55)

    Final t stays unvoiced before the unvoiced s; the words are only written together.

  • यथाभवत् yathābhavat → यथा + अभवत्

    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    ā + a → ā.

  • दुष्टदैत्यानां तथा duṣṭadaityānāṃ tathā → दुष्टदैत्यानाम् + तथा

    anusvāra sandhi · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)

    Final m before a consonant becomes anusvāra.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • गौरीदेहात् gaurīdehāt

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa

    Members
    गौरी + देह
    Vigraha
    गौर्याः देहः, तस्मात् gauryāḥ dehaḥ, tasmāt
    Literally
    “from the body of Gaurī”
    In the verse
    from Pārvatī’s body, as told in 5.85–87

  • दुष्टदैत्यानाम् duṣṭadaityānām

    karmadhāraya

    Members
    दुष्ट + दैत्य
    Vigraha
    दुष्टाः च ते दैत्याः च, तेषाम् duṣṭāḥ ca te daityāḥ ca, teṣām
    Literally
    “of the wicked Daityas”
    In the verse
    the demon host of the third carita

  • शुम्भनिशुम्भयोः śumbhaniśumbhayoḥ

    itaretara dvandva

    Members
    शुम्भ + निशुम्भ
    Vigraha
    शुम्भः च निशुम्भः च, तयोः śumbhaḥ ca niśumbhaḥ ca, tayoḥ
    Literally
    “of Śumbha and Niśumbha”
    In the verse
    the two asura brothers whose defeat is the subject of chapters 5–10
Morphology & anvaya

  • अभवत् abhavat

    Imperfect of √भू with augment a- and guṇa (भव-): ‘came to be’. With the participle समुद्भूता: ‘how, having arisen, she came to be’.

  • शुम्भनिशुम्भयोः śumbhaniśumbhayoḥ

    A dvandva of two names takes the dual: genitive dual in -योः.

  • समुद्भूता samudbhūtā

    Two prefixes, सम् + उत्, on √भू: ‘to arise fully, spring up’; the past participle of this intransitive verb is active in sense.

Anvaya · prose order

पुनः च सा गौरीदेहात् समुद्भूता दुष्टदैत्यानां तथा शुम्भनिशुम्भयोः वधाय यथा अभवत्, (4.42 …)

punaḥ ca sā gaurīdehāt samudbhūtā duṣṭadaityānāṃ tathā śumbhaniśumbhayoḥ vadhāya yathā abhavat, (4.42 …)

A second indirect question of manner (यथा … अभवत्), continued in 4.42 and taken up there by तत्.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

Medhas announces the third story, the Uttama Carita (chapters 5–13): the Goddess will arise again, from the body of Gaurī, to destroy Śumbha, Niśumbha and their Daityas. In chapter 5 Ambikā comes forth from Pārvatī’s bodily sheath and is called Kauśikī (5.85–87).

Traditional interpretation

The liturgical tradition of the Gita Press pāṭha assigns the Uttama Carita to Mahāsarasvatī, as it assigns the first to Mahākālī and the second to Mahālakṣmī.

Verse 4.42

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

रक्षणाय च लोकानां देवानामुपकारिणी।तच्छृणुष्व मयाऽऽख्यातं यथावत्कथयामि ते॥⁠४२⁠॥ह्रीं ॐ

rakṣaṇāya ca lokānāṃ devānāmupakāriṇī |tacchṛṇuṣva mayā''khyātaṃ yathāvatkathayāmi te ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

रक्षणाय · च · लोकानाम् · देवानाम् · उपकारिणीतत् · शृणुष्व · मया · आख्यातम् · यथावत् · कथयामि · ते

rakṣaṇāya · ca · lokānām · devānām · upakāriṇītat · śṛṇuṣva · mayā · ākhyātam · yathāvat · kathayāmi · te

Word by word

  1. रक्षणायrakṣaṇāyafor the protectionn. dat. sg. of रक्षण (√रक्ष् + lyuṭ); dative of purpose, parallel to वधाय in 4.41
  2. चcaandindeclinable (conjunction)
  3. लोकानाम्lokānāmof the worldsm. gen. pl. of लोक; depends on रक्षणाय
  4. देवानाम्devānāmof the godsm. gen. pl. of देव; depends on उपकारिणी
  5. उपकारिणीupakāriṇīhelper, benefactressf. nom. sg. of उपकारिन् (उप + √कृ + ṇini, feminine in -ī); qualifies सा (4.41)
  6. तत्tatthat (story)pronoun तद्, n. acc. sg.; object of शृणुष्व; takes up the यथा clauses of 4.41–42
  7. शृणुष्वśṛṇuṣvahear√श्रु (svādi, 5P ‘to hear’), loṭ (imperative), madhyama-puruṣa ekavacana, ātmanepada ending — an epic (ārṣa) form for classical शृणु
  8. मयाmayāby mefirst-person pronoun अस्मद्, instr. sg.; agent of आख्यातम्
  9. आख्यातम्ākhyātamtold, narratedpast passive participle (kta) of आ + √ख्या (adādi, 2P ‘to tell’), n. acc. sg.; qualifies तत्
  10. यथावत्yathāvatexactly, as it happenedindeclinable (adverb: यथा + the suffix -vat)
  11. कथयामिkathayāmiI (shall) tell√कथ् (curādi, 10 ‘to tell’), laṭ (present), uttama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada; present for the immediate future
  12. तेteto yousecond-person pronoun युष्मद्, enclitic dat. sg.

Translation

and for the protection of the worlds, the helper of the gods — hear that, told by me; I tell it to you as it was.

In readable Englishand to protect the worlds, ever the helper of the gods. Listen as I tell it; I shall tell it to you just as it happened.

‘Ever’ renders the habitual sense of the ṇini affix in उपकारिणी; the future ‘shall tell’ renders the present कथयामि of imminent action.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • लोकानां देवानामुपकारिणी lokānāṃ devānāmupakāriṇī → लोकानाम् + देवानाम् + उपकारिणी

    anusvāra sandhi; m before a vowel · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)

    Final m before the consonant d becomes anusvāra (लोकानां); before the vowel u it stays m and is written joined (देवानामुपकारिणी).

  • तच्छृणुष्व tacchṛṇuṣva → तत् + शृणुष्व

    consonant sandhi · स्तोः श्चुना श्चुः (8.4.40), शश्छोऽटि (8.4.63)

    Final t becomes the palatal c next to the palatal ś, and ś after a stop becomes ch: tac + chṛṇuṣva.

  • मयाऽऽख्यातं mayā''khyātaṃ → मया + आख्यातम्

    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    ā + ā → ā; the Gita Press marks the merged long ā with a double avagraha (ऽऽ), as in तथाऽऽत्मनः (4.39).

  • यथावत्कथयामि yathāvatkathayāmi → यथावत् + कथयामि

    no change · खरि च (8.4.55)

    Final t stays unvoiced before the unvoiced k; the words are only written together.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

No compound in this verse needs analysis.

Morphology & anvaya

  • शृणुष्व śṛṇuṣva

    Imperative of √श्रु (stem शृणु-, with the class-5 nu) with the ātmanepada ending -sva (ṣ after u): शृणुष्व. Classical Sanskrit has only the parasmaipada शृणु; the ātmanepada form is common in epic and Purāṇic verse.

  • कथयामि kathayāmi

    Present of the curādi stem कथय-. A present used of what the speaker is about to do stands for the near future: ‘I am going to tell’.

  • उपकारिणी upakāriṇī

    उप + √कृ + ṇini (habitual agent), feminine उपकारिणी: ‘one who habitually helps’.

  • आख्यातम् ākhyātam

    आ + √ख्या + kta: आख्यात, ‘told’. With मया: ‘(that) told by me’, i.e. as I tell it.

Anvaya · prose order

(सा) च लोकानां रक्षणाय देवानाम् उपकारिणी (यथा अभवत्), तत् मया आख्यातं शृणुष्व; (अहं) ते यथावत् कथयामि।

(sā) ca lokānāṃ rakṣaṇāya devānām upakāriṇī (yathā abhavat), tat mayā ākhyātaṃ śṛṇuṣva; (ahaṃ) te yathāvat kathayāmi |

The first line ends the indirect question begun in 4.41 (‘how she came to be … for the protection of the worlds, the helper of the gods’). तत् takes up the whole of it as the object of शृणुष्व; a short main clause follows (यथावत् कथयामि ते).

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The last mantra of chapter 4 and of the Madhyama Carita. Medhas finishes announcing the third story — the Goddess’s rising from Gaurī for the slaying of Śumbha and Niśumbha and for the protection of the worlds — and promises to tell it exactly. Chapter 5 begins it.

Traditional interpretation

In the Gita Press pāṭha the chapter closes with the recitation bījas ह्रीं ॐ, printed after the verse. They belong to the pāṭha, not to the Purāṇa, and are not part of the verse’s words.

❁

इति श्रीमार्कण्डेयपुराणे सावर्णिके मन्वन्तरे देवीमाहात्म्ये शक्रादिस्तुतिर्नाम चतुर्थोऽध्यायः॥

iti śrīmārkaṇḍeyapurāṇe sāvarṇike manvantare devīmāhātmye śakrādistutirnāma caturtho'dhyāyaḥ ||

Reflecting on the chapter

With Mahiṣa dead, the gods do what they could not do in battle: they praise. Their hymn moves from what she is — beyond the triad’s telling, Prakṛti, Svāhā and Svadhā, liberating knowledge, Veda and livelihood, Medhā, Durgā, Śrī and Gaurī — to what she did, and finds in the killing a mercy that sent even her enemies to heaven. They ask her protection on every side, worship her, and, offered a boon, ask that she come whenever remembered and bless whoever recites these hymns. ‘So be it,’ says Bhadrakālī, and vanishes. Medhas closes the middle story and announces the last: her rising from Gaurī to slay Śumbha and Niśumbha (4.41–42).

Verses for study and memorisation

  • 4.3The gods bow to Ambikā, whose form is the gathered powers of all the gods but who pervades the world by her own power.
  • 4.5She is Śrī in the homes of the virtuous and Alakṣmī in those of the wicked, understanding, faith and modesty.
  • 4.7Cause of the worlds, untouched by the faults of the guṇas, the whole world a portion of her: the primal, unmanifest Prakṛti.
  • 4.11Medhā, Durgā the boat across the ocean of existence, Śrī in Viṣṇu’s heart, Gaurī with Śiva — ‘you alone’.
  • 4.17Remembered, Durgā removes the fear of every creature and gives the untroubled a good mind: who but she is so tender to all?
  • 4.21Her nature is to end the ways of the wicked; in slaying them she has shown compassion even to her enemies.
  • 4.36The boon: remembered again and again, may she destroy our calamities — and the promise for whoever praises her with these hymns, completed in 4.37.
Vocabulary, constructions and insights

Principal vocabulary

  • स्तवःstavaḥa hymn of praise (स्तवैरेभिः, ‘with these hymns’, 4.36)
  • शक्तिःśaktiḥpower; the Goddess’s own power (आत्मशक्तिः) and the gods’ powers gathered in her form (4.3)
  • प्रकृतिःprakṛtiḥprimal nature, the unmanifest source of the world (4.7)
  • गुणःguṇaḥone of the three strands of Prakṛti (त्रिगुणा, 4.7)
  • स्वाहाsvāhāthe call with which offerings go to the gods (4.8)
  • स्वधाsvadhāthe call with which offerings go to the ancestors (4.8)
  • विद्याvidyāknowledge, here the knowledge that is the cause of liberation (4.9)
  • मेधाmedhāintelligence that grasps the śāstras; the Goddess named as it (4.11)
  • दुर्गाdurgā‘hard to reach’: the Goddess as the boat across the hard-to-cross ocean (4.11, 4.17)
  • अलक्ष्मीःalakṣmīḥmisfortune, the opposite of Śrī (4.5)
  • दयाdayācompassion (shown even to enemies, 4.21)
  • प्रसादःprasādaḥgrace, favour (भवतीप्रसादात्, 4.16; प्रसादसुमुखी, 4.30)
  • सौम्यsaumyagentle, moon-like; set against घोर, terrible (4.26)
  • वरःvaraḥa boon (4.35)

Important grammatical constructions

  • The relative–correlative hymn: a या / यस्याः / यया clause taken up by तां त्वाम्, सा or ताम् (4.3–4.5), and यानि … तैः (4.26–4.27).
  • Second-person passives with the Goddess as subject and the agent in the instrumental (हरिहरादिभिः … न ज्ञायसे, 4.7; जनैः उच्चार्यसे, 4.8; मुनिभिः अभ्यस्यसे, 4.9).
  • The impersonal passive: प्रहृतं … महिषासुरेण (4.12), कैर्जीव्यते (4.13), विज्ञातम् (4.14).
  • Imperatives of prayer (प्रसीद, परिपालय, पाहि, रक्ष) and of deliberation (किं वर्णयाम, 4.6; केनोपमा भवतु, 4.22); the passive imperative of courtesy (व्रियताम्, 4.32).
  • The concessive imperative with नाम, and a thought closed by इति with मत्वा (कुर्वन्तु नाम … मत्वेति, 4.18).
  • The participle as a condition (स्मृता, 4.17) and its repetition for ‘every time’ (संस्मृता संस्मृता, 4.36).
  • Epic ātmanepada forms where classical prose has parasmaipada: हिंसेथाः, भवेथाः (4.36–37), शृणुष्व (4.42).
  • Agent nouns in -in and -tṛ, the latter with a genitive object (असुरक्षयकारि, 4.6; परमार्तिहन्त्री, 4.10; हन्तृ हृतदेवपराक्रमाणाम्, 4.21; धात्री, 4.29).

Śākta insights

  • Her form was made of the gods’ gathered powers, yet she pervades the world by her own (आत्मशक्त्या, 4.3), and even Viṣṇu, Brahmā and Śiva cannot tell her majesty (4.4, 4.7).
  • One Goddess is fortune and misfortune, understanding, faith and modesty (4.5), and Medhā, Durgā, Śrī and Gaurī are she alone (4.11).
  • The ritual word, liberating knowledge and ordinary livelihood are all her forms: Svāhā and Svadhā (4.8), the supreme vidyā (4.9), the Veda and Vārttā (4.10).
  • Her severity is her compassion: she slays with weapons so that the slain are purified and reach heaven (4.18–4.19, 4.23), and mercy and relentlessness are seen together in her alone (4.21–4.22).
  • The gentle and the terrible are one face: the moon-like face Mahiṣa struck and the angry one he survived (4.12–4.13); both kinds of form are asked to protect (4.26).
  • Remembrance suffices: remembered, she removes the fear of every creature (4.17), and the boon makes remembrance and recitation the way to her help (4.36–4.37).

Śrīvidyā connections

The hymn shares many names with the Lalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa): Ambikā (4.3; name 295, अम्बिका), Svāhā and Svadhā side by side (4.8; names 535, स्वाहा, and 536, स्वधा), Medhā, Durgā and Gaurī (4.11; names 538, मेधा, 190, दुर्गा, and 635, गौरी), Lajjā (4.5; name 740, लज्जा), the Prakṛti of the three guṇas (4.7; names 397, मूलप्रकृतिः, and 984, त्रिगुणा), Trayī (4.10; name 872, त्रयी), Caṇḍikā (4.4, 4.25; name 755, चण्डिका) and Maheśvarī (4.35; name 750, महेश्वरी). The Lalitā Sahasranāma’s अव्याजकरुणामूर्तिः (name 992) is close in idea to the compassion of 4.21. These are a shared Śākta vocabulary, not evidence that one text depends on the other. The Śrī of 4.11 is Viṣṇu’s consort, not the Śrī of Śrīvidyā, and no specifically Śrīvidyā doctrine — mantra, yantra or the worship of Lalitā — is stated in this chapter.

Chapter
4 — The Praise by Indra and the Gods (शक्रादिस्तुतिः)

Śrīdurgāsaptaśatī, Gita Press, Gorakhpur (Hindi translation by Paṇḍit Rāmanārāyaṇadatta Śāstrī “Rāma”)

Verse entries
42 entries for 42 mantras — uvāca 5, half-ślokas 2, ślokas 35; the counts equal the chapter's colophon
Analysed
42 of 42 entries
Numbering
Gita Press counts every uvāca line and half-śloka as a mantra; the Mārkaṇḍeya Purāṇa (GRETIL, adhyāya 84) numbers by full ślokas and does not number uvāca lines, so its numbers differ.
Corrections to the digital text
None
Variant readings (kept as printed)
4.3: निश्शेष / निःशेष (orthographic)
Uncertain readings or interpretations
4.3, 4.6, 4.7, 4.37
Sandhi patterns
  • Visarga sandhi throughout: -aḥ → -o before voiced sounds (सद्यो विनाशयसि, 4.14; पाहि नो देवि, 4.24), -ḥ → r after other vowels (दोषैर्न, 4.7; नौरसङ्गा, 4.11; कैर्जीव्यते, 4.13), -ḥ → s/ś before t/c (प्रकृतिस्त्वम्, 4.7; हरश्च, 4.4), and -āḥ losing its visarga before voiced sounds and vowels (सुरगणा निहते, 4.2; देवा ऊचुः, 4.33).
  • Visarga kept before a sibilant where assimilation is optional (नताः स्म, 4.3; श्रीः स्वयम्, 4.5; स्वस्थैः स्मृता, 4.17; निहतः शत्रुः, 4.35), and निश्शेष with the assimilated sibilant (4.3, the variant reads निःशेष).
  • Pūrvarūpa with avagraha (निहतेऽतिवीर्ये, 4.2; रिपवोऽपि, 4.19; तेऽम्बिके, 4.27), and the double avagraha for ā + ā (सदाऽऽर्द्रचित्ता, 4.17; तथाऽऽत्मनः, 4.39; मयाऽऽख्यातम्, 4.42).
  • Junctions across the printed line-end of the vasantatilakā stanzas (दोषै- / र्न, 4.7; तृप्तिहेतु- / रुच्चार्यसे, 4.8; एत- / न्नीतम्, 4.14; कर्मा- / ण्यत्यादृतः and भवतीप्रसादा- / ल्लोकत्रये, 4.16).
  • Consonant sandhi: t + ś → cch and ch doubled (उद्यच्छशाङ्कसदृशच्छवि, 4.13; तच्छृणुष्व, 4.42), t → n before n (यन्न, 4.13; यन्नागता, 4.20), t + l → ll (4.16), n + t → ṃst (अस्मांस्तथा, 4.26), and r before r dropped (तै रक्ष, 4.26).
  • One declared hiatus: आत्मशूलस्य | उत्तरस्याम् at the pāda break of 4.25, a metrical licence.
Samāsa constructions
  • Long bahuvrīhis describing the gods and the Goddess (प्रणतिनम्रशिरोधरांसाः, प्रहर्षपुलकोद्गमचारुदेहाः, 4.2; निश्शेषदेवगणशक्तिसमूहमूर्त्या, 4.3; कैटभारिहृदयैककृताधिवासा, शशिमौलिकृतप्रतिष्ठा, 4.11).
  • Nañ compounds of the ineffable (अतुलम्, 4.4; अचिन्त्यम्, 4.6; अपारा, अव्याकृता, 4.7; अविचिन्त्यम्, अतुल्यम्, 4.21).
  • Upapada tatpuruṣas with agent nouns in -in (असुरक्षयकारि, 4.6; दारिद्र्यदुःखभयहारिणि, 4.17; शत्रुभयकारि, 4.22; जगत्त्रयहितैषिणी, 4.40).
  • Nested ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣas of light (खड्गप्रभानिकरविस्फुरणैः, शूलाग्रकान्तिनिवहेन, 4.20) and of distress (परमार्तिहन्त्री, 4.10).
  • Bahuvrīhis in आदि (शक्रादयः, 4.2; हरिहरादिभिः, 4.7; खड्गशूलगदादीनि, 4.27; धनदारादि-, 4.37) and itaretara dvandvas (ऋग्यजुस्, 4.10; आत्मजभृत्यदाराः, 4.15; शुम्भनिशुम्भयोः, 4.41).
  • One avyayībhāva (प्रतिदिनम्, 4.16) and karmadhārayas of title (महेश्वरि, 4.35; भद्रकाली, 4.39).
Grammatical observations
  • नताः स्म (4.3, 4.5) is read as ‘we are bowed’, स्म for स्मः without its visarga, as the metre requires a light syllable.
  • Epic ātmanepada forms are kept as ārṣa: the optatives हिंसेथाः and भवेथाः (4.36–37) and the imperative शृणुष्व (4.42).
  • Sentences run across verses: 4.29–4.30 (one subject, verb प्राह), 4.35–4.37 (condition, boon and the reciter’s blessing), 4.41–4.42 (the announcement of the third story).
  • In 4.25 the south is named with the masculine/neuter locative दक्षिणे beside the feminine locatives of the other quarters, and उत्तर is declined like a pronoun (उत्तरस्याम्).
  • विलोकयताम् (4.20) is a present participle in the genitive plural agreeing with असुराणाम्, not an imperative; ते in 4.23 is two words, ‘they’ and ‘to you’.
  • दृष्टा (4.22) agrees with the nearer of two subjects; अस्मत्तः (4.32) is the Goddess’s plural of herself.
Philosophical themes
  • The Goddess beyond the gods’ powers
  • Her forms in fortune, mind and Veda
  • Gentle and terrible in one face
  • Compassion through severity
  • Remembrance and the fruit of recitation
Open questions
  • 4.3: निश्शेष (Gita Press, kept) / निःशेष (GRETIL, Freund) — orthographic; the same word.
  • Interpretive doubts, none about the reading, are flagged on 4.3 (नताः स्म as ‘we are bowed’ or the particle स्म), 4.6 (the locative सर्वेषु … असुरदेवगणादिकेषु), 4.7 (दोषैः as ‘by faults’ or as agent) and 4.37 (the construction of वित्तर्द्धिविभवैः).
